# Square backlog — picked by the daily code job (see GROW.md)

> Owner direction (2026-09-24): no town levels or XP. The town must become physically larger and denser over time. Existing place/building items below (builders, market alley, clinic, diner, housing, bridge/dock/transit, etc.) are now a priority lane: if four feature runs pass without visible footprint growth, pick the first feasible one before another micro-interaction. All new game work must remain usable on phone landscape. See `TOWN-ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## Empty pockets — the thirtieth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-17; six wishes in two days on one subject — coins in the hand when the pocket is empty: pocket linings turned out at a stall, a coin tin shaken at the ear on the last coin, a sweep for a single coin from the shopkeeper, a copper fished from the fountain rim, a coin under a drain grate prodded with a twig, a bench-counter's pile peered at; merged on run 170 and the six wish lines deleted — and five more on 2026-10-17 written into the owner "Money is real" section (an empty-pocket pat and a recount beside the counter, a fiddle case for a copper, a sweeper's token tapped for its ring, coins tapped down one by one with a pause over the last penny, a pinky in the phone booth's coin return) folded in on run 171 as slices 5–7 and two clauses, the five wish lines deleted; `game/`, under "Money is real" — the saved wallet, `town_growth.can_pay`, `prices.json`, the wage path of run 113 and the mind. Build order, one slice per run; the first slice waits for the first run outside an owner-priority section, as the twenty-ninth's does)
- [ ] (empty pockets 1 — economy + motions; the refusal made visible) When `can_pay` refuses at any counter (bakery, café, general store, stall), the buyer turns out their pocket linings — a new `linings` pose (both hands dive to the hips over 0.25 s, the hands flip out and hang, a shrug, a 0.4 s hold, the hands drop — never a still frame), scuffs the paving once with a foot and says a line (`mind.line("broke")`, a new `minds.json` `default` category: "Change. It was here."); the player gets the same pose and a toast; a resident whose last coin goes on a purchase lifts the empty tin to its ear and shakes it once (`tin` pose — 0.2 s up, one shake, a 0.3 s hold, down) with a payday line (`mind.line("payday")`); the plain purchase and the plain refusal are unchanged (100%); probe: the pose on refusal, the tin on the last coin, the two lines, the player half.
- [ ] (empty pockets 2 — economy + jobs; an earning path at the counter itself, the rule that every gate has one nearby) The counter broom: a broom leans by every staffed counter; C with an empty wallet (or a resident whose `can_pay` just refused — a `mind.score` term on the "broke" moment, not a fixed roll) takes it and sweeps the floor in front of the counter for 5 s (the sweepers' existing `sweep`, same timing) and the shopkeeper slides one coin across the wood (`prices.json` `sweep_coin` 1, once per counter per town-day, through the wage path so the ledger sees it) — the sweeper picks it up and taps it twice on the counter to test its ring before pocketing (a `ring` beat inside the existing `stoop`: 0.2 s up, two taps at 4 Hz, the head tilted to listen, the pocket) and the player does the same with a toast ("Sounds right."); litter tokens within 1.5 m vanish; probe: the coin once a day, the refusal of a second sweep, a resident doing it unprompted, the litter.
- [ ] (empty pockets 3 — interactions + places; coins on the ground) The fountain rim and the drain grate: once a town-day a copper lies on the square fountain's rim (a `coin` prop the fox's scavenger rules already know) — C within 1 m fishes it out (`dip` pose: one knee down, the hand into the water 0.3 s, a 0.4 s wipe on the trouser leg, the pocket) with a flat line about someone else's wish (`mind.line("wish")`); a street drain grate (a new `PropKind` on the main road's kerb, outside the z −0.1..4.1 lane band) holds a coin nobody can get — C crouches and prods at it with a twig (`prod` pose: a crouch, the arm down between the bars, three jabs at 2 Hz, up empty); the phone booth's coin return is the third spot: a passing resident (a `mind.score` term on an empty wallet) hooks a finger in the chute (`hook` beat: 0.3 s reach, a 0.4 s feel, a head-shake, on) and once a town-week there is a copper in it for whoever checks first, the player too; and the mind says a line (`mind.line("drain")`); residents do both on their rounds (a `mind.score` term for a low wallet); probe: the daily coin, the dip, the prod that never pays, the lines, the grate outside the lanes.
- [ ] (empty pockets 4 — relationships + motions; counting in public) The bench count: a seated resident with three or more coins may count them in its palm (`count` pose — the coins slid one by one by the thumb at 1 Hz, the head bowed, 4 s), and a neighbour on the same bench leans sideways to look (`crane` pose: the trunk tilts 0.3 rad toward the counter over 0.5 s, the head turning, a 1 s hold, back) — the counter snaps its hand shut (the pose's early exit, 0.15 s) and says a dry line about prying (`mind.line("pry")`); `mind.befriend()` is not called — this is the one shared moment that cools a tie by a notch; the player on a bench can count too and gets craned at; probe: the count, the crane, the snap, the tie.
- [ ] (empty pockets 5 — economy + motions; the refusal continued) The recount: after slice 1's `linings`, the refused buyer steps 0.6 m aside from the counter (so the next customer is served — the counter's queue of run 72 moves up), bows its head and recounts its loose change in its palm (slice 4's `count` pose, 3 s) with a flat shortfall label over the head ("2 short") instead of a bubble, then either walks off to the nearest earning path (the counter broom, the odd-jobs board, a wage spot — a `mind.score` term, never a fixed roll) or, if the recount finds enough (a wallet within 1 coin of the price), steps back and pays; the player gets the same label and the step-aside is theirs to make; probe: the step aside, the label's number, the queue moving, the pay-after-recount.
- [ ] (empty pockets 6 — economy + jobs; a second street earning path beside the busker's hat) The fiddle case: an open fiddle case prop (velvet lining, two coins already in it) at the square's edge, outside the road band; an idle resident who owns a fiddle (a new wearable-in-hand from the general store, `prices.json` `fiddle`; one resident starts with one) stands behind it and plays a short phrase — a new `fiddle` pose (the case under the chin, the bow arm sawing at 2 Hz with the wrist leading, the fingering hand's elbow lifted, a sway at 0.5 Hz, 8 s, a bow-flourish out — never a still frame; the phrase is four notes from the chime pack's `_tone_wav`), passing walkers pause 2 s within 3 m (`mind.score` on `social`, the busker's crowd rule) and one in three tosses a copper into the velvet (the `toss` of the pebble games, the coin's arc into the case); the player with a fiddle plays too (C at the case) and keeps what lands; the case is emptied into the player's or owner's wallet at dusk; probe: the pose timing, the pause, the toss rate, the dusk count.
- [ ] (empty pockets 7 — economy + motions; paying by hand) Coins on the wood: at any counter a buyer who pays with coins (not a card — there are none) taps them down one by one with the index finger (a new `tally` pose: the hand from the pocket to the counter, one tap per coin at 2 Hz with a small head-nod each, the other hand flat on the wood), and when the last coin is also the wallet's last the hand hovers 2 s over it before sliding it across (the hesitation is `mind` — a `thrifty` trait hovers 3 s, a `generous` one 1 s); the shopkeeper waits with `wait`; the player pays the same way when the wallet holds at most the price + 1 and sees the hover; probe: the tap count equals the price, the hover by trait, the plain pay above price + 1 untouched (100%).

## Small things between elders and children — the twenty-ninth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-16, written into the owner's "Money is real" section by mistake and merged on run 168; three wishes in one day on one subject — **a seated elder and a passing child exchange a small thing at a public spot**: a pocket treasure shown for a nod, a crust taken from a bag and scattered for the ducks, a humbug taken from an open tin with cupped hands. The "Elders teaching children" system above is the game the elder *runs*; this is the smaller thing that passes hand to hand without a game, and it reaches the economy (day-old bread, a tin from the general store) and the minds (what the elder remembers). The owner-priority Climb pack took run 168's implementation slot as in runs 143 and 146; build these in order on the first run outside an owner-priority section, one slice per run, each with the player half and the resident half.)
- [ ] (share 1) The pocket treasure: a child resident whose routine passes within 3 m of an elder seated on a public bench (the storysitter after "teach 4", else any roster elder of the "Elders and children" system) stops for 4 s with a `show` pose (both hands cupped forward at chest height and opened on a 0.4 s beat, a glance up, the hands closed again — never a still frame) holding its `treasure` (a pebble, a bottle cap or a feather — `props3d.gd` builder, one per child by uid, kept in the pocket like the coins of run 107), the elder answers with an `approve` pose (a slow nod with the head tilted, both hands resting on the stick, 1.2 s) and `mind.line("treasure")` (six deadpan approvals in `minds.json`'s `default`), the child skips on; the player with any held item and C within 1.5 m of a seated elder shows it the same way and hears the line with the item's name; a task "show an elder what you found"; `probe_share.gd`: the stop, the two poses, the line, the player's show, the skip.
- [ ] (share 2) Crusts for the ducks: the bakery's day-old shelf (one `crusts` paper bag at 10:00 from yesterday's unsold loaves of the run-72 loop, 1 coin, the baker's `mind.line("dayold")`) that a seated elder buys on the way to the pond bench; a child whose routine passes sits beside (the seat-taken rule of polish run 67), takes a piece with the hand-to-hand verb of "Sharing food" and a `scatter` pose (an underarm fling of crumbs on a 0.6 s beat, three throws, the arm straightening and the fingers opening — never a still frame), two of the pond's ducks (`quad3d`/bird rig, the `goose.gd` flock) walk over and `peck` for 6 s then drift back; the player with the bag and C at the pond margin scatters the same way (the bag empties in three, the empty bag is a bin item); a task "feed the ducks with an elder"; probe: the shelf's hour and price, the sit, the three throws, the two ducks, the empty bag.
- [ ] (share 3) The humbug tin: a `tin` item (a small lidded box, `props3d.gd` builder, `humbugs` count 8, 2 coins at the general store of run 114, refilled by the elder when empty) on the seated elder's lap; a child approaches with a `cup` pose (cupped hands held out at waist height, a shy half-step and a stop, 0.8 s), the elder's `offer` pose (the tin held out and tilted, the lid thumbed open on a 0.5 s beat, held, the lid thumbed shut), one humbug to the child's hand and the existing `eat` with a `suck` meta that keeps the cheek bulged 20 s, the elder's slow `approve`, the child's hurried `skip` away; the player with empty hands and C within 1.5 m gets one the same way; the elder's mind remembers who took three in one tower-day and says so with `mind.line("greedy")` and refuses a fourth (one refusal, never more — the plain outcome stays first); a task "take a humbug from an elder's tin"; probe: the count, the four poses, the refusal arithmetic, the refill.

## Elders teaching children — the twenty-eighth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-16; three wishes in one day on one subject — **a seated elder who passes a small game to a child at a public spot and keeps its time with the walking stick**: a cat's cradle on a park bench, hopscotch on a chalk grid with the stick tapping the count, paper boats at the pond margin nudged off the reeds with the stick's crook. Run 163 moved the first to the wishes section as a pair-in-waiting ("a second makes a pair; a third makes a system"); the two the town wrote into the owner's "Money is real" section the same day made the shape plain: the town wants *the elders of run 103's Story Sunroom out on the benches with the children*, not three props. Merged on run 164 in build order, one slice per item; the three wish lines deleted. Rules for every slice: the elder is a seated resident with the `elder` role of "Elders and children" (the storysitter first, any roster elder later), the child a `resident_kid.gd` kid; the pair forms through a `mind.score` term "teach" once per tower-day per pair and both say a `mind.line` from a new category; the player with C and empty hands takes the child's side; every slice ships both halves' poses with their own beats (never a still frame), a task, a probe; nothing costs, nothing gates, and the children's plain routine (follow, skip, nobody knocks them over) is untouched.)
- [ ] (teach 1) The cat's cradle on the park bench: a looped `string` item (a thin loop, `props3d.gd` builder, kept on the storysitter's belt socket) and a `cradle` pose pair — the seated elder's hands held apart with the loop between them, four figures on a 0.8 s beat (the fingers hooking the far string, a spread, a twist, a lift), the standing child's hands reaching in to take the figure on the fourth beat and the shared nod — started by the storysitter on a park bench when a child's routine passes within 3 m and the "teach" term wins; the player with C at the bench and empty hands takes the child's side; a task "learn the cat's cradle from an elder"; `probe_teach.gd`: the beat, the handover, the nod, the once-a-day. (Wish: "Elderly residents sitting on park benches can invite nearby child residents over to demonstrate a cat's-cradle string trick, holding a looped string prop between their hands until both finish with a shared nod.")
- [ ] (teach 2) Hopscotch and the stick's count: a permanent chalk grid on the square's paving (eight numbered squares, `props3d.gd` builder `chalk_grid(pos)`, white-ink quads, off the road band z −0.1..4.1), a `hop` pose for the child (one-foot hops on a 0.45 s beat through the single squares, a two-foot landing on the doubles, a turn at the top — the `skip` of run 102 is a walk, this is a sequence), the seated elder's `tap` pose (the walking stick lifted a hand's width and tapped on the paving at each landing with a nod) and the count as `mind.line("count")` — eight deadpan numbers in `minds.json`; a child who lands on a line (one hop in six, from the uid, never `randf`) sits out a turn on the bench; the player with C at the grid's foot hops with SPACE and the elder counts the same; a task "hop the grid while an elder counts"; probe: the grid, the eight taps, the miss, the player's count. (Wish: "Child residents can play hopscotch on a permanent chalk grid in the square by hopping through numbered squares in a sequence, while an elderly resident seated nearby gently taps their walking stick on each landing to cou")
- [ ] (teach 3) Paper boats at the pond margin: a `paper` folded into a `boat` item with the `fold` pose of "Elders and children" slice 3 (three folds, the item swaps), a `launch` pose for the child (a crouch at the margin, the boat set on the water with both hands, a two-finger push — never a still frame), the boat drifting on `Water3D` at 0.15 m/s with the ripple phase and snagging on a reed every 3 m (a `snag` flag, the boat turning in place), the seated elder's `crook` pose (the stick reversed, the crook reached out 1.2 m over the water, a draw back) that frees a snagged boat within reach, a boat past the far margin kept by the duck (the goose's take verb) and said over with `mind.line("boat")`; the player with C and a paper at the margin launches the same, and C with empty hands beside the seated elder borrows the stick for one crook; a task "launch a paper boat and keep it off the reeds"; probe: the drift, the snag, the crook, the duck. (Wish: "Child residents can launch a folded paper boat into the duck pond margin, while an elderly resident seated nearby uses the crook of their walking stick to nudge snagged boats back into open water.")
- [ ] (teach 4) The elders go out: at 10:00 and 15:00 the storysitter (and any roster elder later) walks from the Sunroom to the nearest free park bench with the stick — a `cane` walk pose (the stick's tip set a half-step ahead on each beat, the free arm still, the pace 0.8 × walk) — sits an hour and walks back; while an elder is seated the children's routines gain the "teach" term that draws the nearest two within 12 m, so slices 1–3 have their pairs without a scripted meeting; the player sitting on the same bench with C hears `mind.line("bench")`; a task "sit an hour with an elder on the park bench"; probe: the hours, the cane walk, the two children, the hour's end.

## Wayside chores — the twenty-seventh system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-15; four wishes in one day on one subject — **a niche beside an existing place where a resident on their way pauses for a short three-beat hand chore and walks on**: a watchman's brazier by the square archway (warm the hands, two stamps), a boot-scraper by the police station steps (two draws of each heel, two toe knocks), a drying yard behind the allotments (peg, pinch, unpeg) and a knife-grinder's alcove under the street overhang (treadle, thumb the burr, wipe the steel). Run 160 read the first three as three posts; the fourth, written into the owner's "Money is real" section the same day, made the shape plain: the town wants *wayside stops on routes*, not four props. Merged on run 161 in build order, one slice per item; the four wish lines deleted. Rules for every slice: the stop is a `niche` spot built in `props3d.gd` off the road band z −0.1..4.1 and registered in one place (`town_niches.gd`, a new layer between `places` and `boat` in the chain — update the chain comment), with a `pose` (its own family: windup, beats, recovery — never a still frame), a `beats` count and a `why` term; a resident whose route passes within 3 m stops when `mind.score` raises that term (cold at dusk, mud after the park, a wet-linen chore, a dull-blade chore — never a `randf()`), once per tower-day per niche per resident; the player does the same with C and empty hands, and every niche brings a task in `tasksFor`-style town tasks and one `mind.line` category; nothing solid in the walk line, nothing refuses, nothing costs. Builds after the owner-priority sections at the end of this file, like "Estranged pairs" and "Bells and clocks".)
- [ ] (wayside 1) The niche layer and the brazier: `town_niches.gd` (register `{id, pos, pose, beats, why, hours}`, `near(route) -> id`, `stop(res, id)` that plays the pose for its beats and resumes the route, the once-a-day memory on the mind) plus the first niche — the watchman's brazier beside the square archway: a coal brazier prop lit from dusk to dawn (`props3d.gd`, ember glow on an unshaded quad, no light node), a `warm` pose (both hands out palms down over the coals, the shoulders hunched, two foot stamps on a 0.5 s beat, a rub of the hands, never a still frame), the `why` term "cold" raised for the bellman of "The rounds" and any late-routine resident between dusk and dawn; the player with C; a task "warm your hands at the brazier before midnight"; probe `probe_niches`: the register, the stop, the once-a-day, the pose beats. (Wish: "Add a watchman's brazier niche beside the square archway where late-patrol residents pause to warm their hands over hot coals, stamping their feet twice before resuming their rounds.")
- [ ] (wayside 2) The boot-scraper by the police station steps: an iron bar set in a stone (`props3d.gd`), a `scrape` pose (one foot up on the bar, the heel drawn back along it twice, a swap, two toe knocks on the stone on a 0.4 s beat, never a still frame), the `why` term "mud" raised on a resident whose last spot was the park or the meadow and whose next is a doorstep — four draws instead of two after rain, and a wet footprint trail from the park ends at the scraper; the player with C; a task "scrape your boots before the station"; probe: the term, the rain length, the trail's end. (Wish: "Add a boot-scraping niche beside the police station steps where residents returning from the park pause to scrape each heel against an iron bar and knock their toes twice before walking onto the pavement.")
- [ ] (wayside 3) The drying yard behind the allotments: two line posts and a wicker hamper (`props3d.gd`), a `peg` pose (both arms up to the line, a pinch of the cloth between finger and thumb for 0.3 s, a sheet lifted from the hamper with a shake, never a still frame); the stitchhouse's mended things (run 82) hang here one town-hour before they go back, residents with the `wash` chore from `minds.json` peg and unpeg them (the `why` term "linen"), C at the hamper with empty hands pegs the next one, rain sends the nearest to unpeg everything with the existing `storm` glance first; a task "peg the laundry"; probe: the hour, the rain, the hamper count. (Wish: "Add a laundry drying yard behind the allotments where residents with wet linens pause at a line post to peg up a sheet, test its dryness with a pinch, and unpeg it into a wicker hamper.")
- [ ] (wayside 4) The knife-grinder's alcove under the street overhang: a treadle emery wheel in an alcove (`props3d.gd`, the wheel spins while treadled, sparks as unshaded quads), a `grind2` pose family named `hone` (the right foot treadling on a 0.7 s beat, the blade held to the stone with both hands, a thumb drawn across the edge for the burr, a wipe along an oily rag, never a still frame), the `why` term "blunt" raised on the bakery's and café's kitchen residents once a tower-day — they carry a visible knife there (a held item with the carry verb) and back; the player with C and a held knife; a task "have the bread knife honed"; probe: the carry there and back, the beats, the once-a-day. (Wish: "Add a knife-grinder's alcove under the street overhang where residents carry dull kitchen blades to treadle an emery wheel, test the burr with a thumb against the stone, and wipe the steel with an oily rag before returni")
- [ ] (wayside 5) The cobbler's alcove behind the bakery: an open alcove with a stool and a small iron last (`props3d.gd`, off the road band z −0.1..4.1), a seated cobbler resident with a door job there from morning to dusk, a `tack` pose (the boot held sole-up on the last with the left hand, three taps of a tack hammer on a 0.4 s beat, a turn of the boot, a wrap in brown paper with two folds, never a still frame — the cobbler's half) and the `why` term "scuffed" raised on a resident who has been knocked over three times since the last tower-day (`mind` already counts hits) — they walk up, hand the boots over with the existing `give`, stand in socks for the three taps, take the parcel back; the player with C and empty hands within 1.5 m hands over their own boots the same way and walks barefoot for the beats (a `bare` flag on the figure, no speed change); a task "get your boots tacked"; one `mind.line` category "cobbler"; probe: the term, the three beats, the parcel. (Wish, 2026-10-15: "Add an open cobbler's alcove behind the bakery where a seated cobbler resident accepts scuffed boots from passing walkers, taps them three times with a tack hammer on a small iron last, and hands them back in brown paper" — written into the owner "Money is real" section; moved here on run 163 as the system's fifth stop: it is the same shape, a niche beside a place with a three-beat hand chore.)

## Estranged pairs — the twenty-sixth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-14; three wishes in one day on one subject — **two paired residents who fall out because of the player and find their way back**: a bond that breaks for two tower-days when the player knocks them into each other so they cross the street and avert their eyes, a former partner who stops four paces back and turns their back at a stall until the other has gone, and a bench where two estranged residents seated at opposite ends sit a silent minute and nod to each other to end it. Merged on run 154 in build order, one slice per item; the three wish lines deleted (the first had been moved out of the owner's "Money is real" section on run 153, the other two were written there again today). Rules for every slice: the pair is `resident_pair.gd` of run 95 and the memory lives on both minds (`resident_mind.gd`, saved with user://minds.json, two tower-days); every state has its own pose, never a borrowed one; nothing refuses the player, nothing costs; a resident in `drive` or `chase` is never pulled into any of it. Builds after the owner-priority sections at the end of this file, like "Bells and clocks" and "Street trades, second wave".)
- [ ] (estranged 1) The knock: a pair the player knocks into each other (the existing hit push lands one body on the other within 0.5 s) gets `estranged_until` two tower-days on both minds; while it lasts `resident_pair` never pairs them, and when the other is within 6 m each crosses to the other lane (the existing lane change) with an `avert` pose — the head turned hard away, the near shoulder raised, the stride shortened for three steps, a glance back on the fourth, never a still frame — and `mind.line("avert")` once per meeting (add the category to `minds.json`'s `default`); `probe_pair` gains the estrangement and its end.
- [ ] (estranged 2) The stall standoff: an estranged resident arriving at a stall or counter where the former partner already waits stops four paces (2.4 m) back and turns their back (`sulk` pose — arms folded, weight on one hip, the head down and to the side, a foot tapping twice a second, a half-turn to check over the shoulder every few seconds, never a still frame) until the other has stepped away, then steps up to queue as usual; a player in the queue sees the gap and may step into it — the straightforward queue is untouched; probe: the four-pace stop, the wait, the resumption.
- [ ] (estranged 3) The bench nod: two estranged residents who end up seated at opposite ends of one bench (the existing seat routine; a `mind.score` term "same_bench" lets a sulking mind still pick a bench its partner sits on, with a low weight) sit a silent tower-minute — no `mind.line`, hands in laps — then turn and nod to each other (`nod` pose — a slow dip of the head with the shoulders following, a held beat, a lift, both bodies within 0.3 s of each other, never a still frame), `estranged_until` clears on both and `resident_pair` schedules their next walk as a pair within the tower-hour; a player who sits between them gets the nod over their head and a `mind.line("thaw")` from one; probe: the minute, the nod, the cleared memory and the scheduled walk.
- [ ] (estranged 4 — the fourth wish on the subject, written into the owner "Money is real" section on 2026-10-14 and moved here on run 155: "a three-day pair bond … lockstep on day one, a scowl and opposite kerbs on day two if nudged, opposite ends of the same bench on day three") The three-day arc: `resident_pair.gd` schedules a bond as a three-tower-day arc instead of one walk — day one the pair walks in lockstep (the existing paired walk with strides phase-locked, a `mind.befriend()` at the end), day two a nudge from the player (the hit push of slice 1, or simply a body walked between them for 2 s) estranges them for the rest of the day with the `avert` crossing of slice 1 and a `scowl` pose when they pass (brows down — the head tilted forward 0.15, the mouth line flattened on the face texture, the arms stiff at the sides, one slow exhale that drops the shoulders, never a still frame), day three they are steered to opposite ends of the same bench by the `same_bench` score term of slice 3 and the nod ends it; a pair never nudged on day two simply keeps walking; probe: the three days on the pair's clock, the lockstep, the scowl and the bench.
- [ ] (estranged 5 — the fifth wish on the subject, written into the owner "Money is real" section on 2026-10-14 and moved here on run 156: "three consecutive mornings walking side by side → a mutual bond flag; an interruption or shove makes them turn away in an argument pose and costs a full day of solo wandering before they pair again") The quarrel: a bonded pair (three mornings of lockstep — slice 4's arc, or three ordinary pair walks on consecutive tower-days — sets `bonded` on both minds) that the player interrupts (a shove on either, or the player standing in their lane for 2 s so they split round them) stops and faces off with an `argue` pose (both bodies squared up a pace apart, one arm chopping the air on a 0.6 s beat, the other hand on the hip, heads thrust forward, then both turn their backs with the `avert` of slice 1 — never a still frame), says one `mind.line("argue")` each (new category in `minds.json`'s `default`, dry, no joke), then each wanders alone for a full tower-day (`resident_pair` refuses them; the `same_bench` term of slice 3 still works) before the bond resumes; the player's and the residents' halves are the same: a player who shoves a bonded resident is remembered by both minds (reputation, not XP — the next pair walk crosses the street when the player is near). Probe: the bond after three mornings, the argue beat, the solo day, the resumed pair.

## Street trades, second wave — the twenty-fifth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-12; five wishes in one day on one subject — **a street artisan who takes a thing from your hands and gives it back changed**: a knife-grinder at a treadle (written twice, once into the owner's "Money is real" section and once moved out of it on run 144), a cobbler tapping tacks into an iron last, a bookbinder's screw press that turns loose leaves into a bound ledger, a cooper driving hoops over staves into a barrel. Merged on run 145 in build order, one slice per item; the five wish lines deleted. Run 144 had noted that a third repair-trade wish would make a system — this is it, and it is the second wave of "Trades on the street" (runs 80–82: the cobbler's bench, the cutler's wheel, the tailor's stitchhouse), so the first two slices *deepen* those benches instead of building duplicates. Rules for every slice: a trade is a `trades.json` entry worked by `town_trades.gd` like the first wave (a bench prop, a seeded resident with a door job, a pose per verb, a thing taken and a thing given back); customers come because of work done (woodcutting, the garden plot, walking, letters), never on a timer alone; prices and wages read `prices.json` ("Money is real"); residents bring their own items and the player may; nothing refuses the plain walk past. Builds after the owner-priority sections at the end of this file, like "Bells and clocks". Run 147 folded in the day's three later wishes: an umbrella mender's trestle became slice 2.6, and a grindstone barrow and a cobbler's pegging trestle restated 2.1 and 2.2 — the three wish lines deleted.)
- [ ] (trades 2.1 — the treadle; both knife-grinder wishes) The cutler's wheel (run 81) gets a treadle: the stone turns only while a foot pumps — a `treadle` pose (one foot on the pedal rocking at 2 Hz, the hands steadying the blade to the stone, the torso leaning in on each push) for the cutler and for a player who takes the pedal (C at the pedal while the cutler holds the blade — two bodies, one wheel), friction sparks as a 0.3 s `CPUParticles3D` burst on the push, and a new dull-blade reason: the axe after 6 chops (run 90) and the trowel after 4 diggings (run 70) go dull (a `dull` flag on the item; the chop pose slows 20%) and come back sharp. The wheel on a hand-cart (the second wish's cart) waits for slice 2.5.
- [ ] (trades 2.2 — the last) The cobbler's bench (run 80) gets an iron shoe last and brass tacks: `tack` pose (the left hand holds the boot on the last, the right hammers three taps and reaches for the next tack — a beat of four), worn boots from distance walked (every 600 m walked marks the player's and a resident's boots `worn` — the walk gets a 0.1 s longer stride-contact, visible), returned with a reinforced heel (a darker heel box on the boot mesh) that resets the distance; residents whose job walks (the postman, the sweeper) come first.
- [ ] (trades 2.3 — the press; a new trade) A bookbinder's screw press beside the letter room (run 99): `press` pose (both hands on the spindle's bar, a half turn and a lean each beat, then the bone folder along the edge — a windup, a hold, a recovery), takes loose leaves (three or more notes from the noticeboard of run 100 or letters) and gives a bound ledger item that the ledger table (run 112) buys for the price in `prices.json`; the binder resident binds the town's own week of notices every Monday and shelves it in the book box (run 98).
- [ ] (trades 2.4 — the block; a new trade) A cooper's trussing block near the woodcutter's stack (run 90): `hoop` pose (the mallet lifted over the head and driven down on the hoop, the body dropping with it, three strikes then a step round the barrel), takes four planks and gives a barrel item (carry with `plonk`, run 117) that the general store (run 114) buys and the rain barrels of "Weather people feel" fill — the first trade whose product another system consumes.
- [ ] (trades 2.5 — the cart and the memory) The knife-grinder's wheel goes on a hand-cart (the second wish) that the cutler pushes to a different street each day (the north lane, the market, the quay) — `push` pose for the cart, residents who hear the wheel bring their blades; and every artisan keeps a per-player tally in the saved minds (reputation, not XP): the fourth job is free, a line of thanks, and a resident who was turned down for not paying tells the next bench.
- [ ] (trades 2.6 — the ribs; the day's sixth wish, folded in on run 147) An umbrella mender's trestle on the street beside the umbrella rack (run 76): `lever` pose (one hand clamps the canopy to the trestle, the other works a wooden spoke lever in three pushes — a windup, a push, a recovery — then both hands snap the canopy open), takes a bent umbrella (one carried through a storm, `storm` of run 74, or one that was thrown, is marked `bent` in its meta and folds crooked) and gives it back straight with a crisp snap; residents bring their own after rain, the player may; `prices.json` `buy.mend_umbrella`; the mender says `mind.line("mend")`.

## Bells and clocks — the twenty-fourth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-03 and 2026-10-11; three wishes on one subject — **a staffed civic signal, a clock or a bell, that sets one town-wide state everything else answers**: a post-office clock niche with a timekeeper who winds it and sets your watch, a rooftop clocktower whose clockkeeper's hourly chimes light the street lamps at dusk, open the night stalls and send residents to bed, a weather house on the quay whose keeper rings a bell for fog, rain and heat and sets a town-wide visibility the lanterns, the umbrella stall, the basins and the boats read. Merged on run 143 in build order, one slice per item; the three wish lines deleted (the clock niche had waited since 2026-10-03, the two 2026-10-11 wishes were written into the owner's "Money is real" section and moved on runs 140 and 142, where run 142 noted that a third clock wish would make a system — this is it). Rules for every slice: there is one town clock (`town_base`'s day fraction and `is_night()`), the chimes and the bell *read* it and *announce* it, they never own a second one; every announcement is a state other systems read (`town.hour`, `town.visibility`), never a direct call into lamps or stalls; the keepers are seeded residents with door jobs like the baker, speak with `mind.line` categories in `minds.json`, and the player can do their verbs (wind, strike, ring) with the same poses; "Mist Quay" does not exist in `game/` — the jetty (run 78) is the quay; nothing refuses, costs or blocks the plain walk. Builds after the owner-priority sections at the end of this file, like The rounds.)
- [ ] (bells 1) The clock niche: a clock face set into the post office's street wall (run 99's letter room; a 0.5 m dial with an hour hand and a minute hand that turn with the town clock, paper face, ink numerals, a mauve minute hand — the first place in the town that shows the time), a timekeeper resident (door job "timekeeper", seeded, a small key on `socket_belt`) who comes out twice a day (at 0.25 and 0.75 of the day) and winds it with a `wind` pose (the key hand turning at chest height in eight quarter turns with a pause between, the other hand flat on the wall, the head close to the dial; windup, eight turns, a step back to read it), then says one of three `minds.json` "clock" lines ("Four minutes slow. Acceptable."); C at the dial sets the player's watch — a toast "Town time: 14:20" in the deadpan tone, and the HUD's weather line gains the hour; the player can wind it too with the same pose (the keeper watches, `mind.befriend()`); `probe_clock.gd` wants the hands' angles at 0, 0.25, 0.5 and 0.9 of the day.
- [ ] (bells 2) The clocktower and the hour: a clocktower on the square's roofline (built by `town_build`'s `_house` + a 3 m tower with the niche's dial on all four faces and a bell under a cap — structural: a new enterable interior behind the square's east door, the clock room with the weights and the escapement, one ladder, the keeper's chair), a clockkeeper resident (door job "clockkeeper") who climbs to the bell room before each town hour and strikes the hour (`strike` pose: a mallet in two hands, a windup over the shoulder, the swing, the ring and a recoil — once per hour, so one at 1:00 and twelve at noon); the strike sets `town.hour` and emits `hour_struck(h)` in `town_base`, and three systems now read it instead of the raw day clock: the street lamps light on the dusk strike (not on `is_night()`'s edge — the lamplighter of "A lamplighter by name" starts his round on it), the night stalls of the rounds open on it, and `resident_mind`'s sleep need rises on the 22:00 strike; the player can strike it (C at the mallet; the keeper lets you once a day, `mind.line("clock")`), and a wrong strike (one too many) is a `mind.score` "gossip" term for the day.
- [ ] (bells 3) The weather bell at the jetty: a small weather house beside the jetty (run 78; a hut with a barometer dial and a bell on a bracket), a weatherkeeper resident (door job "weatherkeeper") who rings a pattern when the weather changes — two strokes for rain, three for fog, one long for a clear hot afternoon (the rounds' "hot afternoon") — with a `ring` pose (one hand on the bell rope, pulled down and let go, the body dipping with each pull); the ring sets `town.visibility` 0..1 (clear 1, rain 0.6, fog 0.3) in `town_base` and emits `weather_rung(kind)`; the lamps light early under 0.5, the umbrella rack (run 76) is where residents go at two strokes (a `mind.score` "shelter" term, not a fixed roll), the punt stays moored under 0.4, and the splash basins' and the boats' later slices read the same value; the player can ring the pattern (C at the rope, the keeper corrects a wrong pattern with a line), and a correct ring before the keeper's earns 1 coin from `prices.json`'s new `weather` prize once a day.

## The rounds — the twenty-third system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-11; five fresh wishes on one subject — **a municipal worker on a timed circuit through the town, with a vehicle or an animal, serving whoever is on the route**: a lamplighter on a high-wheel cargo tricycle at dusk, a night watch bellman with a leashed mastiff from dusk to dawn, a donkey-drawn cistern cart on hot afternoons, a horse-drawn night omnibus from dusk to dawn, a river ice barge at the quay on hot afternoons. Merged on run 141 in build order, one slice per item; the five wish lines deleted (four had been written into the owner's "Money is real" section, the tricycle one sat under "Wishes from the town" and extended "A lamplighter by name" under Jobs and vehicles and "The lamplighter's cart" under Grows from Star lantern — those two stay as the lamplighter's job and the tower road's lanterns; rounds 1 is the vehicle). Rules for every slice: the round is a real route through real places (the street, the square, the park, the riverpath, the jetty) on the town clock (`is_night()`; "a hot afternoon" is weather `clear` with the day between 0.45 and 0.7 — there is no heat state yet, so the cart and the barge come when the sky is clear, and heat itself is a later "Weather in `game/`" slice); the worker is a seeded resident with a door job like the baker, drives or leads through the same `Car3D`/`quad3d.gd` code a player could use, and speaks with `mind.line` categories in `minds.json`; every animal meets the animal standard or is not built; the player can do the worker's verbs (ring the bell, pour the bucket, ride the omnibus, carry an ice block) and residents answer through a `mind.score` term, never a fixed roll; nothing refuses, costs or blocks the plain walk — a round passes you, it never pens you in; "Mist Quay" does not exist in `game/` — the jetty (run 78) is the quay. Builds after the owner-priority sections at the end of this file, like Animals that read your pace.)
- [ ] (rounds 1) The lamplighter's tricycle: `game/scripts/trike3d.gd` — a high-wheel cargo tricycle (one 0.7 m front wheel, two small rear wheels under a crate, pedals the rider's feet turn with a `pedal` pose: the knees pump at the wheel's rate, the hands on the bars, the back straight; Kenney/Quaternius CC0 parts where they beat primitives, LICENSE beside them) driven through `Car3D`'s `input` so a player can ride it too (C at the crate while it is parked); at `is_night()` the lamplighter (the seeded resident from "A lamplighter by name") rides it along the street and the riverpath at walking speed, stops at each lamp for the `light` pose, pulls an oilskin coat from the crate when the weather turns to rain (`don` on the move; the coat a wearable with a socket, worn until dawn) and parks it at the tram stop overnight; at dawn the reverse round. Probe: the round visits every lamp, the oilskin goes on in rain only, the trike is parked at the stop by 0.35, the player can ride it while it is parked.
- [ ] (rounds 2) The night watch bellman and the mastiff: a seeded resident (job "watch") with an iron handbell (a 0.25 m bell with a clapper on `hand_r`) and `mastiff3d.gd` on `quad3d.gd` with the full dog standard (play bow, roll) plus a leash (a rope from the collar to the hand re-laid every frame like the zip line's cable, 1.8 m — the dog never further) who walk the square and the park from dusk to dawn; at each lamp the `ring` pose (0.3 s the bell rises to the shoulder, three 0.25 s forearm swings with the clapper wav, 0.3 s lower) and `mind.line("watch")` naming the hour and the weather ("Eleven. Clear." — three lines per weather in `minds.json`); a resident asleep on a bench (run 108's nap) within 3 m is nudged — the bellman's `pat` on the shoulder, the sleeper wakes into the stretch (aftermath 1) with `mind.line("nudged")` and goes home through a "home" `mind.score` term; the player C at the bellman borrows the bell for one ring. Probe: the round, a ring at each lamp with the right hour word, a bench nap ended within 10 s of the pass, the leash length, the dog's two species moves.
- [ ] (rounds 3) The cistern cart: `donkey3d.gd` on the quad rig (every minimum motion; two species moves: the ear-pinned bray and the stubborn stop — four legs planted, head down, 2 s) hitched to a two-wheel cart with a wooden tank, a brass tap and four tin buckets on hooks; on a hot afternoon the road-damper (a seeded resident, job "damper") leads it (the `lead` pose: one arm back to the rope, short steps) down the street and round the square with a mist from the tank's rear nozzle (a `CPUParticles3D` fan, 2 m, 0.6 opacity) that settles dust — the skid dust cars raise lives 40 % shorter on a damped road for 60 s — and at each drinking bucket by a workplace (the bakery, the cobbler, the cutler, the garden plot — new 0.3 m buckets on posts) the `pour` pose refills it; a working resident with a new "thirst" need (it rises with waged work) drinks there (`drink`) and says `mind.line("water")`; the player C at a full bucket drinks the same way and the bucket drops a third. Probe: the round on a hot afternoon only, the buckets refilled in route order, a worker drinks within 30 s, the dust lifetime on the damped road, the donkey's bray at a sprint past (the pace rule).
- [ ] (rounds 4) The night omnibus: the post-office horse (pace 2's `horse3d.gd`) in harness to an omnibus body — a long `Car3D` spec "omnibus" with no engine sound, two benches under a roof, three steps at the back, a lamp at each corner — driven by a seeded driver (job "omnibus") on a loop street → square → riverpath → street from dusk to dawn at walking speed, the horse's walk cycle at the cart's speed; a resident caught in rain without an umbrella (run 76) with the omnibus within 10 m boards through a "shelter" `mind.score` term (the `board` pose, then `sit` on the bench) and rides until the rain stops; at 0.35 (sunrise) every rider whose "rest" need is high is driven to their own door and steps off into the stretch; the player C at the back steps boards and rides (the camera follows; C steps off anywhere). Probe: the loop closes, a resident in rain boards and leaves when the rain stops, a sleepy rider is at their door by 0.4, the player's ride.
- [ ] (rounds 5) The ice barge: a flat barge (`town_boat.gd`'s hull stretched to 5 m, no pole — a tow line to a bank post the ice-cutter winds with the `moor` pose) that comes to the jetty on a hot afternoon with six ice blocks (0.4 m cubes in a translucent `_mat` that shrink 10 % a minute in the open and are gone by dusk); the ice-cutter (a seeded resident, job "ice") carries one block at a time (`carry` two-handed at 0.6× walk) to the café's and the market stall's counters — a new "ice" counter prop: the block goes into a tin box and the counter's cold drinks become sellable from `prices.json`, the first stock that melts — and residents gather on the bank through a "cool" `mind.score` term (a sit on the bank and a `fan` pose: one hand fanning the face in 2 s loops) saying `mind.line("ice")`; the player C at the barge takes a block, carries it (the same 0.6× walk, the block melting in the hands) and sells it at a counter for the `prices.json` ice price. Probe: the barge docks on a hot afternoon only, six blocks become two counters' stock, a block carried 90 s is 15 % smaller, three residents on the bank within 60 s, the player's sale.

## Animals that read your pace — the twenty-second system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-10; five fresh wishes on one subject — **an animal that answers how you move**: a goose that pecks sprinters and bows to walkers, a hitched horse that stomps at a sprint and lowers its muzzle to a walk, a tethered goat that bleats and butts a sprint into its rope, a terrier that follows bread scraps and sits when you pause, a tomcat that purrs for a still hand and bolts up a tree from a run or a shove. Merged on run 136 in build order, one slice per item; the five wish lines deleted (three had been written into the owner's "Money is real" section, two sat under "Wishes from the town"). Rules for every slice: the animal standard holds (`quad3d.gd` or `bird3d.gd` rig, every minimum motion or nothing); the animal's two species moves *are* its answer to the pace; the pace is read from the body's own speed (over the run threshold = a sprint, under it = a walk, no movement for 1 s = still) and residents trigger the same answers when their routine runs or walks past (a resident late for a shift gets honked at too); nothing refuses, costs or stops the plain walk — a peck, a stomp or a butt is a flinch (`hurt` 0.4, the existing stumble), never a knock-down; each ships with a `mind.line` category in `minds.json` for the resident who owns or feeds it, and a probe. Builds after the owner-priority sections at the end of this file, like Aftermath motions.)
- [ ] (pace 1) The park goose: `game/scripts/goose3d.gd` on the bird rig (bob, peck, flap, hop) plus a waddle (a side-to-side four-beat with the tail wag), a neck-bow and a hiss-honk (neck out, wings half, a two-tone honk wav) — it waddles a 12 m loop on the park path; a body passing within 2 m at a sprint → the honk and a 1.2 m chase with one peck (`hurt` 0.4 stumble; residents within 8 m look); at a walk → the neck bows for 0.8 s and the goose steps aside; the plain walk past is untouched. Joggers (busy 3) get honked, strollers bowed to; the park keeper says `mind.line("goose")`. Probe: a sprint past triggers the peck, a walk the bow, the loop closes, nothing beyond 2 m.
- [ ] (pace 2) The post-office horse: `horse3d.gd` on `quad3d.gd` with the full minimum set (idle with blink and ear flicks, walk, run, a rest-stand with one hind hoof cocked for "sit", lie, stretch, look at the player, reaction, tail swish, two species moves: hoof stomp with a snort and the muzzle-lower) hitched to a rail outside the post office (run 99's letter room): a sprint within 3 m → the stomp and the snort (head tossed, a dust puff); a walk up to it → the muzzle lowers and C pats it (`pat` pose: one hand out flat along the neck, 1.2 s, the ear flicks forward); the postman pats it at shift start and says `mind.line("horse")`. Probe: the stomp at a sprint, the pat at a walk, no reaction beyond 3 m, the hitch holds (the horse never leaves the rail).
- [ ] (pace 3) The market goat: `goat3d.gd` on the quad rig (idle chew with the jaw moving, walk, trot, run, sit, lie, stretch, look, flee to the rope's end, tail flick, two species moves: the bleat and the head-butt) tethered on a 2.5 m lead by the market stall, chewing vegetable scraps from the stall's crate (one per hour; the stallkeeper refills it like the bakery); a body sprinting into the rope's circle → a bleat and, one time in two, a butt (`hurt` 0.4 stumble — the plain outcome stays at 50 %: the goat only bleats); a walk → it comes to the rope's end and sniffs the hand. Probe: the butt rate over 20 sprints is 7..13, a walk never, the chew counter drops, the lead's length holds. (The town's 2026-10-10 stray-goat wish — a scruffy goat trotting over to inspect a standing body for bread scraps, a blunt butt if shoved, then off behind the nearest tree — was written into the "Money is real" section and is folded in here on run 137: the goat's "still" answer is the scrap inspection, muzzle to the body's hand and `mind.line("goat_sniff")`; a shove gets the butt and a trot to the rope's end behind the stall.)
- [ ] (pace 4) The stray terrier: `dog3d.gd` on the quad rig with the dog standard (play bow, roll) — unowned, it sleeps under the bakery's eave; a body carrying bread or scraps gets a follower at 3 m (the dog has a small mind of its own with one need, hunger, and a `mind.score` term "scraps"), it sits when the body stops (0.6 s sit) and barks once with a head jerk when someone within 10 m is shoved (the `_chase` hit event); C with scraps in hand → the existing `pass` and the dog eats, wags, and follows for 60 s more. Residents who buy bread (takeaway) get the same follower and say `mind.line("dog")`. Probe: the follow distance, the sit on stop, the bark on a shove, the feed and the 60 s.
- [ ] (pace 5) The ginger tomcat: `cat3d.gd` on the quad rig with the cat standard (groom, arch) — it roosts on the three low walls and the park bench in turn (20..60 s each, a jump up and down with the spine stretch); a body still within 1 m for 1 s → the eyes half-close and C strokes it (`stroke` pose: bent at the waist, one hand along the back, 1.5 s; a purr wav looped while the hand moves); a body running within 6 m, or a shove within 6 m → the cat darts up the nearest tree (`climb` on the trunk, 0.8 s) and comes down after 30 s. Residents on a bench stroke it through a "cat" `mind.score` term and say `mind.line("cat")`. Probe: the stroke at a still approach, the bolt at a run and at a shove, the roost rotation, the climb-down.

## Aftermath motions — the twenty-first system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-09; three fresh wishes on one subject — **the body carries what just happened for a few seconds**: a stretch after sitting, a limp after a knock-down, a shiver when idle near water or in damp weather. Merged on run 133 in build order, one slice per item; the three wish lines deleted (the shiver one had been written into the owner's "Money is real" section). Rules for every slice: each is its own `pose_request` with a windup, a hold and a recovery (never a still frame, never a borrowed pose); the player does it too (standing from a seat, getting up after a knock-down, idling by the river) and residents do it through their minds (a `mind.score` term, not a fixed roll); the plain outcome is untouched — the walk resumes on its own, nothing refuses, nothing slows for longer than the motion itself. Builds after the owner-priority sections at the end of this file; the "Recoveries" idea under "Grows from Sliding bookcase" is the same family for the tower's poses and should share the file.)
- [ ] (aftermath 1) The stretch after sitting: `game/scripts/stick3d_after.gd` with `stretch` — 0.3 s both arms rise and the chest opens back, a 2.2 s hold with a slow side sway and a yawn-shaped head tilt, 0.5 s the arms come down; `resident_life` plays it when a seat routine ends (bench, chair, low wall) before the next pick, the player gets it when standing from any seat after at least 8 s seated; a resident nearby says `mind.line("stretch")` (three deadpan lines in `minds.json`). Probe: a seated resident stands into the pose for 3.0 s and walks only after; a 3 s seat gets no stretch.
- [ ] (aftermath 2) The limp after a knock-down: `limp` in the same file — a 3 s uneven gait layered on the walk (one knee held 0.3 bent, that side's stride 40% shorter, the torso dips on each of its steps, the near hand on the hip for the first second; the side is the one the hit came from), set when a body gets up from `down` (residents in `_chase`, the player in `town_player`'s get-up), walking at 0.7× for those 3 s, then the plain stride. Probe: after a knock-down the first 3 s of walk show the stride asymmetry and 0.7× speed, then normal; a body that was never down never limps.
- [ ] (aftermath 3) The shiver by the water: `shiver` in the same file — after 10 s stationary outdoors within 6 m of water (the river, the lake, the pond) or in rain without an umbrella, 2 s: the shoulders tuck up, both arms cross the chest, a 9 Hz tremor in the elbows and a quick head shake, 0.4 s release; the resident's mind gets a "cold" need term that sends them to the stove or an eave and a `mind.line("cold")`; the player shivers idle on the jetty. Probe: a resident idle 10 s on the jetty shivers once and again after another 10 s, never while walking, never under an umbrella.

## Money in hands — the twentieth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-05, written into "Later — phone" by mistake again and moved on vision run 107; all three fresh wishes were one design — **a coin that passes between people**: a busker whose hat collects tips, an IOU ledger table where a resident reads their tab and drops a copper, an odd-jobs board where one resident pays another a coin for a task. None of it could exist without the coin item twelve "Economy in `game/`" lines had waited on since polish run 71, so slice 1 is that canonical line, moved here and built; slice 2 is the canonical "The counter charges", moved too; then the three wishes in build order, one slice each. Three wish lines deleted. Run 110: four more 2026-10-05 wishes were written into "Later — phone" again and were this system a second time — busking and odd jobs were already slices 3 and 5 (lines deleted, their extra notes folded in), the stale crust and the pawn post became slices 6 and 7. Run 113: the same five 2026-10-05 lines (odd jobs, pawnbroker, Safe, busking stage, raffle) were in "Later — phone" a fifth time and are slices 5, 7, 8, 3 and 9 — deleted again. Rules for every slice: a coin is always a real item that moves (ground → hand → pocket, pocket → dish) with the `stoop`/`pass` beats, never a number that changes; the plain outcome stays reachable at zero coins — nothing in the town refuses service for want of a coin; residents pay from their own pocket the way the player does; the other nine "Economy in `game/`" lines stay where they are and now build. Run 111: the same two 2026-10-05 wish lines (odd-jobs board, busking stage) were written into "Later — phone" a third time and deleted again — slices 3 and 5 already carry them; slice 3 built. Run 112: five 2026-10-05 wish lines were in "Later — phone" again and all five were coins — the odd-jobs board and the busking stage for the fourth time and the pawnbroker a second time (deleted; slices 5, 3 and 7 carry them), the Small-Change Safe and the penny-pool raffle were new and became slices 8 and 9; slice 4 built)
- [ ] The stale crust (slice 6, economy + relationships — the 2026-10-05 "short on coins" wish): at the 17:00 closing the baker sets one unsold loaf aside on the dish end as a `crust` item (the bread mesh at 0.7 scale, darker `c9a26b`, two bites instead of three); a resident arriving at the counter with `coins == 0` (the run 110 `no_coin` branch) first plays a new `pockets` pose (0.2s both hands slap the hips, 0.5s both turn outward palms-up with the elbows out and the head tilting, 0.3s drop) and, if a crust is on the dish, takes that instead of a loaf and eats it in two bites with `mind.line("crust")` (four lines); with no crust the loaf is on the house as today. The player at the counter with an empty pocket gets the same choice (toast "A crust, or on the house." — C again takes the loaf). Plain outcome: everyone still eats.
- [ ] The Small-Change Safe (slice 8, economy + places — the 2026-10-05 deposit-booth wish): a squat iron box (0.8×1.1×0.7, `_box`, ink-grey, a mauve dial disc on the front) under a small roof on the east side of the square outside the road band, and a `clerk` job for one seeded adult (`uid % 24 == 9`) who stands behind it 09:00–16:00; `C` with a coin while the clerk is there hands one over with `pass` (run 86) and the clerk plays a new `dial` pose (0.3 s the right hand to the dial, 0.5 s two turns of the wrist, 0.3 s the door swings on a `set_door` tween and the hand drops the coin in) — the coin leaves the pocket and `safe[uid]` counts it (a paper tag on the shelf per person, up to three); `C` again with an empty pocket asks it back after a 20 s hold (`held_until`, "Give it a minute." until then): the clerk dials and the coin comes out with `pass` into the hand → pocket. A knocked-down person never drops safe coins (`drop_coin` reads the pocket only). Residents deposit on a pass when `coins >= 3` and `mind.brave < 0.4` (one per day) and withdraw when they hit the counter's `no_coin` branch before it goes on the tab. `mind.line("safe")` four lines. Plain outcome: three coins at most and the clerk is away two-thirds of the day; nothing needs it.
- [ ] The penny-pool raffle (slice 9, economy + quests — the 2026-10-05 raffle wish): a drum on a stand by the fourth market stall (a short cylinder on an axle with a crank handle, `_box` legs, outside the road band) with a slot on top; `C` with a coin drops one in with the `palm` timings reversed (hand from the pocket to the slot at PALM_AWAY) and gets a paper `ticket` (the `paper` item at 0.6 scale with a mauve stripe, meta `draw` = the day) in the hand; residents with `coins >= 2` and `curious > 0.6` buy one on a pass (one per day). At 16:00 the busker (until "Stallholders by name" ships; then stall four's holder) cranks the drum with a new `crank` pose (0.3 s hand to the handle, 1.2 s two full turns with the shoulder leading, 0.3 s release) and one ticket wins: the pool comes back as real coins (`drop_coin` ×n from the slot onto the ground, bouncing — the winner stoops them one by one), a resident winner says `mind.line("won")`; losing tickets go in the nearest bin on the next pass. Plain outcome: the pool is a handful and the drum takes a coin only when you choose to.

## Elders and children — the nineteenth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-04, written into "Later — phone" by mistake again and moved on polish run 101; all five fresh wishes were one design — **an older resident who looks after the town's children at a place of their own**: a Story Sunroom where a storysitter reads to queued children, a "Grand Rattle" cart an elder pushes with children aboard, a Lullaby Lodge where an elder settles children for a nap, a Patch & Porch where an elder tutor makes a keepsake with a child, a Memory Post where an elder answers children's notes. Not five buildings; one house and one keeper with five things to do. The 3D town has no children yet, so slice 1 is the `game/` half of the old canonical "Children, and the population growing" line (it stays where it is for the web). Two older wishes were folded, not merged: the 2026-10-03 Listening Bench (a resident who tells one memory line to whoever sits) is the elder's line in slice 2; the 2026-10-03 mender's corner is the tailor (run 82) and "Hand a torn thing over the table" under Items — no second counter. Seven wish lines deleted. Not built this run — run 101 is a polish run; the next feature run builds slice 1. Rules for every slice: children are residents (`ResidentBase`, `fig.base_scale` 0.62, never `fig.scale`) with the same poses as everyone, scaled; a punch or kick at a child lands above their head (the strike line is adult shoulder height) and they step back — nobody knocks a child over, a rule of the world rather than a resident-only power; cars already stop for anyone in a lane; the player can join every activity in the child's place (sit on the cushion, ride the cart, take the lesson) with the same pose; the plain `C` take is never blocked; no coin. Run 102 found four more fresh 2026-10-04 wishes in "Later — phone", all this system again: a Story House with a storyteller = slice 2 (its "calms shaken residents" half: a resident just up from `down` who sits on a cushion stands up a second sooner — no new wrinkle on being hit); The Silver & Spark community centre with a programme coordinator = slices 2–3, the same house and keeper, not a second building; a horse-trolley with an elder driver = slice 5's Grand Rattle (no horse — the Animal standard has no horse rig yet); a Toy Lending & Repair House = new slice 7 below. Wish lines deleted. Run 104 found three more fresh 2026-10-04 wishes in "Later — phone", all this system a third time: a park-edge "Dayhouse" with a daykeeper (toy shelf, storytelling, sit-and-stretch pairs for toddlers and elders) = the Sunroom and its slices 2 + 7, with the stretch as new slice 9 below; a "Hand-and-Heart Workshop" where an elder makes a keepsake with a child = slice 3 (the Patch & Porch star; the painted bird is a second keepsake for a later slice, not a second building); a story-boat at the boathouse pairing one elder and one child = new slice 8 below on the existing punt. Wish lines deleted.) Run 106 found three more fresh 2026-10-04 wishes in "Later — phone", this system a fourth time: a Hearthhouse story circle pairing one elder and one child on a stage = slice 2 (the Sunroom rug and the storysitter; the "story-card" token is the lent book of run 106 — no token item); a Play & Learn school with a craftkeeper folding boats and tying knots = slice 3 (the Patch & Porch lesson — add a `boat` fold as its second product when slice 3 ships, not a second building); a slow tram with a tram-keeper shuttling one elder and one child = slice 5 (the Grand Rattle — the cart is the tram; the storysitter is the keeper). Three wish lines deleted.
- [ ] The Patch & Porch lesson (slice 3, items/interaction): a low table on the Sunroom's porch; mornings 10:00–11:00 the storysitter and one child sit at it and play a new `fold` pose (0.3s pick a corner, 0.5s crease with the heel of the hand, 0.3s turn it over — three folds) that turns a `paper` into a `star` item (five-point flat star, `f2d16b`, r 0.05); the child carries the star home and leaves it on their house's `shelf` spot (it stays visible there, at most three per shelf); the player `C` at the table holding a paper takes the same lesson and gets a star (`C` with a star at a resident = give; a parent who gets one says `mind.line("star")` and `mind.befriend`s the player).
- [ ] The Memory Post (slice 4, builds on Letters and notes): a small red-lidded slot box on the Sunroom's front wall (`memory` spot); children walking past drop a `paper` drawing in it (`sort` pose, the run 99 pose, at child height), the player can too; at 09:00 the storysitter empties it (a `pin`-cycle per paper, the run 100 count bundle) and pins one reply on the square noticeboard — a note with a mauve border (`kind` "reply") that a child who reads the board takes home; the box is visible as full (lid ajar) when it has three or more.
- [ ] The Grand Rattle (slice 5, vehicles axis): a four-wheeled wooden hand cart with two bench seats and a bell parked beside the Sunroom (`Car3D`-free: a `Node3D` moved along a fixed loop of path points — lane → square's north edge → back, never inside the road band z −0.1..4.1); at 11:00 the storysitter pushes it (the `push` pose family, wheels turning with distance) with up to two children seated (`seat` pose, bouncing 1cm on a 3Hz wobble), rings the bell at each corner (`ring`: 0.2s reach, two shakes, 0.2s back); the player `C` on a seat rides, `C` at the handle pushes it along the same loop and children climb in.
- [ ] The toy shelf (slice 7, items axis — the Toy Lending wish, and the 2026-10-04 Play & Tell wish's toy-check booth; that wish's scheduled story sessions drawing children are run 103, so the line was folded here on run 107): a low shelf on the Sunroom porch holding three toys (`top`: a turned cone with a stripe; `ball`: a mauve sphere r 0.07; `hoop`: a thin torus r 0.22) as real items; children take one in the morning and play with it near their parent (`spin`: 0.2s crouch, 0.3s twist of the wrist, then watching it turn for 2s; the ball is thrown and fetched with the existing throw), and put it back by 17:00; a toy that is dropped and stepped on loses its stripe (`worn` meta) and the storysitter mends it at the porch table with `sew`. The player `C` at the shelf takes or returns a toy the same way; nothing is lent on a timer and nothing is refused.
- [ ] The story punt (slice 8, vehicles/relationships axis — the story-boat wish, on the run 78 punt and the run 94 south landing): on clear days at 11:30 the storysitter walks to the north jetty, boards the punt as the rower and one child boards the stern thwart ("Rowing with a passenger's weight" under Grows from Jetty and punt, built here if still open); they pole to the south landing and back (the existing `row`/`moor` chain) while the storysitter says one `mind.line("story")` per crossing and the child answers `mind.line("kid_ask")` (new category, four lines); the player `C` on the stern thwart while the storysitter rows takes the child's seat and hears the same line; a knocked-over punt (the water rule, run 78) ends the ride at the nearest bank with both walking home, no new wrinkle.
- [ ] The rug stretch (slice 9, motions axis — the Dayhouse wish's "sit-and-stretch pairs"): a new `unfold` pose (0.3s both arms up overhead with the fists turned in, 0.6s hold with the back arching and one knee lifting to shake the leg out, 0.4s arms drop and the knee sets down) played by anyone who stands after sitting `crossleg` 20s or more — children and shaken adults in `_leave`, the player in `_story_tick` after `cross_up` — and, 14:40–14:45, by the storysitter and the first child side by side on the rug before the book opens (the pair face each other, the child half a beat late); the player on a cushion at that time joins the same way with `C`.

- [ ] (relationships + Climb, 2026-10-17 — the relationships axis, thinnest at 104 open lines by whole-word keyword; wider, from run 171: the Wind cliffs floors now hold two tower residents on hop tops — the hurdler and the high jumper — who never look at each other) Floor-mates: when the hurdler and the jumper stand on the same floor (both `plan`s on band n — the loader already passes the row as `taken` to the bar, so the pack knows the row's `cx`), each turns its head (`look_yaw` toward the other, 1 s) when the other leaves `idle`, and when the other's run ends well (the hurdler's `_rest` after a row with no bump, the jumper's `clears` going up) the watcher claps — a new `clap` pose (hands meet three times at chest height, 0.25 s a beat, the shoulders lifting with each, a nod at the end — never a still frame); a knock or a bump gets a slow head-shake instead (`look_yaw` −0.3..0.3 over 0.8 s, no new pose); the camp note adds one line a tower-day ("the hurdler and the jumper on 22 have cheered each other 4 times today" — a per-floor `cheers` tally, reset with `day()`); the player gets the same clap from both when their own vault or clear is counted on that floor, and nothing when alone; `probe_hurdle`/`probe_bar` gain the look, the clap after the other's good run, the shake after a bad one, the tally.
## Grows from Children (run 102 — the first small residents; they follow, skip, and nobody knocks them over)
- [ ] Hand in hand over the road (relationships axis, builds on run 102): when a parent's route crosses the road band (z −0.1..4.1), their child walks on the parent's road-side-away hand and they hold hands across — new `hand` pose pair (0.2s reach, the arms meeting at the child's shoulder height and staying linked while walking, 0.2s let go on the far kerb); the child's `follow` target becomes the parent's hand point instead of 0.7m beside. The player walking past a child at the kerb with an empty hand and `C` leads them over the same way, and the parent says a `kid_found` line on the other side.
- [ ] Bedtime first (time axis, builds on run 102): children go home at 19:30 — an hour before the adults' `is_night` — and the parent walks them to the door (the same door route), opens it, sees them in, and comes back out to their evening; inside, the child lies on the bed slot (or the floor beside it, `rest`) and the house's window light dims one step. A child still out at 20:30 makes the parent use the `where_kid` fetch even within 12m.
- [ ] The dog and the children (animals axis, on the `quad3d.gd` dog): a child within 3m of the dog stops following for up to 8s, crouches (`pet`) and the dog answers with its play bow; one time in three the child skips off in a small circle and the dog bounds after it (the dog's run cycle); the parent stands and watches (`wait`) instead of walking on. The player's `C` at the dog with a child nearby invites the child in the same way.

## Letters and notes — the eighteenth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-03; three fresh wishes circled one design — **a paper that travels from person to person through a place**: an indoor Letter Room with a scribe and pigeonholes, a noticeboard on the square's north wall where a paper note is pinned and residents stop to read, a rooftop hatch where a messenger folds and drops notes to the street for anyone to catch. Merged run 99 in build order, one slice per item; the three wish lines are deleted. Three more fresh wishes the same day were folded, not merged: the seed-exchange shelf is "Leave one, take one" slice 2 (the seed tin), the reading alcove is the reading room / book-on-the-bench lines under "Grows from Book box", the staffed lost-and-found stall is "The Return Booth" (Trades slice 5) — wish lines deleted. The pantry, clock niche and boatsmith wishes were written under "Later — phone" by mistake and moved to "Wishes from the town". Rules for every slice: the plain `C` take is never blocked; a paper anyone drops is a paper anyone can pick up; residents post, take, pin and read with the same poses as players; no coin.)
- [ ] The rooftop drop (slice 3, interaction): on the stair house's flat roof (7, −4, reached by its outside stairs) a resident with a letter folds it (new `fold` pose: 0.3s bring both hands together → 0.5s two creases, the hands sliding apart and back → 0.2s pinch the point) and throws it over the front parapet; it glides down in 2–3s (a `flying` entry with low gravity and a side-to-side sway, rotation following the swing) and lands as an item on the street; a person standing within 0.8m of where it will land can catch it with `C` while it falls (the `grab` action, it goes to the hand); residents within 8m who see one land walk to it one time in two. Plain outcome: it lands and lies there.
- [ ] The morning round (slice 4, jobs): at 10:00 the scribe takes up to three letters (stock −3) and walks them to three house doors other than their own, posting each through the door with the `sort` reach at door height (a letter appears on that house's floor inside the door); the owner of that house picks it up next time they leave and reads it on the step (`read` 3–5s, `mind.line("letter_got")`); the player can do the same with `C` at any closed door while carrying a letter. A house that received a letter shows it from the street only by the resident reading on the step — no marker.
- [ ] Addressed letters (slice 5, relationships): a letter written at the desk (see "The scribe writes at dusk") carries a `to` (a friend's handle from the writer's `mind` ties, else a random resident); `C` on a letter in hand shows `To: <handle>` as a toast before reading; the scribe's round takes a letter to the addressee's door; handing it to the addressee yourself (`give_to_resident`) makes them `befriend` the writer +0.03 and say `mind.line("letter_from")` — a small reputation effect for the carrier (fond +0.02), no counter shown.

## Leave one, take one — the seventeenth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-03, written by the town into the "Later — phone" section by mistake and moved on polish run 97; three of five were one design — **a small tended shelf where a single item is left and a single item taken, kept by one resident who tidies it and remembers who gave**: a book-exchange booth by the church, a seed-exchange corner in the park with a ledger board, a tool-lending step in Old Court with a return tag (the 3D town has no church or Old Court yet — the slices place them at the bakery, the plot and the woodyard instead). The other two were folded, not merged: the Lost & Found corner is "The Return Booth" (Trades slice 5) and the mender stall is the tailor (run 82) — no second counter for either. Wish lines deleted. Rules for every slice: one item in, one item out, no coin (the coin item does not exist yet); the plain `C` take is never blocked — taking without leaving works and the keeper only says a line; residents use the shelf in their routines exactly as players do, with the same poses.)
- [ ] The seed tin and the ledger (slice 2): a tin box with a hinged lid and a small slate ledger on a post at the garden plot's gate; `seed` packets (new item: a folded paper 0.08×0.11, a coloured dot for tomato/cabbage/pumpkin) left by `C`, taken by `C`; a ledger chalk tally (one stroke per gift, cleared at dawn) drawn on the slate; a packet taken and `C` on an empty plot row plants it (the plot's existing grow stage starts at 0) — gardeners leave one spare packet when they pick a row bare. Pose `jot`: 0.3s raise the chalk, three 0.2s strokes, 0.3s lower.
- [ ] The tool step (slice 3): a two-step stone stoop beside the woodyard with a pegboard (hammer, saw, trowel); `C` takes a tool and hangs a paper tag with the borrower's colour on its empty peg; the tool does its one job (hammer → the repair `fix` pose on a crack works without the repairer; saw → cuts a log in two at the woodyard; trowel → plants a seed packet); `C` at the board with it hangs it back and the tag comes down. A resident who fixes a crack borrows the hammer first one time in three. The keeper calls an unreturned tag out once at dusk (`mind.line("tool_due")`), no penalty.
- [ ] Who gave what (slice 4, relationships): the keeper remembers the last three givers (uid or the player); a resident who takes something a friend left says `mind.line("from_friend")` and `befriend` +0.02 toward the giver; the player who left three things is greeted at the box with `mind.line("regular_giver")` — a reputation effect, no counter shown.

## Walking in pairs — the sixteenth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-02; all five fresh wishes were one design — **two residents linked as a pair who walk together, fall out when something breaks them apart, and make up at a place**: a paired walk that splits if shoved, a hand-over during the quarrel, a ribbon pinned on a promise board, a basket two people carry in step, a "pair's table" at the café for tea and talk. Merged run 90 in build order, one slice per item; the wish lines are deleted. Run 91 found four more fresh wishes that were this system again — a daily companion who walks your route and sits beside you and goes cold for a day if shoved = slices 1–2 (the "for a day" shortened to the 90s sulk: a whole day of cold is a block on a pair, not behaviour); a shared note the pair passes = new slice 7; a folded apology a player carries across = new slice 8; an apology post with a mailcarrier = slice 8's later half. Wish lines deleted. Run 94 found six more, all this system again — a bench-for-two with an apology token = slices 2 + 8; a shared ribbon with a low pair-post and a sheltered board = slice 6 (the pair-post is the same board, folded); an apology postbox = slice 8's later half; a half-loaf split for two = slice 2 with run 85's halves (a pair that sits to make up splits whatever one of them carries — no new stall); a mend stall for a broken shared keepsake = the tailor (run 82) mending the slice 5 basket, no new stall; a shared umbrella = new slice 9. Their "stolen by a player" halves are the existing plain take (always succeeds, breaks the link), not a new wrinkle. Wish lines deleted. Rules for every slice: pairs come from `mind.befriend` ties (`rel` friend in `minds.json` or affinity > 0.6), never assigned at random; the player can prompt a reconcile but never force a link; the quarrel is a few seconds of visible behaviour (a `bicker` pose with its own windup/hold/recovery), never a block on `C` or on taking; a shove still knocks over exactly as before — the split is a consequence, not a third variation of being hit.)
- [ ] The pair's table at the café (slice 3): a two-seat table spot outside the café counter (`kind` "pairs", two slots, off the road band z −0.1..4.1); a sulking pair that both reach it sits for a 10–12s "tea and talk" — two cups from the counter, alternating `drink` and `talk`, interruptible by any hit (no shield); finishing clears the sulk and both say a `mind.line("made_up")`; the player can sit in one seat with a resident they are fond of for the same beat (fond +0.03).
