- [ ] Making up is gossip (relationships, run 96 — builds on Make up at a bench): a resident within 6m of a `bicker` beat remembers it (a `_saw_quarrel` entry on the resident: the two uids and a 120s expiry); in their next `_chat_force` the opener is `mind.line("saw_quarrel")` naming neither ("Those two again.", "Words by the bench."), and if they then see the same two `makeup` they say `mind.line("saw_mend")` once (no pose: a 0.6s look with `look_yaw` toward the pair). No fond change, no block — it is only talk.
- [ ] The cold shoulder (relationships, polish run 97 — builds on the refusal fix: a refused make-up now stays cold until the sulk ends): while `_cold_until` holds, if the two pass within 3m both turn their heads away (`look_yaw` ±0.8 for 1.5s, the far shoulder dropping 0.1 — a `snub` pose with a 0.2s lift of the chin, a 1.0s hold, a 0.3s return) and one says `mind.line("snub")` one time in three; a witness within 6m counts it as `_saw_quarrel` for the gossip slice. No block on `C`, taking or walking.
- [ ] A peace offering (slice 4): during the `bicker` beat a resident carrying an item one time in three holds it out with the `pass` pose (run 86) instead of jabbing; the other takes it (`take_gift` path) and the sulk is cleared on the spot; the player holding food near a bickering pair can do the same with `C` toward either of them.
- [ ] A basket for two (slice 5): a wicker `basket` item (a shallow box with a hoop handle) at the market; a linked pair walking to the meadow carries it between them — both right/left hands on the handle (`carry` pose variant `carry2`), matched step; the player can take it (a plain take, always succeeds) which breaks the link and starts the bicker beat; set down on the meadow it becomes a picnic spot for two.
- [ ] The promise board (slice 6): a small cork board on a post by the fountain-side path (density, not structural) where a pair that made up pins a ribbon (a short coloured strip in their two figure colours, `pin` pose: 0.3s reach → 0.4s press with the thumb → 0.2s step back); ribbons stay a day; a ribbon taken by a human is just a ribbon (an item), and the pair who pinned it say a `mind.line("ribbon")` when they next pass the empty pin — no chase, no block.
- [ ] A shared note (slice 7, town wish 2026-10-02): a linked pair carries one folded `note` item (a small paper box, the existing `paper` look folded in half) that the inner walker hands to the other at each bench they pass (the `pass` pose, run 86); a human can take it like any item (always succeeds) — the pair then walk apart for the rest of the day instead of side by side, and resume when anyone sets the note back on that bench (`C` with it at the bench). No chase, no block.
- [ ] A folded apology (slice 8, town wishes 2026-10-02, two merged): a split pair's sulker leaves a folded `apology` item on the bench they last shared; the player can carry it to the other one (`give_to_resident`) and the `reconcile` beat of slice 2 plays wherever they stand; left a day, it blows off. Later half: a red apology post on the square (density) where residents drop one and a postie (Jobs: "A postie") delivers it on the next morning round — delayed make-ups over days.
- [ ] Under one umbrella (slice 9, town wish 2026-10-02): when rain starts and one of a linked pair holds an umbrella (`has_umb`) and the other does not, the holder steps in to 0.35m and tilts the canopy over both (`umbr` arm pushed 0.25 rad toward the partner, a new `share_umb` pose for the partner: 0.2s duck in → hold with the near shoulder hunched and the head tilted under → 0.2s straighten on leaving the rain); they walk at the holder's pace to the nearest eave instead of running; the player with an umbrella can do the same beside a resident they are fond of (fond +0.02 per minute). Taking the umbrella is the plain take (always succeeds) and starts the bicker beat; nothing else blocks.

## Sharing food — the fourteenth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-01; all four fresh wishes were one design — **food that passes from one person to another instead of being eaten alone**: splitting an item with whoever sits beside you, a one-second pass-plate between neighbours, a tray that carries three things to a table, and a food-runner who moves the bakery's day-end bread to a communal larder. Merged here run 85 in build order, one slice per item; the wish lines are deleted. Rules for every slice: the player can do whatever a resident does with the same pose (`town_meals.gd` is the layer for the player half, `resident_life.gd` for the resident half); a halved item is one bite smaller (meta `bites` on the item, 0.27 scale per bite — the same count the player's eating uses); nothing is ever taken out of a hand that did not offer it; the plain outcome (eat alone) stays the likely one.)
- [ ] Crumbs on the bench (slice 3): every halving leaves three ink-dot crumbs on the bench seat (`_crumbs` in `town_meals.gd`, cleared after 90s); pigeons from "Scavengers" land on a bench with crumbs when nobody sits on it and `peck` them away, and a resident about to sit on a crumbed bench brushes it first with a new `brush` pose (the flat of the hand sweeping the seat twice, 0.25s each, a 0.3s clap of the hands) — the player sitting there does the same automatically.
- [ ] The tray (slice 4): a `tray` item on the café counter (a flat wood box 0.4 × 0.3m) that holds up to three food items as children; carrying it plays a new `tray` pose (both forearms level at the waist, the elbows tucked, a slight wobble at 2Hz that grows with speed) and walking is ×0.75; set down on a bench or the picnic blanket (`C` facing it) it becomes a `share` source: each resident who sits there takes one item and eats it. The café's own resident carries one to the busiest bench at 12:00.
- [ ] The communal larder (slice 5): a small timber cupboard with two doors and three shelves on the market side of the bakery (`larder` spot); `C` with food puts it on a shelf (up to six), `C` empty-handed takes one — residents who are hungry (`mind` need < 0.35) and pass within 8m take one on 1 of 3 passes, and a resident carrying a spare plot crop puts it in. The doors swing open with the same `set_door` tween while used.
- [ ] The food-runner (slice 6; needs slice 5): at 18:00 a `runner` job carries whatever is left on the bakery counter to the larder in a basket (the `carry` pose with a basket item, one trip per two loaves), says "Day-end." to anyone in the way; the player can take the run instead by `C` on the counter after 18:00 with the basket, and residents then thank the player by name the next morning at the larder.

## Takeaway — the fifteenth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-10-01; nine fresh wishes in one day all circled **food you buy to carry away and finish somewhere else**: buying a wrapped item at a counter, finishing it seated, a warming cabinet, a bottle refill, a duck-feeder, a community table. Merged run 88 in build order, one slice per item; the wish lines are deleted. Three wishes were already in "Sharing food" and are not repeated: the tray (its slice 4), splitting (run 85's halves), offering to a neighbour (run 86's pass). Rules: a takeaway is a real carry item (`wrap` or `cup` meta, `bites` like every food); the player and residents use the same counter, poses and spots; the wrapper/cup left behind is a real item too.)
- [ ] Takeaway at the counter (slice 1): the bakery and café counters sell a wrapped pastry (paper cone around a bun, 2 coins) and a paper cup with a lid (1 coin) as carry items; `C` at the counter with coins buys one (a new `pay` pose: reach into the pocket 0.3s, hand across the counter 0.25s, take the item back 0.3s); up to three residents queue 0.9m apart on a `queue` spot line when the counter is busy (the first one served, the others shuffle forward one step), hungry minds (`mind` need < 0.4) score the counter higher, and leave holding the item; task "Buy something to take away".
- [ ] Finish it seated (slice 2): sitting with a takeaway eats it bite by bite (existing eating, 0.27 scale per bite) and leaves the empty cone or cup in the hand; a resident does the same on any free bench; the empty goes to a bin (`C` at a bin, a `toss` underhand 0.4s) or, one time in four, is left on the bench for the sweeper; a nearby resident comments once via `mind.line("litter")` when it is left.
- [ ] A warming cabinet (slice 3): a glass-fronted box on the café counter; `C` with a wrapped pastry or a cup puts it in for 3s (a lamp-glow light inside), it comes out with meta `warm` for 60s (a thin steam line above it, `water._ring`-style but upward); eating a warm item plays a `savor` pose (eyes closed, head tilt back 0.5s, slow exhale); residents warm theirs on cold or rainy hours only.
- [ ] Bottle refill (slice 4; needs the water pump or a fountain): an empty reusable `bottle` item (sold at the café, 1 coin, kept after drinking); `C` at the pump/fountain fills it (a `fill` pose: crouch, hold the bottle under the spout 1.2s, cap it), drinking takes three sips (`drink` pose) and empties it again; residents with a bottle refill when thirsty instead of buying a cup.
- [ ] Feed the ducks (slice 5): a small duck-feeder booth on the river's slow bank; `C` with a wrapped pastry crumbles it (a `toss` with three small arcs of crumbs, 0.8s), ducks within 12m swim to the bank and peck for 6s, and the empty wrapper stays in the hand for a bin; residents with leftovers feed the ducks on sunny afternoons; task "Feed the ducks".
- [ ] The community table (slice 6, last): a long table on the square with a small chalk board; `C` with a takeaway leaves it (up to five), `C` empty-handed takes one; items older than a day turn into scraps, and a `steward` job sweeps scraps into the compost bin at 19:00 (the sweeping pose plus a basket); the player can take the steward's round at the board.

## Trades on the street — the thirteenth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-30; five of six fresh wishes were one design — **a tradesperson at a small stall who does a short timed craft on request, and residents who detour there when something of theirs needs it**: a cobbler resoling shoes, a cutler at a grindstone, a tailor sewing, a launderer washing, a recoverer handing back found things. Caught on polish run 79, so nothing was built this run — the next feature run builds slice 1. Merged here in build order, one slice per item; the wish lines are deleted. The recoverer's Return Booth is the 3D half of "Lost and found" below and points there. Every slice follows the rules: the player can do the craft with `C` at the same bench with the same pose, the resident half runs on its own, the plain outcome (walk past) stays the likely one, and each pose has windup → hold → recovery. Run 81 found four of the six sitting wishes were this system again — a timber cobbler's hut = the shipped slice 1; a bookbinder's bindery stall = new slice 6 below; a Fountainworks booth that nets, tags and returns fountain finds = slice 5's Return Booth wearing a fountain, folded into that line; a groundskeeper's tool-shed with a rake-and-plant service = new slice 7, the plot's own trade. Wish lines deleted. Polish run 83 found four more fresh wishes that were this system again — a cobbler stall = the shipped slice 1 (its "hidden anti-slip / faster-recover" half dropped: a hidden buff is invisible and knock-down gets no third wrinkle); a fountain-salvager's skim booth and wring-bench = slice 5's Fountainworks net plus slice 4's `wring`, already folded; a watchmaker's winding stall = new slice 8; a busker's stage with a tuning stall = new slice 9 (the tuning stall is the trade, the busker its customer). Wish lines deleted. Run 84 found three more: a launderer at the Washyard = slice 4; a cobbler stall with 'stitch-and-hammer' = the shipped slice 1; a fountain-side Lost-and-Found attendant who fishes, tags and holds = slice 5's Return Booth with its Fountainworks net, already folded. No new slice; wish lines deleted.)
- [ ] The launderette in the Washyard (slice 4; needs "Wet and slow, dry and quick" under Weather people feel for the `wet` flag): a `tub` spot with a mangle behind the café; a resident who ends a rain walk `wet` detours there, the launderer plays `wring` (both hands twisting a cloth over the tub, 0.4s lift, four 0.3s twists with drops from the cloth, 0.4s hang on the line — `WRING_T` 2.0) and the customer's ink lightens back to dry with a line ("Dry enough."); the player `wet` at the tub gets the same; hung cloths on the line sway with `gust`.
- [ ] The Return Booth (slice 5; the 3D half of "Lost and found" below — build that line's counter as this booth rather than twice; run 81 folded a "Fountainworks" wish here: put the booth on the east plaza beside the fountain, and let the keeper also net an item thrown into the fountain disc — it lands on the counter with a `net` scoop, 0.3s dip, 0.5s lift, 0.3s shake — instead of it vanishing): a `booth` spot on the central square where a resident with a `lost` record (from "Fetch it back from the den" under Quests and relationships) is told by the booth-keeper where their thing is; the player dropping any item on the booth counter (`C` while carrying, facing it) hands it over with `grab`, the keeper plays `handover` (a two-hand present, 0.3s lift, 0.6s hold at chest height, 0.3s lower) and the owner, if within 20m, turns and walks to the booth to collect it — the first item that goes home by way of a third person.
- [ ] The bookbinder's press (slice 6; from a run 81 wish): a `press` spot at a stall with a screw press (two boards, a threaded post with a T-handle that turns while used) and a stack of three paper-coloured books; a `paper` item that has been in the river or the fountain (a `wet` flag set by `in_water` on drop, drawn as a darker `d8d2cc`) is what gets bound — a resident carrying one detours here, the binder plays `stitch` (seated, left hand holding the block, right hand pulling a needle up and across at 1.0Hz, a 0.3s reach for the thread and a 0.4s turn-the-handle recovery, `STITCH_T` 3.2) and hands it back dry and with a mauve spine ("Pressed."); player half: `C` at the press holding a wet paper sits you for the same, `C` empty-handed turns the handle for one loop.
- [ ] The groundskeeper's shed (slice 7; from a run 81 wish — the plot is the one workplace with tools and nobody who owns them): a small timber tool-shed on the meadow beside the pump (a one-door box with a rake, a hoe and a seed tin hung on the outside wall) with a `shed` spot and a `groundskeeper` job stamped on a meadow-facing door; when a `plot` row has been picked bare for over two minutes the groundskeeper walks to it with the rake (a `rake` pose: both hands on the handle, four 0.4s pulls toward the body with a 0.3s reach-out windup and a 0.3s lean-on-the-rake recovery, `RAKE_T` 2.2) and then sows it (the existing `water` arm path with a seed tin — three small ink dots dropped in the row) so the row restarts at stage 0; player half: `C` at the shed takes the rake (held like the can), `C` at a bare row rakes it with the same pose and restarts it.
- [ ] The watchmaker's winding stall (slice 8; from a polish-run-83 wish): a `watch` item (a wearable-sized ink disc with a mauve strap, `face` slot is taken by glasses so it rides the left wrist socket — add `socket_wrist` to `stick_rig.gd`) worn by one resident in eight; a watch that goes in the river or fountain with its wearer (`in_water` while worn) stops — its two hands freeze at that minute. A `winding` spot at a narrow stall on Market street (a glass-topped case with three watch discs, a loupe on a stand) with a `watchmaker` job stamped on a market door; a resident whose watch stopped detours there on 3 of 5 picks while the watchmaker works and waits in `wait`; the watchmaker plays a new `wind` pose (loupe raised to the right eye in a 0.3s windup, left hand holding the watch at the chest, right thumb and finger turning the crown in four 0.3s twists, 0.4s hold-it-to-the-ear recovery — `WIND_T` 2.3) and the hands start again ("Ticking."). Player half: wearing a stopped watch, `C` at the stall is the same wait; with no watchmaker, `C` plays `wind` on a case watch ("Don't overwind it." from a watchmaker within 12m). Plain outcome (walk past, watch stays stopped) ≥40%.
- [ ] The tuning stall and the busker (slice 9; from a polish-run-83 wish): a `tune` spot at a small stall by the fountain (a bench with a fiddle and a lute leaned on it, a tuning fork on a nail) with a `luthier` job; one resident in twelve carries a fiddle item (held, `hold` socket) and plays `fiddle` (bow arm sawing at 1.6Hz, head tilted onto the chin rest, a 0.3s raise-and-tuck windup and a 0.3s lower recovery — `FIDDLE_T` 3.0) at the fountain rim for 30–60s with an open case at their feet; every fourth session knocks the fiddle out of tune (the fiddler's `say` bubble shows a crooked `~` and nearby residents pull a face — `wince`, reused from `fight_poses.gd` only if it reads as a wince, else a new 0.6s `wince`), and the fiddler detours to the stall where the luthier plays `tune` (fiddle upright on the knee, left hand twisting a peg in three 0.3s turns, right finger plucking once per turn, `TUNE_T` 1.5). Player half: carrying the fiddle, `C` at the fountain rim plays; at the stall, the luthier tunes it. Coins in the case wait for "Coins in play" — until then residents drop nothing.

## Weather people feel — the twelfth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-29; four of five fresh wishes were one design — **a person's body answering the weather**: umbrellas and hoods when rain starts, standing under an eave to watch it, drifting into shade and fanning in midday heat, a shared umbrella rack — as opposed to "Weather in `game/`" below, which is about things (the fox, the river, the plot) answering it. Merged here run 73, one slice per item, in build order; the wish lines deleted. Rules that bind every slice: the pose rule (each reaction is its own `pose_request` with windup/hold/recovery in `stick3d_poses.gd` and the same name in `stickman.ts`); the reverse rule (whatever the player can do in the rain, residents do on their own routine at the same spots); the holiday rule (rain never blocks `C`, never takes an item out of a hand, and walking in the rain stays possible — shelter is what residents *prefer*, not a wall); no new synced state (the room only relays poses). Not built this run — run 73 was structurally due and built the terrace; the next feature run picks slice 1. Polish run 75 (2026-09-29) found three more fresh wishes that were this system again — a loaner-umbrella booth, a misting bench on hot days, puddles that trip a dasher — and folded them into slices 2, 5 and a new slice 7 below; the wish lines are deleted. Run 76 found one more (a loaner-umbrella rack/post/bench spot with a wet-weather idle and a brief extra shove-resilience) — the rack itself, built this run; the "shove-resilience" half is dropped by the holiday rule (being hit is a core action and gets no third wrinkle) and the wish line is deleted. Run 77 found three more (a covered shelter booth/awning spot with a "wait-sheltered" action = slice 1's eave plus slice 6's awnings; a misting kiosk in the park = slice 5's misting-bench branch; a community umbrella-rack booth with a "covered" idle that blocks = the shipped slice 2, its "blocks" half dropped by the holiday rule) — all folded, no new slice, the wish lines deleted. Run 78 found three more: a public umbrella-rack booth that "shortens shove distance" = the shipped slice 2 again (the shove half dropped, holiday rule); a deployable awning residents and the player pull down when rain starts = slice 6 (its "shelters held items / shortens shove distance" halves dropped — weather never touches what is in a hand); storm shutters shopkeepers close at sunset or in heavy rain = a new slice 8 below (its "keeps indoor props safe from snatching" half dropped — nothing may block taking). Wish lines deleted.)
- [ ] Wet and slow, dry and quick (slice 3): once umbrellas and eaves exist, being out in `rain` without an open umbrella or an eave sets `wet = true` on player and resident (the ink colour of the figure darkens 12%, a drip particle from the hat socket every 0.8s), walking speed ×0.85 for the player *and* residents (same rule), and the first bench you sit on afterwards says "Wet." — dries after 20s out of the rain. Never blocks anything; a wet resident who greets you adds "Dreadful." to their line.
- [ ] Hoods when there is no umbrella (slice 4): the `scarf` wearable and a new `hoodie` back-socket wearable get a `hood` state (a half-sphere on the hat socket, scaled up in 0.25s when rain starts and back down when it stops — the `umbr` windup shape, no arm); residents wearing one skip the rack and shelter less (they keep walking, 50%). Player: `C` with nothing in reach while wearing one flips it (the same slot the hat take-off uses today: only when no hat is worn, so hat take-off keeps working).
- [ ] Midday heat (slice 5; the first new weather state since rain): `weather == "hot"` (clear + the sun's top third of the day, 1 in 3 days by seed) — residents stop running (`RUN` unused, walk ×0.9), a `shadow` spot appears under each tree at the crown's sun-side (registered by `_tree`, only while hot) that they weight ×3, and a resident standing there or on a bench in the heat plays `fan` (the 2D `fan` exists: a paper or the hand flapping at the chin; the 3D one is new — wrist flick from the elbow, 3Hz, the other hand on the hip, a pause every ~4s to wipe the brow); `C` at the garden pump in heat splashes the face (`splash` pose: both hands cupped up to the face, a shake of the head, drops from the hat socket for 2s) — residents on their plot routine do it after watering. The fox lies in its den mouth in heat too (same branch as rain, Weather in `game/`). (Town wish 2026-09-29, folded here run 75: a misting bench in the park that runs on hot days — becomes this slice's bench branch: the park's east bench gets a `mist` pipe (a thin box along the backrest + a `CPUParticles3D` spray emitting only while hot), a resident on it plays `fan` at half the rate and says "Better.", the player sat there gets the same spray; the wish's "slow nearby chases" half is dropped — a chase is a core action and gets no third wrinkle.)
- [ ] Awning paths in rain (slice 6): the market's stalls and the bakery/café fronts get an `awning` strip (a thin box 2.2m up, already drawn as counters' roofs?) — residents walking in rain between two spots that both lie along the market front route through the awning line (`crossings()` adds waypoints 0.5m under each awning in x order when `weather == "rain"` and both ends are within the market's x range); visible as a shuffle of umbrella-less residents along the shop fronts rather than across the plaza.
- [ ] Slick puddles (slice 7; needs the three meadow-path puddles that "The river rises in rain" under Weather in `game/` leaves behind): *dashing* (`dash_until`, the double-tap) across a puddle plays a new `slip` pose — 0.15s windup (both arms fly up, the back foot shoots forward), 0.4s hold (a low half-squat catch with one hand near the ground), 0.3s recovery (straighten, a shake of the hand) in `stick3d_poses.gd` + `stickman.ts`; the 2D `trip` is a fall and means something else — and ends the dash there. Walking across is safe and nothing ever leaves the hand: the wish's "held item slips free" half is dropped by the holiday rule (plain outcome = you cross, 100% when walking). Residents only hurry in rain (`WALK` ×1.7 in `resident.gd`), so a hurrying resident who crosses a puddle slips the same way, same catch, and says "Careful." to nobody; puddles dry one by one after rain so the slip is a two-minute thing, not a permanent trap. (Town wish 2026-09-29, folded here run 75.)
- [ ] Shutters at dusk and in a downpour (slice 8; from run 78's wish): the bakery and café front windows get a `shutter` pair (the `_window` shutters already drawn on half the houses, hinged as two thin boxes) that the shop's own resident (`job == "baker"`, and the café's once the Café loop names one) closes with a new `shut` pose at sunset and when `weather == "rain"` for more than 20s — a 0.3s reach up, a 0.5s pull with both hands, a 0.3s step back — and opens again at 06:00 or when the rain stops; the player can do the same with `C` at a shutter (same pose, both directions), and a closed shutter changes nothing else: the counter still serves, the door still opens, nothing is protected — it is what a shopkeeper does in the evening, not a lock.

## Scavengers — the eleventh system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-28; twelve wishes written into four different sections on one day — a fox, two stray cats, two pigeon flocks, a magpie, a stray dog, a raccoon, a squirrel, a hedgehog, an otter, a gaggle of geese — and every one of them was the same design: **an animal that reacts to a knocked-down (or seated, or distracted) person** — takes the small thing they dropped to a stash spot, or sits on/beside them, and can be lured off with food. Not twelve animals; one system with twelve costumes. Merged here in build order (found 2026-09-28, run 69), one slice per item, the wish lines deleted from Game / 3D town / North lane / Wishes. Rules that bind every slice: the Animal standard (a new animal ships on a rig with every minimum motion or not at all — so the existing fox, dog and cat go first and new animals wait for their rig items under "Animals"); the holiday rule (the plain outcome — you pick the dropped thing up — stays the most likely: an animal only acts on what nobody has taken for a beat, never blocks `C`, never stuns; the "stun nearby humans" and "delay pick-up" halves of the wishes are dropped for that reason); every stash is a visible place you can walk to; and getting it back is a quest, not a mystery.)
- [ ] The dog sits with the shoved (second — existing dog rig): when a resident goes `down` within 6m of the dog, it trots over, sits (`sit`) 0.8m from them facing whoever hit them, and barks (a new quad act `bark`: head jerks forward twice, ears up, body hops 2cm — its own timing) once every ~2s until the resident is up; it does nothing to the human but look. A resident who was sat-with says "Good dog." on getting up. Also from the wish: the dog trots after anyone carrying `bread` until they `C`-feed it (the "Give" verb from Items, dog flavour).
- [ ] The cat curls on the fallen (third — existing cat rig): a cat within 5m walks to a `down` resident and lies (`lie`) against their side for the rest of their down time plus 2s, purring (a tiny body scale pulse); it does not slow pick-up or recovery (holiday rule). The cat also bats one small item (`cup`, `paper`) lying within 1m of where it lies 0.4m further away with a paw swipe (new quad act `bat`) — a nudge, not a theft. Coaxed off with `C` (pet) as today.
- [ ] Birds take the shiny things (fourth — blocked on Animals / "Bird rig"): pigeons/magpie/crow on `bird3d.gd` land beside a fresh drop that is small (`cup`, `paper`, a wearable), peck at it twice, then fly it to a roost spot (the lane's middle roof, the fountain rim) and drop it there — roofs you can reach by the stair-house. Breadcrumbs (a `bread` bite dropped with `C`) lure them down first.
- [ ] The squirrel stashes food (fifth — blocked on Animals / "Squirrel with the quad rig"): only edible drops (`apple`, `bread`), carried up the trunk into a hollow; shake the tree (`shake`) to get it back — the first time shaking a tree gives back something that was yours.
- [ ] The raccoon and the bins (sixth — new animal, quad rig, all minimum motions): lives in an alley den behind the market bins, raids an open bin at night (needs the café/bakery bin loop from Items first: things you binned come back out), tugs a small drop from a fallen resident's side; lured with any food.
- [ ] Geese (seventh — bird rig + the goose's own moves: honk, neck-out, wing-spread): a gaggle of three at the meadow bank forms a loose ring around a fallen resident and honks at the human who did it; they take *worn* things (a hat knocked off — see "Hats fall off" below) and parade them to the water; bread lures them.
- [ ] The otter at the river (eighth — new animal, its own rig: swim, dive, surface, roll): fetches things drifting in the river ("Things fall in the river" under Grows from River and bridge) and leaves them on the bank spot — a helper, the one scavenger who gives back; nudges a fallen resident's shoulder once (a `nudge` act) instead of taking anything.
- [ ] The hedgehog (ninth — the odd one out, folded here because it is still an animal keyed to how still a person is): snuffles the garden edges at dusk, curls into a ball (`curl`, own rig) when anyone runs within 1m — a sprinter who runs into the ball trips (the web `trip` pose ported) — and uncurls to follow a person who has stood or sat still for 5s.
- [ ] Getting it back is a quest (tenth, the close — see "Fetch it back from the den" under Quests and relationships in `game/`): the robbed resident remembers what was taken and where, asks you for it, and thanks you by name when you bring it.
(Run 70 triage: four more wishes landed in "Wishes from the town" the same day, three of them this system again in new coats — a raccoon at the bins at dusk that snatches drops unless you "offer a snack" (= the sixth slice, raccoon; the lure is already the apple rule from the fox), a squirrel that snatches and dashes up a tree with a "follow" button (= the fifth slice; the "shake the tree to get it back" is already there, no chase button — the holiday rule says the plain outcome stays picking it up yourself), and a crow at dawn/dusk taking shiny things to roosts, bribed with "offer shiny" (= the fourth slice, birds; the crow joins the pigeon/magpie list, and the bribe is the same lure verb). Folded, no new lines. The fourth — a stray cat that nudges slow residents for petting and hisses and bolts when a human sprints past — is not a scavenger; it is the still-open "Cat life" line under Animals almost word for word (pet only if you approach slowly, arch and hiss if you run at it), folded there.)
(Run 72 triage: three more wishes on 2026-09-28, two of them this system again — a river fox that "nabs trinkets from slow walkers" and drops them for tossed food (= the shipped first slice plus the apple lure; nothing new), a nocturnal raccoon at the bins bribed with a timed "offer food" action (= the sixth slice; the lure stays the food-on-the-ground rule, no timed button — holiday rule). The third, a park goat that butts sprinters flat and trails food-carriers, is the dog's bread-trailing rule (second slice) plus a new animal, so it went to Animals / "Goat" with the butt softened to a stumble. Wish lines deleted.)

## Skill buildings — the tenth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-27; nine of twelve fresh wishes merged into one build order: each pairs a themed civic building (or, for two of them, a piece of a still-missing riverside place) with a named instructor/keeper job, where a short `C` session performs or teaches a genuinely new locomotion `FigPose` the town didn't have yet — motions-axis growth arriving with its own building each time, rather than another wrinkle on something that already works. The other three of the twelve — a dojo/Sensei's forward-roll, a physiotherapy clinic's limp-gait rehab, and a bouldering gym's "recover from high knocks" — are the same shape as the still-open bathhouse/orthotics-workshop slices under "Grows from A clinic on Market street" below (a service building + job that shortens recovery from being hurt) and are folded there instead, not merged here; see that section's own updated note. Built in this order, cheapest (no new place) first:)
- [ ] A municipal running track (third — the wish names no map either; a painted lane on the street or a park verge, terrain rather than a building, no new place needed): an `athletic coach` job; `C` at the line sprints a short seeded distance (new `sprint` `FigPose`, high knee-drive) then plays a `pant` `FigPose` (bent over, hands on knees) on finish.
- [ ] A communal cloakroom (fourth — ties to temperature/wetness state Weather hasn't shipped yet; scope the trigger down to "just came out of the water" until then): a `cloakroom` building, a `cloakkeeper` job; a resident or player recently dunked can `C` there to borrow a cloak (new `ItemKey`) and stop a new `shiver` idle from playing — closes the motion library's still-open `shiver` line.
- [ ] A yoga terrace in the park (fifth — the same mechanic as the yoga studio above at a second location, the Lamps-on-a-clock/House-windows-glow chain's own "same trick, new prop" shape): a park `PropKind`, no building; `C` performs the same held stretch the studio uses.
- [ ] An open-air public lido (sixth — ties into the still-open "A lifeguard job" thin-axis item above rather than inventing a second lifeguard job; build the two together): a `lido` place (a pool prop) with `lifeguard` as its job; `C` at the poolside performs a two-arm dive (new `dive` `FigPose`) then a slow stroke while swimming.
- [ ] A roller rink pavilion (seventh): a `rink` building, a `skatekeeper` job; `C` there laces on skates and glides a loop (new `skate` `FigPose`, a rhythmic side-to-side kick).
- [ ] A riverfront boathouse (eighth — no longer blocked in `game/`: the river exists since run 68, build it on the meadow bank at `TownPlaces.RIVER_S`; see the new places-axis item below, which this and the next item both need): a `boathouse` on the still-missing riverside map, a `boatman` job; `C` at the dock rows a two-seater skiff (new `row` `FigPose`).
- [ ] A dance pavilion along the river path (ninth — blocked on the same river path, and on a new `phonograph` prop the wish also names): a second spot for the `dancer` job once the path exists; `C` there joins a synchronized four-step group dance instead of the solo practice above.
- [ ] A Kneeling Terrace (tenth — merged 2026-09-28 from a fresh town wish, 2026-09-27 second batch, that named no map; sits on Market street or the square like the yoga studio, no new place needed): a `kneelterrace` building spot, a `caretaker` job; `C` nearby holds a kneeling rest for a few seconds. Needs its own new `FigPose` (`kneel`, both knees down, hands resting on thighs) — distinct from `sit`/`crouch`, neither of which reads as kneeling.
- [ ] A Trampoline Pavilion, the Spring Loft (eleventh, last — town wish, 2026-09-27, found still sitting untriaged in "Wishes from the town" this run; a park `PropKind`, no building needed, same no-new-place shape as the running track and yoga terrace above): a `jump-instructor` job; `C` at the pavilion plays a new `bounce` `FigPose` (a springier, higher, bent-knee-recoil jump distinct from the ordinary `jump` — the wish's own ask, "hop to reach higher") held for a couple of reps before landing.

## Grows from Community dance hall
- [ ] A partnered two-step (thin axis: motions, 2026-09-27 — chain from today's dance hall, the wish's own "partnered hold," deferred there): when two residents (or a resident and a player) are both `C`-dancing at the same `dancehall` spot within the same few seconds, pair them — a second `dance` variant (or a shared parameter on the one pose) faces the two figures toward each other with arms linked in a frame instead of each dancing solo, moving in sync off the same clock the solo sway already uses. Closes the "`dance` (two variants)" line in the motion library's "Play and rest" list below.

## Grows from A yoga studio
- [ ] A synchronized breathing session (chain, 2026-09-28 — folded here from a near-duplicate "Sun Room" town wish found in "Wishes from the town" this run rather than built as a second yoga building next door to the one that just shipped): when two or more residents are `C`-using `yogastudio1` in the same few-second window, sync their held stretch to one shared breathing clock (a slow inhale/exhale scale on the existing `yoga` pose, everyone moving together) instead of each on their own independent `_t`-driven sway — the wish's own "synchronized deep-breath motion" without a second building or a second `FigPose`.

## Passing words — the ninth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-26, a second wave arriving after the Word-relay triage below; three wishes — a one-word greeting toward whatever's nearest when two residents cross within 20px, a paired two-line remark when two pass going opposite directions, a dry greeting/murmured reply when a walker passes near someone seated — merged into one build order, one slice per item. A different shape from Word relay below: no token travels or degrades, no board/lamp/bench prop involved. A different shape from "Grows from Affinity"'s passing-glance family too: no `pairsFancying` gate — *any* two `routine` residents qualify, so a fixed low seeded chance (same hash+bucket trick `wave` hello already uses) keeps it rare instead of `affinityOf` doing that job. Not built this run — 2026-09-26 polish run, ships no feature; the next feature run picks the first slice.)
- [ ] A one-word greeting toward the nearest thing (wider — needs a new fixed table before building): when two `routine` residents pass within ~20px (same proximity+seeded-bucket gate as `wave`, a separate pool/threshold so the two don't double-fire on the same pass), each says one word drawn from a new small `PropKind → word` table keyed by whichever prop is nearest to *them* individually right then (lamp → "light.", bench → "seat.", fountain → "water." …); table needs designing first. Scope to "nearest object" only, per the wish's own note — no weather half until Weather exists.
- [ ] A paired remark when paths cross head-on (hardest — needs a design call before building, not just a table): when two `routine` residents moving in opposite x-directions pass within the same gate as the two above, show a two-line exchange (a call from one pool, a reply from another). The wish asks them to "pause for one second," but `routine()` computes every resident's position as a pure function of the room clock — actually halting one for real would desync it from what every other client computes for that same resident a moment later. Scope the "pause" to a cosmetic beat (freeze the *rendered* pose only, the same trick `wave` already uses without touching `moving` or position) rather than a real stop, or drop the pause and just show both lines as they continue past each other.
(Two of the second wave don't get their own line: 'a replay booth' — a resident records one short line, played once to the next passer, then clears — is the same talkbox-booth idea run 50 already folded as 'Record and Play' under Word relay below; 'pin a one-line whisper to any bench, board, or the fountain, plays once to the next passer and clears' restates Leave-a-murmur/Echo-board's own pin-then-play-once mechanic in plain terms. Both folded here with no new line.)

## Grows from A greeting near someone seated
- [ ] A player passing a seated resident gets the greeting too (chain from today's item, wider): today's check only fires between two `routine`-mode residents (a moving one, a seated one) — extend the walker half to the player too (same distance/`act === 'sit'` check, just read against `b.x`/`b.d`/`b.moving` instead of a second npc) so a seated resident murmurs a reply when a human walks past, not only when another resident does; the seated resident's own line already works for anyone in range, only the trigger side needs widening.

## Word relay — the eighth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-26; five wishes about a short phrase or single word passed hand to hand between residents, degrading a little on each hop — a swapped word, a muffled vowel or consonant — a different shape from Resident-run-rounds' fixed-cast scripted game below and from Turn-based games' two-person score: here the "game" is the message itself changing as it moves, not a score or a race. One of the five (a rolling one-word-per-turn sentence at the chess table) turned out to be the same shape as the still-open "A word-chain sentence board" item under Resident-run rounds below — no second line for it, see the note there. The remaining four are a genuinely new shape, merged here, one slice per item, in build order. None of these can hold a real synced token either — the room only relays the fixed `ev` kinds the note under Grows from Bins lists — so, same as every clock-seeded family already in this backlog, the actual phrase-at-each-hop has to be a pure function of (seed, spot, bucket), and any human part is cosmetic against it, not a mutation. A sixth wish, 'Record and Play' [town wish, 2026-09-26, found still sitting untriaged in "Wishes from the town" during the 2026-09-26 polish run — a talkbox booth records a one-word clip that nearby residents may play back with one vowel muffled or swapped], is the same degrading-token shape as Leave-a-murmur/Echo-board below wearing a booth costume — folded in here with no seventh line.)
- [ ] An addressed note (town wish, 2026-09-26, third wave, merged here — genuinely new shape: targeted rather than proximity-based): a resident tags their most recent line to a specific resident handle (reuse the board/crate's `Loose.note`+`boardOrigin` pattern — a pinned note with a named recipient instead of "no name on it"); the *named* resident's own routine, not just anyone passing, reclaims it the next time they are near the spot, the same reclaim check the board/shelf items already run keyed by owner instead of by proximity to a stranger. No new `ev` kind, no free text (the line comes from the same fixed `MURMUR_LINES`/`REPLY_CALLS`-shaped pools every other slice in this family draws from).
- [ ] A Listening Post on the street (town wish, 2026-09-26, merged here — the most involved of the five, a relay along several spots rather than one board): a single-word whisper token would pass along a short chain of adjacent spots, muffling one letter per hop, so the word at the far end rarely matches the word that started — needs the chain itself designed (which spots count as "adjacent", how many hops) before building; note here rather than guess at scope.
(Two earlier wishes, 2026-09-26 — 'harmonize', appending a word instead of muffling one, and 'intonation mark', a tone tag that travels with the line — describe a mutation *of the line's content* rather than just who holds it next; neither has anything to attach to until Echo Board/reply board/Listening Post/the addressed note above actually lands a real line-carrying prop, so noted here rather than given their own slice. A third wave (2026-09-26, thirteen more wishes, triaged this run): 'call-and-response' (prize stage) is today's reply-board shape wearing different dressing, folded, no new line; 'murmur tap' restates Leave-a-murmur's own forward-or-anchor choice, folded; 'listening posts' duplicates the still-open Listening Post line above outright, folded; a second 'harmonize' and a second 'intonation mark' repeat the two content-mutation wishes already parked in this note, folded; 'trail ping' is the reply-board/Listening-post shape again with path dressing instead of a board or a chain, folded; 'mumble chain' is a word-swap variant of the same muffle-per-hop shape Listening Post/the second murmur hop already cover, folded; 'public echo' is the already-shipped Echo Board's own pin-then-replay-to-passers shape, folded. Four of the thirteen were genuinely new but don't fit the pure-clock trick this whole family relies on and need their own design pass before a build-order line, same carve-out as the word-chain board's 'veto' problem (Resident-run rounds, above): 'whisper-back' asks for a reply *invisible to everyone but one resident*, which no client-rendered world can actually hide (every screen draws the same head-bubble); 'loop tile' wants an overdubbable multi-layer recording several residents edit over time, more synced state than a fixed `ev` kind or a seed+bucket function can hold; 'reply coin' wants a token that forces whoever picks it up to "speak the line verbatim next," which needs free text on a world item (`ItemKey` is a fixed enum, no payload) and a way to constrain what a human says, neither of which exists; 'question prompt' wants a player-facing UI of three tappable one-word suggestion buttons, a new interaction surface none of this family's flat-line/slate mechanics have needed so far. One of the thirteen ('an addressed note') was new and buildable — see the build-order line above. A fourteenth wish, unrelated to this whole family (residents exchanging an ambient one-word greeting referencing whatever's nearby, not a passed-and-mutated line), stayed in "Wishes from the town" below rather than being folded in here.)

## Grows from Murmur
- [ ] A murmur that travels a second hop (chain from Leave a murmur, today's item, wider — the seed of the still-open Echo Board/Listening Post slices above): today's echo is one hop only (pinner → one echoer, same bucket); let a resident who just echoed a murmur become, for the rest of that bucket, a fresh "pinner" of the already-muffled line at their own position — a *different* third resident within `MURMUR_ECHO_R` gets the same `MURMUR_ECHO_PCT` roll to echo it again, muffling a second word (`muffle()` called twice, tracking which index is already "—"). The first two-hop version of the degrading-token idea the rest of Word relay's build order still wants, and it needs no new prop — just a second pass of the same check.

- [ ] A passerby comments on the call (chain from Reply board, today's item): the same passerby-comment shape Checkers/Pebble-toss/Bocce/Light-chain-board/Echo board already got (`routine`-mode resident within ~150px, small seeded chance right when the `REPLY_WINDOW` bucket turns over, a flat line — "someone's shouting again." / "no one ever answers that one.") — the sixth clock-seeded-slate spot to want it, no new state, same reasoning as the other five.

## Resident-run rounds — the seventh system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-25; nine wishes shaped as a tappable 'Start X' action beginning a fixed 60-90s resident-run round with a human able to join as a player or a judge — five turned out to be the already-shipped or already-open Turn-based-games slices wearing new names ('Join Game' names nothing the section's own clock-seeded slates don't already do; 'Ring the Bell' is the still-open stage-buzzer quiz board with 'judge' for 'buzz'; 'Fountain Flip' is the already-shipped Pebble-toss; 'Start Blitz' is the already-shipped Checkers; 'Start Charade' is the still-open Quick-charades slice), folded away with no new build-order line; the remaining four are a genuinely different shape from Turn-based games' single-spot two-person seeded score — a scripted round spanning several residents (and, for two of them, a prop that doesn't exist yet) that a human can tap into as a player or a judge — merged here, one slice per item, in build order)
- [ ] A parcel relay at the post office: a human starts it, then a fixed small cast of residents pass a visible parcel token post office → relay locker → finish pennant on a timed lap — needs two new props (a relay locker, a finish pennant) this run didn't build. The lap "time" should be the same seed+bucket trick every other slice in this family uses; a human's own leg of the relay is cosmetic against it, same as a tap-in elsewhere here.
- [ ] A word-chain sentence board: residents each add one seeded word in turn to a running sentence at a new board prop, and a human can tap to "veto" a word. The veto is the one genuinely new wrinkle in this whole family — every other slice's human input is purely cosmetic against the clock, but a veto that actually changes the sentence would be a real mutation, and the room only relays a fixed set of `ev` kinds (see the note under Grows from Bins) with no synced tally to hold one. Either the "veto" has to be judged the same clock-seeded way as everything else here (cosmetic, not a real veto) or this needs its own small design pass before building — note here rather than guess. A second town wish (2026-09-26, "Start Chainline") asked for the same one-word-per-turn shape again at the chess table — no new information, folded here rather than into a second line.
- [ ] A toy-duck race at the pond: a new duck-launch kiosk prop; residents "sign" lanes and each gets one seeded drift down the pond, a human can tap in as starter or judge — same shape as the rest of this family (seed+bucket decides the winner, the tap-in is cosmetic), needs the new kiosk prop this run didn't build.
- [ ] A paper-boat regatta at the fountain (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here — same scripted-round-spanning-several-residents shape as the rest of this section): residents fold, launch and nudge paper boats across the fountain on a timed lap, the family's usual seed+bucket trick deciding it; a human's "Join as Cox" is cosmetic against that clock. Needs no new prop — the fountain already exists — so it's the least blocked of today's four new adds.
- [ ] A hide-and-seek at the toss kiosk (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here): residents each hide three pebble tokens around the square/street/park on a seeded schedule and a human can tap in to search; needs a new kiosk prop — likely the same square-kiosk the still-open Ring-toss item (Turn-based games, below) already wants, so build one kiosk and let both slices share it rather than raising two.
- [ ] Hot potato at a hot-sack booth (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here): a warm-sack token passes between a small cast of residents on the family's usual clock, a bucket stops it and the holder is "out" for the rest of the round; needs a new hot-sack booth prop this run didn't build.
- [ ] Tag at a park tag post (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here): one resident starts as "It" and hands it off along the family's usual clock; a human can tap in as a Runner (cosmetic against that clock, same as every other slice here) or just watch as a Spectator; needs a new tag-post prop in the park this run didn't build.

## Turn-based games — the sixth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-25; four wishes — checkers at the park chess table, a fountain pebble-toss, bocce at a park court, ring-toss at an alley booth — all the same shape: two residents take turns on a short timed move at a themed spot and log a score on a slate/scoreboard, with a human able to tap in and take a turn — merged into one build order, one slice per item. None of these need a synced score: the room only relays a fixed set of `ev` kinds (see the note under Grows from Bins) and adding one is out of scope, so the turn/score sequence has to be a pure function of (day, table key, room clock) the same way passing-glance/wave/affinity-pairs already are — every screen computes the identical score without new state. A human's tap-in is a real, visible action (sent in `pos.pose` like leaning/shaking already are) but is cosmetic against that shared clock, not a mutation of it — the deterministic score keeps advancing whether or not a human ever taps in.)
- [ ] Ring-toss at an alley or square-kiosk booth (third — blocked on `marketalley` for the alley variant, the map "The found stall in the alley" under Lost and found already names and which hasn't landed yet; the square-kiosk variant needs no new place and could land first if the alley keeps stalling; a third town wish, 2026-09-25, asked for the exact same kiosk-and-match-board shape again — no new information, still the same fold): once either spot exists, the booth/kiosk is the same seeded-turn/score shape as the rest of this section.
- [ ] A stage-buzzer quiz board (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here — same joinable/timed/board-logged shape as the rest of this section, but the "turn" is answering a posted question against a clock instead of moving a piece): reuses the park prize stage (`stage1`, already drawn) rather than a new prop; a resident's question text is just a seeded pick from a small fixed line pool (no LLM call, no free text), the buzzer press is `C` within a short seeded window per round the same way a bucket flip already gates chess/pebble-toss, and "right" vs "wrong" is itself decided by the same clock-seeded roll — a human's press is cosmetic against it, same as every other slice here.
- [ ] Paper-distance contests: a paper-plane launch line and a paper-boat launch post (town wishes, 2026-09-25, merged as one slice — same shape, air vs water dressing only): borrow a kit at the line/post, `X` within a short window launches (reuse `throw`, lower arc for the boat), the "distance" is the same seed+bucket score trick as pebble-toss dressed as a number instead of two names, posted to a nearby scoreboard prop. Build the plane (square or street, no new place) before the boat (needs a launch post at the park duck pond, `pond2`).
- [ ] Hopscotch on a painted lane (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here — turn-based like Checkers, just standing instead of seated): a painted hop lane on the street (no new place, drawn on the ground like the church steps' rectangles), residents and a tapped-in human alternate short seeded turns, `X` for the hop (reuse an existing motion — check whether `jump` reads fine before considering a new `FigPose`), score on a nearby slate the same seed+bucket way.
- [ ] Timed memory-match on a flip-slab (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here — closest in shape to Checkers of anything in this batch, two-player turn-based with a board): a public flip-slab on the street; `C` to flip, alternating seeded turns, matches tallied the same seed+bucket way onto a small board. No new pose (reuse `chess`'s seated reach, or `stand` if the slab is upright).
- [ ] A scavenger dash with a sprint board (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here as the odd one out — every other slice in this section is one human at one themed spot; this one sends the player across all three outdoor maps in 45 seconds to tag three "hidden quick-posts", which is a different shape (a timed multi-map fetch, not a turn at a prop) wearing this section's clothing): scope down before building — "hidden quick-posts" needs real spots to tag (reuse existing named props like `lamp1`/`tree1`/`bench4` rather than inventing floating markers) and "fouls" needs a definition that isn't a new `ev` kind; note here rather than build until that's worked out, since it doesn't fit the pure-clock trick the rest of the section relies on.
- [ ] Quick-charades at the prize stage (town wish, 2026-09-25, merged here — last in build order, a genuinely different shape from the rest of the section, not a duplicate): a human borrows a prompt card and prop at a square kiosk, gets a 60s turn on the park prize stage (`stage1`, same spot Busking and the still-open stage-buzzer quiz item above reuse), and residents/other humans "vote" by tapping a nearby clap post instead of the turn itself producing a score. The vote count can't be a real synced tally (the room only relays the fixed `ev` kinds the note under Grows from Bins already lists, and a live multi-tapper counter isn't one of them) — follow this section's own established trick instead: the clap post's "score" is the same pure `(seed, floor(t/10))` bucket function pebble-toss/chess use, and any human's own clap (`C` at the post, sent in `pos.pose` like a tap-in elsewhere in this section) is cosmetic against it, not a mutation — same "the deterministic result keeps advancing whether or not anyone taps in" rule the section's own intro states.

## Keep-lock — the fifth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-24; seven wishes — a mend counter at a Change Room, a drying rack, a bin-lock, a handheld mend kit, a seed-bundle, a rinse trough, a sort-shelf — all asking for the same close: a resident finishes a short timed service on an item, tags it, and it stays briefly un-grabbable right after, distinct from "Service drop-offs" below which only covers the drop-and-service half and never says what happens to the item once it's done — merged into one build order, one slice per item)
- [ ] Rinse trough / drying rack at the fountain (merges two of the seven wishes with the still-unbuilt "Rinse-and-hang at the fountain" chain item under Service drop-offs below — same spot, same action, a drying rack is just the tail end of a rinse): once that chain item lands, route its finish through the keep-lock above instead of leaving the freshly-rinsed item grabbable on the spot.
- [ ] Sort-shelf tagging (extends the shipped Sort the scraps, `SquareGame.tsx`): draw the sweeper's trash/reuse/repair choice as a small label over a `sort`-flagged item while it waits, and route the sweeper's pickup through the keep-lock above instead of a silent vanish.
- [ ] Handheld mend kit (merges the "quick-mend at the Change Room counter" wish with this one — same ask, "anywhere" vs. a named counter — no Change Room prop exists anywhere in `world.ts`, so fold both into the shipped board-based Mend request rather than inventing a location): a carried `mend kit` item (new `ItemKey`) lets a *resident* fix a nearby broken prop or board-pinned item on the spot instead of only ever a repairer's opportunistic roll — same `fix` pose, same keep-lock tail once done.
- [ ] Seed-bundle at the spare-seed plot: same blocker as the existing "A seed deposit at the garden" chain item under Service drop-offs below (needs a growth/crop state that doesn't exist yet) — don't build twice, note here until that lands, then give its finish the keep-lock tail too.
- [ ] Bench cubby stash (town wish, 2026-09-24, merged here — a distinct shape from the rest of this section, closer to Bin-lock than to a mend/rinse/clean finish): a resident stows a small item under any bench (new ~20s `stow` action, no new prop) creating a `stashed` token that keeps that specific spot un-grabbable for the duration, the bench-side sibling to Bin-lock's per-prop timer above.
- [ ] Sweep bundle at the broom rack (town wish, 2026-09-24, merged here — extends Sort-shelf tagging above rather than duplicating it): a resident sweeps loose small items within ~200px into one bundled loose item (12s), which then goes through the ordinary sort/bin path above instead of vanishing item-by-item — the bundling is new, the keep-lock tail is the same one Sort-shelf tagging already wants.
- [ ] Pegboard hang-tool lock (town wish, 2026-09-24, merged here — blocked on a location: no pegboard/maintenance-yard prop exists anywhere in `world.ts`): once a maintenance yard exists (see the new places item below), a resident hangs a tool on its pegboard (~20s) and the tool is locked against humans for 60s — same shape as Bin-lock, just a new prop to hang from instead of a bin to empty. Note here until the yard lands. A later wish (2026-09-24) asked for the same lock on a pushable maintenance cart instead of a fixed pegboard — same blocker, build as one item once the yard exists rather than two.
- [ ] Watering gets a keep-lock tail (town wish, 2026-09-24, merged here — the first Keep-lock slice on an already-shipped core action rather than a Service-drop-off): after the existing `water` action finishes on a garden or sapling, tag it `watered` and route it through the keep-lock above for ~60s, the same way Mend's finish already does — no new prop, no new pose, just wiring the existing watering interaction's finish through `lockAt`.
- [ ] A washyard basin for prop upkeep (town wish, 2026-09-24, merged here — blocked on a location: no washyard/basin prop exists anywhere in `world.ts`, same shape of blocker as Pegboard above): a resident dips a rag and wipes down a scuffed bench or signpost (reuse the existing `BREAKABLE`/`broken` prop-damage state as the "scuffed" condition rather than inventing a new one), then the prop itself locks against human tampering for 60s — the prop-lock shape Bin-lock established, applied to street furniture instead of a bin. Note here until a washyard spot lands. A later wish (2026-09-24) asked for the same rag-scrub-and-lock at a park "hose-post" instead of the washyard — same blocker, same shape, build as one item once either spot lands rather than two.

## Coins in play — the third system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-23 to 2026-09-24; eight wishes about earning and spending a coin, scattered across "Residents grow too" and "Wishes from the town", merged into one build order the same way Lost-and-found and Keeping-it were — the town wrote three more of the same shape after the first merge (polish caught it, 2026-09-24), then five more after that, all still the same shape (this run caught it, 2026-09-23)

A real wallet-spend needs a new `/api/pond`-shaped endpoint (out of scope here); do not fake it with a client-only decrement of the `coins` state, since that would drift from the server total on reload — a half interaction, against the rules. Skip until a spend endpoint exists.

The "human pays a coin" half needs the same missing wallet-spend endpoint as Buy a hot drink above — skip until that lands. Needs both the missing wallet-spend endpoint (human side) and resident wallets (see "Resident wallets and savings", unbuilt) to update multiple residents' counts — the most blocked of the three merged wishes, so it is last in this build order.

- [ ] A fountain tip-slot for a named resident (town wish, 2026-09-23, merged here from "Wishes from the town" 2026-09-24 — **blocked on the same spend gap**): a human drops one coin at the fountain targeted at a specific resident; only that resident may collect it. Needs the missing spend endpoint to hold the human's coin against the pin, and the new prop besides — more blocked than the three single-resident wishes above.
