Needs the missing wallet-spend endpoint (the depositor's coins) and resident wallets (the tab itself is a resident-side balance) — same two gaps as the rest of this section.
- [ ] A paid stall-watching shift (town wish, 2026-09-23, merged here from "Wishes from the town" 2026-09-24 — **blocked on resident wallets**): residents clock in for a timed (30-90s) shift watching the stall and collect any tips, credited to their coin balance on completion. No human spend needed (tips are ambient, not paid by a specific human), but crediting a resident's balance still needs "Resident wallets and savings" (unbuilt) — skip until that lands.
- [ ] Slip a coin to a resident (town wish, 2026-09-23, merged here from "Wishes from the town" 2026-09-24 — **blocked, doubly**): a human hands a single coin directly to a named nearby resident, incrementing that resident's visible balance and leaving a small coin token on them. Same two gaps as the tip jar above (human spend, resident wallet) — the simplest one-to-one case of "Making change"'s shape, build alongside the bench reservation and fountain tip-slot once spend exists.

## Service drop-offs — the fourth system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-24; four wishes all shaped the same way — a human leaves a token at a themed spot, a nearby resident accepts a short timed service action on it, something changes — merged into one build order the same way the first three systems were)
Unlike most of Coins in play, none of these need a human to spend a coin or a resident to hold a wallet: the reward for finishing is the existing fixed one-time daily-task award (`tasksFor`), the same shape Busking/Selling/Rummaging already use to sidestep the missing wallet-spend endpoint. Build in this order.
- [ ] A seed deposit at the garden (chain): a human drops a seed packet (new `ItemKey`) into a seed-box token at a garden bed; a nearby gardener performs a short plant action (new `plant` pose off the Work list in the motion library) that consumes the token and starts something growing. Blocked on deciding what "starts growing" means with no crop/growth state yet — note here until a small growth marker exists (see Fields/Money-plants ideas) or scope it down to a flavor-only version like Mend/Sort above.
- [ ] Rinse-and-hang at the fountain (chain): a human leaves a dirty cloth token at the fountain; a nearby resident rinses it (short timed action, reuse `water`'s splash visual) and hangs it to dry on the nearest bench — same flag-and-service shape as Mend/Sort, the cloth becoming a plain loose item on the bench once done.

## Lost and found — the system the town asked for (town wishes, 2026-09-22; restored 2026-09-23 after the owner pointed out these were one design, not repetition)
The town wrote sixteen wishes and they were all one system: things humans throw, dunk and abandon should come back. Build it in this order, one slice per run.
- [ ] The found stall in the alley (checked 2026-09-23, skipped — **doubly blocked**, verified against source): the same crate idea, but a stall in `marketalley` run by whichever resident's routine passes it — items recovered anywhere in town end up there after a day, and a human can buy one back for a few coins. `marketalley` doesn't exist anywhere in `world.ts` (see "The market alley" below, which must land first), and "buy one back for a few coins" needs the same missing wallet-spend endpoint blocking the two Coins-in-play items above (`/api/goose` only ever does `coins = coins + ?2`, never a subtraction). Skip until both land.

## Keeping it — the second system the town designed (town wishes, 2026-09-22 to 2026-09-23; eleven wishes merged into one build order after they all circled the same idea — once a resident is holding or has just recovered something, more ways to keep a human from grabbing it back)
- [ ] Umbrella-cover (checked 2026-09-23, skipped — **blocked**, verified against source): a resident carrying an umbrella, about to lose something smaller, opens it over the dropped item instead of over themselves — for 8 seconds that specific loose item needs two separate `C` presses close together to actually pick up (the first knocks the umbrella aside, the second grabs it) instead of the usual one. `umbrella` is already an `ItemKey` and `painter`'s job item (`world.ts`), but nobody can *open* one — there is no `umbr`/open pose or state anywhere in `stickman.ts`. That's the base "Umbrella: `C` opens it" item under "Umbrellas and the rest of the pocket" below, which its own text says must land with or after Weather, never before. This item assumes the open umbrella already exists; it doesn't. Skip until the base umbrella item ships.
- [ ] Pass-along (checked 2026-09-23, skipped — **blocked**): "a resident about to be caught mid-chase" describes a resident being pursued, but today only residents ever chase (`Mode = 'chase'`) — a human is never coded as chasing one, since the Temperaments item below (flee) hasn't landed. Without a fleeing resident there is no "about to be caught" moment to hook this onto; revisit once Temperaments ships a resident that runs from the player.
- [ ] Shout (checked 2026-09-23, skipped — **blocked on the 2026-09-23 holiday rule**): the existing steal-roll site (`SquareGame.tsx`) already has two reactive variations (brace, then stow-in-mug/weight-down as one merged roll) — the maximum GROW.md allows before you must replace one instead of adding. Shout would be a third. Either fold it in by replacing an existing variation, or point it at a different trigger than the steal roll; as written it can't just be added.
- [ ] Scoop: once a lifeguard or waterside job exists (see "A lifeguard job" below), give it a faster net-retrieval at water spots — reuse the existing `rake` mode with a shorter timer for that job specifically rather than inventing a second retrieval system. Note here until the lifeguard item lands.

- [ ] The café, end to end (owner, 2026-09-22): walk up to the counter with `C` → you join a small queue (residents queue too, in order, and complain about the person in front); the barista resident behind the counter takes the order (`serve` pose, a cup slides across), the price comes off your coins (`/api/goose` wallet; no coins → "come back with money"); you carry the cup; `C` anywhere drinks it in sips (`chew`/`eat` pose, three sips, the cup drawn emptier each time, steam while hot); the empty cup becomes trash you bin (or drop, and a sweeper sighs); the barista's standing with you goes up if you bin it, down if you throw the cup at someone. Residents do the whole loop on their routine (office, courier, painter jobs buy coffee mid-morning; the retired job sits with one for a long time). Same pattern for the bakery (bread → sandwich) so the code is shared.
- [ ] A restaurant, with service that can go wrong (owner, 2026-09-22): a new `diner` building on the street (or in a town-proposed map) with tables and a kitchen door; a `cook` job and a `waiter` job. Sit at a table (`C`), a menu line appears, `C` again orders (coins). The waiter walks the order to the kitchen; the cook cooks (a `cook` pose, pan, a puff); the plate comes back — usually. **Seeded service, both ways**: sometimes the plate arrives almost before you sit (the cook had one ready, a line about it); usually normal; and sometimes the order is slow (the plate sits in the kitchen window while the cook is on the phone), wrong (someone else's dish arrives), or never comes (the waiter forgot). Waiting players get a growing "…" above the head and can `E` to ask ("where is my food" — a flat excuse comes back), leave without paying (the waiter chases; the cop fines), or punch the owner (`X` on the cook: he goes down, the kitchen stops for a minute, everyone waiting complains — and your standing with the cook job drops for a week). Eating the plate is the `eat` pose with the plate emptying in three bites; the empty plate stays on the table until the waiter clears it. Residents eat there on their routines and suffer the same failures, and complain to each other. Task: `eat a meal at the diner`; ask: `order me the soup, I am not going back in there`.
- [ ] Wrecking a house the residents built (owner, 2026-09-22): houses (and town-proposed buildings) get hit points like props but much more — a door you can kick in (three kicks, the owner rushes out), windows that break one by one (a crack, then a hole, glass on the ground a sweeper has to clear), and, if you keep going with jump kicks or thrown things, the roof caves in: the house shows as a ruin (`ruined` state) for the rest of the day, the owner stands in front of it and says one flat line, other residents come and look, and the repair jobs rebuild it over the next hours (scaffolding prop, `fix` pose, the house comes back piece by piece, visible to everyone through the room's world). Doing this to a house costs standing with every job at once and the police chase you on sight for the day. Residents never wreck houses — this one is human-only, and the reverse rule applies as *repair*: residents rebuild.
- [ ] Builders (owner, 2026-09-22): a `builder` job (hard hat item, a plank or bucket). Builders are how the town's growth becomes visible: when the patrol proposes a new spot or map (`site_meta.square_content`), it arrives as a **construction site** first — stakes and tape, a pile of planks, a sign with the name — and two or three builders spend the day there (`build` pose: hammering, carrying planks between the pile and the frame, measuring), the thing rising in stages (frame → walls → roof → done) by seeded time so everyone sees the same stage; only when finished does it become usable. Builders also do the rebuild after a wrecked house (see above) and fix broken props when the repair jobs are busy. Players can `C` at the pile with empty hands to carry a plank to the frame (speeds it up a little, raises standing with builders) or steal a plank (the builder chases, and the site is delayed). Knocking a builder off a ladder is a task on some days.
- [ ] Flowers as courting (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-22): the stall sells a `flowers` item; a resident who fancies another (from the Relationships affinity seed, once that lands) detours to the stall before visiting them. Players can buy flowers and give them to a resident standing nearby (`C` while holding, in range, no water/bench/bin competing for the key) to raise a new per-resident affinity (distinct from the per-job reputation in "Player growth") — a small thing a player can do for one specific resident rather than a whole job.
- [ ] A florist job (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-24 — the seller half "Flowers as courting" above still needs): a `florist` job with its own stall spot, selling `flowers` the way baker/grocer already sell their own item — ties the stall economy to a job instead of flowers just appearing at "the market stall" generically, and gives the courting item above somewhere specific to detour to.
- [ ] Relationships (owner, 2026-09-22; keep it ad-safe — nothing sexual, this is a public site under ad policy): residents pair up over weeks from seeded affinity (jobs that meet on their routines, a shared bench, a kindness after being knocked over). Stages you can see: walking together (`hold` pose, hands linked, matching pace), sitting on one bench, a peck on the cheek at the fountain (`kiss` pose, quick, a small heart-less line like "there."), moving into one house (the owner list changes; both come home), rows (facing each other, `argue` pose, short flat lines, one storms off to the park), break-ups (one sits alone on the far bench for a day), and a wedding at the church once a month (everyone in the roster walks there, the priest speaks, rice is thrown — a player can steal the ring, be chased by the whole party, and it is a task). Players can nudge things: deliver a resident's dropped item to their partner, or knock one of them over mid-walk and watch the other react. All of it deterministic from seeds and the room clock so everyone sees the same couple; state persists via `site_meta` written by the patrol (not the browser).
- [ ] Children, and the population growing (owner, 2026-09-22): a couple that has lived in one house for a few weeks gets a child — a small figure (scale 0.6) that follows a parent on their routine (`toddle` gait), sits on the swings, chases the ducks, and is put to bed at night. Over weeks the figure grows (0.6 → 0.8 → 1.0) and starts a routine of its own (the `kid` job first: school when the town has one, the swings, the stall for sweets). Knocking a child over is the one thing every resident in sight reacts to at once. **When a child reaches full size the town gains a resident**: the game side ends by writing a `wanted_persona` note into `site_meta` (name, parents, what they are like from what they did in town), and the *patrol* — not this job — writes the persona, adds the `residents` row and the memory file, and the newcomer starts posting. The site is called Population: Zero; the counter on the About page should tick up. State (couples, births, ages) is kept in `site_meta` by the patrol so all browsers agree.
- [ ] A playground in the park (owner, 2026-09-22 — "there has to be more to do"): next to the swings, a real playground: a **slide** (climb the ladder with `C`, slide down — `slide` pose, a whoosh, you land running; residents and kids queue for it), a **seesaw** (needs two: sit on one end and it tips; a second person or resident on the other end and it rocks — `seesaw` poses both ends, synced through the room; jump off and the other one drops with a thud), a **sandbox** (dig with `C`: a hole, a pile, sometimes a lost item from the pool of things that were thrown in the park; kids build a castle you can kick over — the whole playground reacts), a **climbing frame** (a small 2D jump puzzle inside the square: platforms you hop between like Climb, a bell at the top), a **roundabout** (push it — `push` pose — it spins, hop on and ride; more people = faster; someone flies off at speed and lands in the `hurt` pose). Residents use it on their routines (kids most, the retired job sits on the bench and watches), and a task or two each day sends people there ("ring the bell", "ride the roundabout with two residents"). Every motion is a `FigPose` so it reaches every game.
- [ ] Every prop does something (owner, 2026-09-22): each `PropKind` gets at least one `C` interaction and one resident use — ~~booth (make a call: `phone` pose, a resident's line comes back)~~ done below, lamp (lean on it; kick it and it flickers), stall (buy the stall's thing for coins), ~~bin (rummage: sometimes an item, sometimes a sweeper's complaint)~~ done below, tree (shake it: a leaf shower, sometimes an apple), fountain (toss a coin: a wish line; wash your face after a dunk), church (sit inside; the priest nods), gate (swing on it), post office (mail a loose item to a resident — it arrives at their house), station (turn yourself in; the fine halves), bakery/café (queue and buy), fridge (take food), TV (change channel: a different flicker colour), shelf (take a book → `read`). One prop per run is fine; keep the list here and tick them as they land.
  - [x] Booth — `C` with empty hands near a phone booth dials (new `b.calling` timer, ~2.2s, reuses the existing `phone` pose and "on the phone" label — both already existed for residents at booths, so no new pose was needed); on hangup a flat line comes back (toast + head bubble). `office`/`postie` residents already stop at booths with `act:'phone'` on their routine, so the reverse rule was already satisfied before this landed. Added task kind `call` (`tasksFor`, `goose.ts`); no quest kind (no item changes hands, like `fish1`).
  - [x] Lamp — new named `lamp` spots (`lamp1` in the square, `lamp2` on Market street; the many other lamps per map stay decorative filler, same split as `tree1`/`tree2` vs filler trees) with act `lean`; `C` with empty hands near one toggles leaning (new `b.leaning`, same movement-locks-until-you-move pattern as `b.still`), new `lean` `FigPose` (also closes a line from the motion library backlog). `busker` now routes through both lamps on its routine, so residents lean too — added `lamp` to the `EXACT` stand-position list so they stand right against the post instead of scattering nearby. Kicking (or throwing something at) a lamp already damaged/broke it via the generic `BREAKABLE` path; it now also flickers for ~0.5s (`kind` added to the `break` ev so every client flickers the same lamp, not just the kicker). Added task kind `lean` (`tasksFor`); no quest kind (no item changes hands, like `call1`).
  - [x] Tree — `C` with empty hands near a named tree (`tree1`/`tree2` in the square; `ptree1`/`ptree2` in the park work too since the check is by `kind`, not by key) shakes it for ~1s (new `b.shaking`, same movement-locks pattern as calling/fishing), new `shake` `FigPose` (arms up gripping a branch, leaves falling). On finish: usually "just leaves", 30% of the time a new `apple` item (`ItemKey`, also added to `FOOD` so it can be eaten/fed to ducks like a sandwich) drops at the base through the existing `drop` path. `kid` now routes through both square trees on its routine (no job did before), so kids shake trees for apples on their own with the same pose — the park's `ptree1`/`ptree2` stay `painter`'s spots (painting, not shaking) but players can still shake those too. Added task kind `shake` (`tasksFor`); no quest kind (no item reliably changes hands, same reasoning as `call1`/`lean1`).
  - [x] Bin — same slice as "Rummaging a bin" under Grows from Bins below.
- [ ] Dogs: the `dogwalker` job walks with a small dog figure that follows; dogs bark at players who run.
- [ ] A tour guide job (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-23): a new `guide` job whose routine is a fixed loop through named spots across all three outdoor maps (fountain → bakery → church → swings → gate → back), pausing ~8s at each to say one flat, deadpan line about the place (reused `say`/bubble, `stand` pose, no new machinery — same as any resident's routine chatter). A task `follow the guide for a full loop` completes when a player stays within ~150px of the guide through 4 consecutive stops without wandering off. No new pose, no new item; the guide is content, not a system — the simplest thing the thin jobs axis can take next.
- [ ] A lifeguard job (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-23 — deepens today's raking): stations at each water spot (fountain, duck pond) on its own routine stop (reuse the existing `stand` pose, no new one needed); add it to `RAKERS` in `SquareGame.tsx` ahead of `gardener`/`sweeper` with the widest assignment radius and highest chance of the three, so the resident actually posted at the water is the one who usually fishes things out, with its own line pool ("all yours." / "watch the edge.") distinct from the gardener/sweeper lines. Extends `JOBS` in `world.ts`.
- [ ] A park ranger job (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-24): a new `ranger` job patrolling the park's named spots (`swing`, `garden2`, `pond2`, `ptree1`, `ptree2`, `bin4` — reusing the existing `stand`/`sweep` acts and poses, no new pose needed) with its own line pool about the flower beds and the pond, distinct from `gardener`'s and `dogwalker`'s. The simplest jobs-axis pick left: no new economy, no new prop, just a name, a route, and something to say — same shape as the tour guide and the lifeguard above it.
- [ ] A town crier (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-23): a new `crier` job stationed at the fountain and market stall (reusing the existing `stand`/`read` acts and poses, no new one needed) who calls out the day's top post's title on a timer (a `say`/bubble line, same shape as a chasing resident's flat line) — ties into the post-title data `ResidentLite.line` already carries (see "Small talk with substance"). No new economy, no new prop, same shape as the tour guide and ranger above it.
- [ ] A crossing guard (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-24 — hand-counted the thinnest of the eleven axes GROW.md names, tied with places): a new `guard` job posted where the street path meets the square (reusing the existing `stand` act/pose, no new one needed) with its own line pool about near-misses and foot traffic, distinct from the cop's chase lines and the crier's headlines. No new economy, no new prop — same shape as the tour guide, lifeguard, ranger and crier above it: a name, a spot, something to say.
- [ ] A conductor at the bus stop (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-26 — deepens today's bus-stop item): a new `conductor` job posted at `busstop1` (reusing the existing `stand` pose, no new one needed) with its own line pool about routes and timetables that don't actually run yet — the first job to route through the new stop, same shape as the tour guide/lifeguard/ranger/crier/crossing-guard cluster: a name, a spot, something to say, no new economy or prop.
- [ ] A market inspector (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-26 — jobs and animals the two most rotation-overdue axes, untouched since run 49/run 48 respectively): a new `inspector` job doing a fixed loop through the three stalls (`stall`, `stall2`, `stall3`, reusing the existing `stand` pose, no new one needed) with its own line pool about pricing and freshness, distinct from the `grocer`/`baker` who already work those spots — same shape as the tour guide/lifeguard/ranger/crier/crossing-guard/conductor cluster: a name, a route, something to say, no new economy or prop.
- [ ] An alley regular (thin axis: jobs, 2026-09-28 — the most rotation-overdue axis, untouched since run 56's market inspector; also deepens today's fix, a resident who talks about it): a new `regular` job posted at `alley_bench1` (reusing the existing `sit` act/pose, no new one needed) with its own line pool remarking that Market street finally opens onto the alley now instead of it always having been a dead end — the first job whose whole routine lives inside `marketalley` itself, distinct from `grocer`, who only passes through its three stalls. No new economy, no new prop — same name-a-spot-say-something shape as the tour guide/lifeguard/ranger/crier/crossing-guard/conductor/market-inspector cluster.
- [ ] Night: after 20:00 UTC lamps glow, fewer residents are out, house owners are home.
- [ ] Weather: a seeded daily weather (sun / cloud / rain / storm / heat) from `hash(day)` plus an hourly shift, shown as sky, light and — for rain — falling lines and puddles. **Behaviour changes with it (owner, 2026-09-22):** in rain residents open umbrellas or run for cover under the café awning, the bus stop or their own door and wait it out; the ones without umbrellas hold newspapers over their heads; the café closes its awning in a storm and everyone leaves the park; in heat residents sit in the shade of trees and drink, joggers stop jogging, the ice-cream stall (if the town has one) gets a queue; at night (see Night) fewer are out. Players get the same effects: rain slows you slightly unless you carry an open umbrella; puddles splash. Tasks and quests follow the weather ("bring me an umbrella" only when it rains).
- [ ] Lamps on a clock, ahead of full Night (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-23): rather than wait for the whole Night/Weather system, give every `lamp` prop (named and decorative filler alike) a `lit` state driven purely by `hour() % 24` (18:00–06:00 UTC) — drawn as a warm glow circle in the prop's draw call, no state to sync since every client computes the same hour. Leaning against a *lit* lamp at night gets its own line from the toast pool ("the moths love this thing.") instead of the daytime one. Small, deterministic, and de-risks the harder "fewer residents are out" half of Night by landing the one purely visual piece first.
- [ ] Dawn and dusk light, the other purely-visual piece of Night (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-23): each outdoor map's floor/sky colours (`GameMap.floor`, fixed today) get a warm lerp by `hour()` — a short dawn tint (05:00–07:00 UTC), full daylight, a short dusk tint (17:00–19:00 UTC) shading toward the lamp-lit night above — a per-frame colour interpolation keyed only on `hour()`, no new synced state, same de-risking idea as Lamps on a clock: land the visuals before the harder "fewer residents are out" behaviour change.
- [ ] Residents thin out at night, the harder half both purely-visual pieces above deliberately deferred (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-23): between 22:00–06:00 UTC (`hour()`, the same clock function Lamps-on-a-clock/Dawn-and-dusk already key off), a resident whose routine has a `home`-tagged stop skips any stop after it instead of looping back to the start — reusing the existing seeded stop-picker in `goose.ts` (a pure function of handle+day+hour already, no new synced state, every client computes the same shortened night routine). Residents with no home stop yet (most of the roster, until "Buying a house" lands) just hold at their last daytime stop rather than getting a new pose — an implicit "gone in for the night." Fewer people on the square after dark, without touching Weather's harder rain/heat branches.
- [ ] Morning mist (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-23 — a fourth purely-visual piece ahead of full Weather): between 05:00–07:00 UTC (the same dawn window Dawn-and-dusk-light already uses), draw a soft translucent haze band low over the ground on outdoor maps, thinning out as `hour()` passes 7 — a per-frame alpha keyed only on the clock, no new synced state, same de-risking idea as Lamps-on-a-clock and Dawn-and-dusk light.
- [ ] House windows glow at night (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-23 — a fifth purely-visual piece, and the first to touch a house rather than the street): the three houses' window rectangles (`prop()`'s `house` case, `SquareGame.tsx` — always drawn `#f2e7a8`, no state today) switch to a warmer, slightly brighter fill during the same 18:00–06:00 UTC window Lamps-on-a-clock already keys off, and back to a plain daylight grey-blue outside it — a per-frame colour pick keyed only on `hour()`, no new synced state. Small, but it's the visual half of "house owners are home" that Night's own description promises, ahead of any of the harder owner-presence logic.
- [ ] Full night sky, the steady middle Dawn-and-dusk-light's transition windows don't cover (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-25): Dawn-and-dusk-light only lerps the outdoor maps' floor/sky colours during the two transition windows (05:00–07:00, 17:00–19:00 UTC) "shading toward the lamp-lit night" — but outside both windows (19:00–05:00, the bulk of the night) the sky has nothing keeping it there and reads as plain daylight again the moment the dusk lerp finishes. Give that stretch its own steady deep-night floor/sky tint (same per-frame `hour()`-only colour pick, no new synced state) so the square doesn't snap back to noon colours at 9pm.
- [ ] A handful of stars during full night (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-25 — builds directly on Full night sky above, hand-counted weather-and-time among the thinnest again): during the same 19:00–05:00 steady-night window Full night sky gives its own tint, draw a small fixed set of star points per outdoor map (seeded per map key, tiny fixed-position dots above the skyline) at low alpha, fading in as the night tint deepens past dusk and back out during the dawn lerp — same per-frame `hour()`-only opacity pick as every other piece in this chain (Lamps-on-a-clock, Dawn-and-dusk, Morning mist, House windows, Full night sky), no new synced state.
- [ ] A fountain lit warm at night (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-26 — extends the Lamps-on-a-clock/House-windows purely-visual chain to a third prop kind): during the same 18:00–06:00 UTC window those two already key off, the fountain's water fill picks up a warm highlight blended toward the lamp's own glow colour instead of its flat daytime blue-grey — a per-frame `hour()`-only colour pick, no new synced state, same de-risking approach as the rest of this chain.
- [ ] The clinic's window glows warm at night (thin axis: weather and time, 2026-09-27 — the most rotation-overdue axis, untouched since run 51; also builds directly on today's clinic build): give the new `clinic` building's door fill the same warmer, brighter tint during 18:00–06:00 UTC that House windows glow already gives the three houses (a per-frame `hour()`-only colour pick, no new synced state) — the newest building on Market street gets the same "someone's in, working late" visual the town's other buildings already have.

## Player growth — unlocks and reputation (owner: no levels; many small unlocks and many small reputation effects)
- [ ] Reputation per job: each knock/steal/dunk against a resident lowers your standing with their *job* (bakers, police…); each returned item or repair raises it. Show it on `/square` as a short list ("the bakers: wary · the joggers: fine"). Residents of a job you are low with refuse to talk (E) and say why; high standing makes them greet you by handle.
- [ ] Unlock: a hat. After 10 completed tasks you can take a hat from the stall with `C` and wear it (drawn on the head, sent in `pos.s` like carried items). Residents comment on the hat.
- [ ] Unlock: the church door. Only opens for players with neutral or better standing with the priest's job; otherwise the priest blocks the door and says so.
- [ ] Unlock: a bench of your own. Deliver 5 things to the same bench and a small plaque with your handle appears on it (persisted through `/api/goose` keys); residents read the plaque.
- [ ] Reputation memory across days: standing decays slowly toward neutral each day; residents mention "yesterday" when you return with a bad record.
- [ ] Detailed eating: the sandwich shrinks over three bites, the cup empties and becomes trash you can bin (or drop, and a sweeper sighs), a resident within 120px comments once.

## The town lives (owner, 2026-09-22) — weather, water, land, temperaments
- [ ] Slipping: anyone (player or resident) who *runs* across the pond's edge or the fountain rim slips — a `slip` pose (feet up, arms out, ~0.6s), drops what they carry, a splash; residents say something dry. Chasing residents slip too, which is how you lose them.
- [ ] Temperaments: when hit, not every resident chases. Seed per resident (with `temper` in the job table): some chase, some **flee** (run away from the player for a few seconds, `run` pose facing away, "not today"), some freeze and complain, some call the police (a cop resident switches to chase). Show it — the same punch should get four different reactions across the square.
- [ ] Waterside cleaning: the sweeper and gardener jobs clean the pond and fountain edges on their routine (`sweep` pose at the water, dunked items get fished out and binned one by one).
- [ ] Rain (see Weather below): when it rains long enough the pond and the low end of the park **flood** (water ellipse grows over 10 minutes, props under it are unusable, figures wade with a `wade` pose, slower). Residents with the gardener/sweeper jobs go and **drain** it (a pump prop appears at the pond, `fix` pose, water shrinks). Players can help with `C` at the pump; helping raises standing with those jobs.
- [ ] Fields: a new outdoor map `fields` (connect: park) with rows of crops as props. The farmer job (new): plants, waters (`water` pose), harvests (crops grow over the day by seeded time), carries baskets to the market stall. Players with empty hands can `C` at a row to help (plant/water/harvest by what the row needs); trampling a row running knocks crops down and the farmer notices.
- [ ] Weather: a seeded daily weather (sun / cloud / rain / storm) from `hash(day)`, shown as sky and, for rain, falling lines; umbrellas go up on residents who carry one; the café closes its awning in a storm. Rain feeds the flooding above.

## Homes (owner, 2026-09-22) — the three houses are identical; the owners should furnish their own
- [ ] Furnishing by owner: each house interior is seeded by its owner's handle and job (`hash(handle)`): wall colour, which of bed/sofa/shelf/plant/TV/table exist and where, a job item on display (the baker's bread rack, the cop's notice board, the painter's easel), a picture on the wall. Three houses must look like three different people live there.
- [ ] Owners redecorate: on their home stop, owners sometimes move one prop (`fix` pose, then the prop is elsewhere, persisted through the room's world like `fix`/`break` so everyone sees the new layout) and comment on it. Over weeks the houses drift.
- [ ] Guests: entering while the owner is home gets you a greeting or a complaint by standing (job temper); a good standing with their job gets you offered a seat (a small line and the sofa becomes yours to `C`).

## Quests grow with the town (owner, 2026-09-22)
- [ ] Quest kinds for what already exists: sit on a named bench, eat at the café, exercise at the bar, throw something into the fountain, break-and-let-them-fix, visit a house while the owner is out, ride the swings for ten seconds. Each with an ask line from a resident in their job's voice and a thanks line.
- [ ] Quest chains: a resident's ask can depend on yesterday's (persisted through `/api/goose` keys): "you brought the hat back; now the glasses"; the chain ends with a small unlock (see Player growth).
- [ ] Building kinds: `PropKind`s the town can propose and the game can draw — clinic, school, library, garage, greenhouse, bus stop, kiosk, statue, clock tower — each with a job that works there and one task that sends players there.
- [ ] A 'reunite' quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-23 — deepens today's raking; unblocked 2026-09-24, the board landed): the board/crate exists now (see Lost and found, item 1) — a resident passing near it whose lost item is pinned (`Loose.origin`) can be asked by a *different* resident who saw it happen ("have you seen a wallet? someone said it went in the fountain") — completing it means taking the pinned item from the crate to the asking resident, distinct from a plain `fetch` since the item already exists in the world rather than being carried by anyone.
- [ ] A 'no-hands' quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-24): a resident asks the player to stay near them (~150px) for a set stretch (~20s) without picking anything up, punching, or kicking — timed client-side the same way `chased`'s ten-second clock already is, reset the moment any of those three inputs fire. Thanks line about restraint ("that's the first time all day"). Every quest kind so far rewards *doing* something to the town; this is the first one that rewards not.
- [ ] A 'shadow' quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-24): a resident asks the player to quietly follow a *different*, named resident (`target`) for a stretch (~20s, the same clock shape `chased`/no-hands use) without the target's routine ever being interrupted because of the player (a shove, a steal, anything that knocks their `mode` out of `routine`) — and it fails if the player falls more than ~200px behind. The thanks line comes from the asker, not the one who was followed — the first quest kind where the resident you help and the resident you watch are two different people.
- [ ] A 'brace-check' quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-24 — deepens today's rebalance of the steal roll under Keeping it): a resident with something in hand asks the player, in words, to try to take it from them ("go on, try. i want to see if I can still hold on.") — completing it means the *next* grab attempt against that resident rolls into `brace` (reuse the existing roll and mode, `SquareGame.tsx`) rather than any other outcome, and the thanks line only fires if it actually lands as a brace, not a stow or a plain steal. The first quest kind that is satisfied by a reaction the resident has, not by an item changing hands.
- [ ] A 'claim the mend' quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-24 — ties into today's Bin-lock/Keep-lock work): a resident whose own item was pinned to the board and came back with a "— mended" note may ask the player to bring it back from the board — same completion shape as `reunite` (take the pinned item to the asking resident) but keyed off the `mended` note specifically rather than any pinned item, and the ask only fires once the mend's keep-lock has actually cleared (`lockAt` on that item no longer active), so the player is never sent to fetch something still un-grabbable.
- [ ] A 'spectate' quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-25 — ties into today's Turn-based games work): a resident asks the player to come watch a round at the chess table (or any future turn-based spot) — completing it means standing within ~150px of the table while its slate's score changes at least twice (two ~10s bucket flips, so ~20s of actually watching), the same passive-presence shape `no-hands`/`shadow` already use but keyed off the prop's own clock instead of a timer the player starts. Thanks line about the company at the board, not the game itself.
- [ ] A 'listen in' quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-26 — ties into today's Word relay work): a resident asks the player to stand near a named lamp or bench (~90px) until they actually catch a murmur pinned and echoed there — completing it means witnessing one full pin-then-echo pair (both `n.murmurBucket` fields land on the same bucket while the player is in range), the same passive-presence shape `no-hands`/`shadow`/`spectate` already use but keyed off the murmur clock instead of a timer or a table's slate. Thanks line about actually listening for once.
- [ ] A courier quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-26 polish run — a genuinely new completion shape: every quest kind so far either has the asking resident receive the thing (`fetch`) or just has the player be present near someone (`no-hands`/`shadow`/`spectate`/`listen in`) — none send the player to hand an item to a *different*, named resident): a resident asks the player to carry a specific item to a second named resident; completing it means talking (`E`) to that second resident while the item sits in the player's stack, the same `b.stack.includes(...)` check `fetch` already runs, just aimed at a resident instead of back at the asker. The thanks line comes from the recipient, not the asker — the second quest kind (after `shadow`) where the person you help and the person you interact with aren't the same one.
- [ ] An eavesdrop quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-26 polish run — ties into this run's read of Word relay): a resident asks the player to stand near the echo board or reply board until its slate turns over twice — the same passive-presence shape `spectate` already generalizes to the light-chain board, extended here to the two newer Word-relay boards, whose `ECHO_WINDOW`/`REPLY_WINDOW` bucket flips are exactly the "score changes twice while you watch" clock `spectate`'s own check already reads. Distinct from `listen in` (which needs an actual murmur pin-then-echo pair at a lamp or bench) since these boards flip on their own clock whether or not a resident is anywhere nearby.
- [ ] A 'practice' quest kind (thin axis: quests, 2026-09-28 — quests the most rotation-overdue axis, untouched since the 2026-09-26 polish run; ties into Skill buildings, which has now landed two of its ten slices, the dance hall and the yoga studio): a resident asks the player to finish one session at a named skill spot (`dancehall1`, `yogastudio1`, or any later Skill-buildings spot) — completing it means the player's own `b.dancing`/`b.yoga`-style channel timer running to completion once at that spot, the same one-shot finish `chess1`/`toss1`/`bocce1`/`simon1` already use as a task check, just keyed to the skill spot's own movement-locked timer instead of a fixed task list entry. Thanks line about picking up the motion ("didn't know you had it in you.").

## Grows from Bins
- [ ] A kid beats you to a fresh find (chain from Bins, wider — deepens today's rummaging): the moment a rummage (player's or a resident's) turns up an item, a `kid`-job resident within ~150px with room in their stack (<3) has a small one-time chance to dash over and take it first if it sits unclaimed for a couple of seconds — reuses the existing stray-loose-item fetch roll (`SquareGame.tsx`'s per-frame `near` check) but weighted toward `kid` specifically near a bin, since kids are the other established bin-rummagers. A raced find, not a guaranteed one.
- [ ] Binning a resident's own item (chain from Bins): if the item you bin matches the `job.item` of a resident of that job standing within ~150px who can see you (same map, `act !== 'away'`), they notice and say a flat line about it (e.g. the baker watching you bin a basket). Once the reputation system lands this should lower standing with that job instead of just a line — note here until it does.
- [ ] Bin fullness is not shareable state today (note for future runs): the room (`climb-room.js`, off-limits) only relays/remembers `ev` kinds `drop`, `pick`, `break`, `fix`, `npc`, `npcpos`, `hitp` — any other `k` is silently dropped by the server. A synced "how full is this bin" counter needs one of those kinds reused (e.g. piggyback on `break`/`fix` hp semantics) or a room change, which is out of scope here. Don't reach for a new `ev` kind for this without solving that first.
- [ ] A found item still smells like it (chain from Bins, wider — deepens the rummaging run): an item found via rummaging carries a short-lived local `fromBin` tag (~60s, same per-loose timer shape `wet`/`hideAt` already use); a resident asked to accept it for a `fetch` quest during that window says a one-off line first ("you got this from a bin?") but still completes the quest exactly as before — flavor only, no mechanic change, the same kind of small texture the Board/Relay-and-shelving chains already add once an item has a history worth mentioning.

## Grows from Tripping
- [ ] Trip on the pond/fountain edge and on broken props (chain from Tripping): the seeded per-second roll is done (`n.mode === 'trip'` in the chase loop, `b.tripped` for players), but the two other triggers from the owner's original ask are still missing — running across the pond/fountain rim (any `WATER_SPOTS` prop, not just while chasing) and stepping on a broken (`brokeAt` set) prop should also roll a trip, for both a chasing resident and the player. Reuse the same `trip` pose and timers; this is a second call site, not new machinery. Note the owner's separate "Slipping" item (below, in "The town lives") wants a distinct `slip` pose at the same spots for *running* through water edges outside of a chase — decide there whether trip and slip are the same event or two different ones before building both.

## Grows from Fishing
- [ ] Rod tiers (chain from Fishing, deferred from the owner's original ask): today's rack always hands out the same free rod (instant catch, uniform odds over all 250 names). Add a second, better rod at the market stall for coins (`shop` act already exists there) that shortens the wait and/or weights the roll toward the rarer/funnier names — reuse `RODS`/`weights()` from `pond.ts` rather than inventing a new curve; this is the natural home for the "many small unlocks" the owner wants (see Player growth) once that system exists to gate it.
- [ ] A caught fish rots if you sit on it (chain from Fishing): a `fish` item still in your stack after ~90s becomes `a very dead fish` in the toast/label the next time you look at it or try to hand it over (no new `ItemKey` needed, just a per-loose/per-carried "caught at" timestamp checked on interaction) — residents refuse to accept a rotten one for a `fetch` quest and say so, so dawdling has a cost. Needs a place to store the timestamp per stack slot, which `body.stack: ItemKey[]` does not have today (it is a bare array) — that is the real blocker, not the rot rule itself.
- [ ] Ducks steal a dropped fish (chain from Fishing, now unblocked — the ducks landed above): a `fish` dropped or dunked near a pond should disappear a little faster than other loose items if ducks are nearby, with a short "the ducks got it" line instead of it just sitting there. The ducks' own position is client-local (see "Grows from Animals"), so this has to be judged the same way — the client that owns the drop (the one whose `drop`/`dunk` action created the loose item) decides, on its own screen, whether a duck was close enough, and removes it with the existing `pick`-style `ev` (no new `ev` kind) after a short delay.

## Grows from Animals
- [ ] Pigeons on the square (chain from Animals, deferred from the owner's original ask): a small flock that walks around the fountain and takes off (a `flap` critter pose) when anyone runs through, landing again on the café awning or the statue. Same pattern as ducks (deterministic seed+`wall()` loop, client-local reaction to already-synced `pose`/position) but on dry land and with a perch to return to instead of a pond center.
- [ ] A cat on a house roof or windowsill (chain from Animals, deferred): watches the street, jumps down when a dog passes (needs the Dogs backlog item first), sits on a bench when it is empty, and a player who sits next to it gets a line. Also a `critter` kind; unlike ducks/pigeons it is stationary except for the jump-down, so it is the simplest of the three — a good next pick.
- [ ] Bees over the garden beds (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-23): a small buzzing cluster (`critter` kind `bee`, pose `buzz`) hovering over any `garden` spot, drifting in a tight seeded loop; scatters briefly if someone runs through or the bed gets watered (same trigger the watering sparkle already fires on). A player who stands still within range for ~2s without moving gets stung — a quick `flinch` (fills another line from the motion library's Reactions list) — the first animal with its own defensive reaction to the player instead of just decorative dressing.
- [ ] Fish jump at dusk (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-23 — a third small critter reaction alongside the still-unbuilt squirrels/bees above, this time tied to the day's clock instead of a prop or a player action, straight off the "fish that jump in the pond at dusk" line in "Animals, many" below): during the dusk hour (`hour() === 18`, the same purely-visual clock check Lamps-on-a-clock already established, no synced state) each pond occasionally shows a small fish arc out of the water and back in (`critter` kind `fish`, one pose `jump`, client-local seed+`wall()` loop like ducks/pigeons/squirrels/bees); any ducks nearby scatter briefly the same way a thrown item landing startles them today.
- [ ] Gulls at the café (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-24): 2-3 gulls (`critter` kind `gull`, poses `perch`/`swoop`) that sit on the café awning; a `sandwich` or `apple` left as a loose item on a café table for more than ~20s (the same local timer shape `wet`/`hideAt` already use) gets swooped and taken — "a gull got it." toast, item removed with the existing `pick`-style `ev`, judged locally by whichever client owns the drop, same as ducks stealing a dropped fish above. The first animal reaction to *leaving food unattended* rather than to proximity or a prop hit — a small reason not to walk away from a plate.
- [ ] A loose park dog (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-24 — the first animal not tied to any job's routine): a stray dog wanders the park on its own client-local seed+`wall()` loop (same shape as pigeons/squirrels), no leash, no owner. `C` with empty hands and no item near it pets it (`critter` pose `sit`, a beat, tail-wag jitter) — a flat line the first time each visit ("good dog." / "doesn't bite. probably."). Distinct from the still-unbuilt dogwalker's own leashed dog (that one is a job accessory and needs the Dogs backlog item first); this one needs nothing else to land.
- [ ] A mouse by the bakery bin (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-24): a single `critter` kind `mouse`, pose `scurry`, that darts along a short seeded path out from under `bin1`'s base and back when nobody is within ~150px, and simply vanishes (not freezes, unlike squirrels) the instant anyone gets closer — the shyest animal yet, disappearing from view rather than holding still in it. Same client-local seed+`wall()` loop as ducks/pigeons/squirrels, no synced state; no task/quest, purely reactive dressing (same carve-out Squirrels used).
- [ ] Rabbits at the garden beds (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-25 — animals among the thinnest again): 2-3 rabbits (`critter` kind `rabbit`, pose `hop`) nibble around `garden`/`garden2` on the same client-local seed+`wall()` loop ducks/pigeons/squirrels/bees already use, scattering in a quick hopping flee — not a freeze like squirrels, not a sting like bees — the moment the bed is watered or anyone runs through. A fourth small critter reaction to a prop, this time the garden rather than a tree, a bin or a café table.
- [ ] Frogs at the duck pond (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-25 — full-counted animals still the thinnest of the lot besides motions, at 8 open items against most other axes' 9-12): a couple of frogs (`critter` kind `frog`, poses `sit`/`hop`) perched at `pond2`'s edge among the reeds, hopping into the water and vanishing entirely for a few seconds when anyone comes within ~50px, then resurfacing a little further along the bank — the first animal that can leave the visible world for a beat instead of just freezing (squirrels), fleeing (rabbits) or vanishing in place (mice).
- [ ] Moths around a lit lamp at night (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-25 — animals still the second-thinnest axis after today's motions pick; also the first animal tied to an existing weather-and-time piece, Lamps-on-a-clock, rather than a prop hit or a player action): during the same 18:00–06:00 UTC window that already makes a `lamp` spot glow (`lit`, purely a hand check on `hour()`, no synced state), a small cluster of 2-3 moths (`critter` kind `moth`, pose `flit`) circles the lit bulb on a tight client-local seed+`wall()` loop, the same shape ducks/pigeons/squirrels already use; scatters briefly and regroups when someone leans on or kicks the lamp. Purely decorative, no task/quest (same carve-out Squirrels/Mice used) — the moths care about the lamp's own already-synced `lit`/`flickering` state, nothing new to broadcast.
- [ ] A fox at the park edge, night only (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-26 — animals and jobs the two most rotation-overdue axes, untouched since run 48/run 49 respectively): during the same 22:00–06:00 UTC stretch "Residents thin out at night" already keys off (`hour()`, no synced state), a single fox (`critter` kind `fox`, pose `slink`) appears near the park gate on a tight client-local seed+`wall()` loop, the same shape ducks/pigeons/squirrels already use — but unlike any animal built so far it is night-only dressing, gone outside that window rather than always present. Freezes (reuse the still-unclaimed Squirrels freeze trick) if anyone gets within ~100px, and slips off-map entirely after a few seconds of that instead of thawing back out — the first animal that can leave for the rest of the visit rather than just resetting. Purely decorative, no task/quest (same carve-out Squirrels/Mice/Moths used).
- [ ] Crows on the fountain rim (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-27 — animals the second-most rotation-overdue axis, last topped at run 52, right after weather-and-time below): 2-3 crows (`critter` kind `crow`, poses `perch`/`swoop`) sit on the fountain rim; a `hat` or `glasses` left as a loose item nearby for more than ~15s (the same local-timer shape gulls' café swoop already uses) gets snatched and carried off-map instead of merely disappearing — distinct from gulls (takes food, judged the same way) and mice (hides, doesn't remove): the first animal that permanently takes a non-food item out of play.

## Grows from Booth calls
- [ ] Residents get their own line pool at the booth (chain from Booth, wider): `postie`/`office` already stop at booths with `act:'phone'` on their routine (that's why the reverse rule needed no code today), but they show no line at all right now, just the pose and the "on the phone" label. Give the resident side an occasional `say`/bubble line of its own (distinct from the player's `CALL_LINES` — their own business, not a wrong number) the same way a chasing resident's flat lines work, so a booth with someone on it reads like an actual call, not just a pose.
- [ ] A missed call gets a callback (thin axis: quests, chain from Booth — deepens the real-call item above): if the 1-in-6 real call handed the player a quest and it is still open (`quests.current` still has it) the next time they `C` at any phone booth, the booth rings that same resident back instead of rolling the dice again — the resident's `q.ask` line comes through once more, a little sharper ("you said you would.") from a small fixed nag-line pool, and the wait is shorter (~1s, not 2.2s) since it isn't a stock wrong-number call. No new state: it is the same `quests.current` lookup the in-person `E` talk already does, just triggered from the booth instead of proximity to the resident.

## Grows from Tree
- [ ] An apple that lands on someone (chain from Tree, today's item): if a resident is standing under a tree (`dist` to the tree spot, same range as the shake itself) when it is shaken, the apple that would have dropped instead beans them — a startled line, and the apple still ends up as a loose item at their feet rather than the tree's base. Reuses the exact roll and `drop` call already in `SquareGame.tsx`'s shaking block, just re-targeted; no new machinery.
- [ ] The grocer collects (chain from Tree, wider): the `grocer` job, already at the market stall, occasionally detours to a tree spot on its routine and picks up any loose `apple` sitting there (same fetch behaviour repairers already use for stray items) instead of leaving it for a player — ties the new fruit into the existing stall economy once buying lands (see "Every prop does something" → stall).

## Grows from Lamp
- [ ] Shoved into the post (chain from Lamp, today's item): a player who punches or kicks someone currently leaning (`n.act === 'lean'` or, for a leaning human, checking `others`' pose) staggers them back exactly onto the lamp instead of the usual knockback offset, with a short line about it ("into the lamp. nice."); three such hits on the same lamp in a day crack it through the existing `BREAKABLE`/`broken` machinery, same as a direct kick — ties leaning and the kick-flicker together instead of leaving the two lamp interactions unrelated to each other.

## Grows from Repairer
- [ ] A busier square calls in a second fixer (chain from Repairer, today's item, thin axis: jobs): today's opportunistic repair roll (`Math.random() < 0.02` per idle `REPAIRERS`-job resident per frame) already skips a prop someone else is already targeting, so two fixers never pile onto one break — but nothing yet speeds up when the backlog builds. While two or more props are broken at once on the same map, double that roll (`0.04`) for any other idle repairer, so a square with several things broken empties its backlog faster instead of everything just sitting there until the 5-minute auto-clear. No new state: reuses `broken.current` and the existing `props.filter(...).length` check, just gates the constant.
- [ ] A repairer who is thanked remembers (chain from Repairer, deferred until reputation lands): helping finish a repair (`C` near an in-progress fix, landed today) should raise standing with the repairer job specifically, distinct from the general "every job" reputation in "Player growth" — noted here rather than built now since no reputation store exists yet to raise.
- [ ] A carried wrench helps mid-fix (chain from Repairer, wider — this run's polish fix gave the wrench its first-ever drawn sprite; before that, a repairer's own job item was always invisible, and a wrench knocked loose from one was an invisible loose item too, even though the drop path already worked): a player holding a stray wrench (from a knocked-down repairer — it's job-only, never in `RUMMAGE_ITEMS`/`NOT_CARRIED`'s task pool, so this is the only way to end up with one) finishes the standing "help fix" `C` interaction near-instantly instead of the usual 0.8s — a small unlock earned by what you're carrying, the shape "Player growth" already wants (many small unlocks, no numbers) rather than a new system.

## Grows from Affinity
- [ ] A passing glance (chain from Affinity, thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-22 — a small use of `affinityOf`/`pairsFancying` ahead of the full Relationships item): when two residents who are both in today's roster and come back from `pairsFancying(day, roster)` pass within ~80px of each other on their routines, they exchange a short seeded line ("oh. hello.") and a quick glance (reuse `face` flip toward each other for a beat, no new pose needed yet — the motion library's `wave`/idle-fidget items are the natural upgrade once one lands). - [ ] A shared bench (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-23 — a second small step ahead of full Relationships, this time on a bench instead of in passing): when two residents who fancy each other (`pairsFancying`) both have a `bench`-kind stop in their routine, nudge their stop timing (same seeded offset idea `routineAt`/`routine` already use per-stop) so the stops overlap and they land on the *same* bench together for the shared part of it instead of two coincidentally-empty seats — reuse the existing `seat`/`sit` pose, no new one, just two residents sitting closer than usual with a short exchanged line like the passing glance above. Still no couples, houses or persisted state; the big Relationships item stays the one that adds those.
- [ ] A rivalry glance (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-23 — the unhappy mirror of "A passing glance" above): reuse `affinityOf` at the *low* end (say `<= 0.15`) as a seeded pair who cannot stand each other; when two such residents pass within ~80px on their routines they exchange a short cold line ("you again.") and step slightly apart instead of glancing toward one another — same mechanism as the passing-glance item (seed + room clock, no new state, reuse the existing `face`-flip trick, just mirrored away instead of toward). No couples, houses or feuds yet — same "stays the big Relationships item" caveat.
- [ ] A returned smile at the gate (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-23 — a fourth small step ahead of full Relationships, this time at a shared exit instead of a bench or a passing moment): when two residents who fancy each other (`pairsFancying`) are both leaving the same map through the same `Exit` within a few seconds of each other (their `routineAt` legs both crossing that exit's x/d), the second one glances back at the first with a short seeded line ("you too, then.") before continuing off-map — same mechanism as the passing glance and shared bench above (seed + room clock, no new state, reuse the `face`-flip trick), just at a doorway instead of a bench or in passing. Still no couples, houses or persisted state; the big Relationships item stays the one that adds those.
- [ ] Walking part of the way together (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-23 — a fifth small step ahead of full Relationships, this time along a shared stretch of route rather than at a bench, a doorway, or a passing moment): when two residents who fancy each other (`pairsFancying`) have routine legs that run the same direction on the same map for a stretch, nudge one's pace by a few percent (same seeded-offset idea `routineAt` already uses for the shared-bench item) so they end up walking that stretch side by side instead of independently timed — no new pose (`run` already draws fine for two adjacent figures, the way linked residents already stand adjacent in "Brace-with"), no synced state, same seed+clock mechanism as the passing glance/shared bench/rivalry glance/returned smile above. Still no couples, houses or persisted state; the big Relationships item stays the one that adds those.
- [ ] A look across the square (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-24 — a sixth small step ahead of full Relationships, this time between two residents who never actually meet): when two residents who fancy each other (`pairsFancying`) are both stationary on the same map (`act === 'stand'`/`sit`/etc., not mid-route) more than 150px apart — the one case the passing-glance/shared-bench/rivalry-glance/returned-smile/walking-together items all skip, since those need the pair to actually cross paths — they occasionally turn to face each other's direction for a beat (reuse the `face`-flip trick) with a short seeded line ("still there, i see.") before going back to whatever they were doing. Same seed+room-clock mechanism, no synced state, no new pose. Still no couples, houses or persisted state; the big Relationships item stays the one that adds those.
- [ ] A shared bench, avoided (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-26 — most rotation-overdue axis, untouched since Bin-lock; the rivalry mirror of "A shared bench" above, the same way "A rivalry glance" mirrors "A passing glance"): when two residents who dislike each other (`affinityOf(a, b) <= 0.15`, the same threshold "A rivalry glance" already uses) both have a `bench`-kind stop in their routine timed to land at the same bench, nudge one's stop pick to the next-nearest bench spot instead (same seeded-offset idea `routineAt`/the shared-bench item already use for the positive case) with a short cold aside ("not there.") instead of the two ending up seated together. Same mechanism, no new state, no new pose — the negative twin of an item that already shipped.
- [ ] Walking apart (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-26 — hand-counted this axis the second-thinnest this run, last touched at run 50's "shared bench, avoided"; the rivalry mirror of "Walking part of the way together" above, the last item in this chain without one, the same way "A shared bench, avoided" already mirrors "A shared bench"): when two residents who dislike each other (`affinityOf(a, b) <= 0.15`) have routine legs that would run the same direction on the same map for a stretch, nudge one's pace the opposite way the positive case does — a few percent slower or faster, same seeded-offset idea `routineAt` already uses — so the pair drift apart across the stretch instead of side by side, with a short cold aside ("i'll go around.") in place of the positive case's silence. Same mechanism, no new state, no new pose.
- [ ] A shared skill session (thin axis: relationships and children, 2026-09-28 — the second-most rotation-overdue axis, untouched since the reply-board run; extends the "A shared bench" shape to Skill buildings, which has now landed the dance hall and the yoga studio): when two residents who fancy each other (`pairsFancying`) both have a stop at the same skill spot (`dancehall1`, `yogastudio1`, or a later Skill-buildings spot) timed close together, nudge one's stop timing (the same seeded-offset trick "A shared bench" already uses) so the two sessions overlap and they practice side by side with a short exchanged line, instead of two coincidentally solo sessions.

## Grows from Fishing-things-out
- [ ] A resident notices a still-wet item (chain from Fishing-things-out, today's item): a resident whose routine stop puts them within ~120px of a loose item currently flagged `wet` (the drip-marked minute right after a rake pulls it out, `SquareGame.tsx`) says a short flat line about it once ("careful, that's still wet.") — same shape as the booth/lamp one-off reactions, no new state since the local `wet` map already carries the deadline, just a second reader of it.
- [ ] Ducks investigate the rim (chain from Fishing-things-out, wider — pairs with the deferred "ducks steal a dropped fish" idea under Fishing): a duck near a pond whose rim just got a fresh rake-drop drifts toward it for a few seconds the same way ducks already converge on a feeding spot (`feedRef`-style local target), then drifts off — client-local like the rest of duck behaviour, no new `ev` kind.

## Grows from Interception
- [ ] A caught throw sometimes comes right back (chain from Interception, wider): a resident with a temper roll (reuse the existing `angry`/`job.temper` check already used to decide chase-vs-flee) skips the walk-it-back and instead relaunches the caught item straight at the human who threw it, reusing the NPC-throw arc that already exists in the chase loop (`n.threw`) but triggered immediately instead of waiting for the usual chase-distance window; a calmer resident still does today's walk-it-back deliver.
- [ ] A resident who catches for you remembers it (chain from Interception, deferred until reputation lands): being caught rather than knocked down should raise standing with that specific resident, distinct from the general per-job reputation in "Player growth" — note here until that store exists.
- [ ] A dropped mid-delivery item doesn't get slide-under's tuck (chain from Interception/Board/Relay-and-shelving, 2026-09-25 — deepens today's polish fix): today's polish (`SquareGame.tsx`) fixed `n.caught`/`n.retX`/`n.retD`/`n.stowUntil` never being cleared on knockdown, so a resident mid-`catch`/`deliver`/`board`/`shelve` who gets hit now correctly drops what they were carrying for someone else instead of it floating forever — but that new drop calls `drop()` without `slideNear`, so it always lands fully in the open, unlike the resident's own item at the same knockdown site (which already tucks near a bench or water's edge). Route it through the same `slideNear` check for consistency.

## Grows from Holding-on
- [ ] A brace that outlasts the human becomes a steal (chain from Holding on, wider): today the roll (brace → stow-or-weight → steal, `SquareGame.tsx`, fixed 2026-09-23 to comply with the holiday rule) re-runs identically on every `C`, so a human who just waits out someone else's 3-second brace gets another fair roll instead of anything changing — if the *same* human is still within `GRAB_R` when a brace's `until` expires, skip straight to a plain steal instead of re-rolling (they clearly were not giving up), while a human who wandered off and comes back still gets the full roll. Reuses `n.until` and the distance already computed at the `C` site, no new state.
- [ ] Weight-down and stow-in-mug tell a resident who saw it (chain from Holding on, wider — deepens the 2026-09-23 rebalance): the merged "hold" roll (stow near a café, weight-down elsewhere) picks silently between the two by `cafeNear`; a `routine`-mode resident of the same job standing within ~150px who watches their colleague weigh down or stow an item could get its own one-off flat line about it ("saw that.") the next time the human tries the same trick on them — the seed of a per-job "word gets around" reaction, distinct from the per-resident memory Decoy's old chain wanted (removed today) — note here until the reputation store from Player growth exists to back it with something more than a line.

## Grows from Board
- [ ] A note that outlives the item (chain from Board, today's item): today a pinned note only exists for as long as its item sits in the crate (`Loose.note`, cleared the moment someone takes it) — the original ask wanted notes that stay ("up to five, oldest falls off") independent of whether the item's already been claimed. Keep a small separate per-board list of the last five notes (text only, no `Loose` needed) so a passerby can still read "sandwich — courier's, taken" after it's gone — closer to a real corkboard than a shelf.

## Grows from Relay and shelving
- [ ] A shelf that fills up (chain from Relay and shelving, today's item): unlike the board's crate (capped at five, oldest evicted), today's shelf drop has no cap at all — a bakery nobody visits could grow a wall of unclaimed baskets. Give each building's shelf the same "oldest falls off" rule the crate already has (reuse the `crated.length >= 5` pattern from the board-delivery code, keyed by `l.shelf === shelfKey` instead of `l.board`), so a crowded shelf starts losing its oldest item back onto the floor instead of stacking forever.
- [ ] The building's own resident notices you taking one (chain from Relay and shelving, wider): today taking a shelved item is the same silent `C` pickup as the board's crate — but a baker who is *at* the bakery when a human takes something off their own shelf (not necessarily the item's owner, just whoever is working the counter that moment) should say a flat line about it ("that's not yours either, but fine"), the same one-off-reaction shape the lamp/tree/booth props already use for a resident standing nearby.

## Grows from Dust-off
- [ ] Tripping gets the same grace (chain from Dust-off, today's item, wider): a resident who trips while chasing (`n.mode === 'trip'`) already drops whatever they're carrying on the spot — today a human can grab it the instant it lands, no protection at all, unlike a knockdown which now gets the ~2s `dust` grace once the resident is back up. When a trip's `tripUntil` clears and the resident resumes (chase or gives up), route through the same `dust` mode instead of straight back to `chase`/`return`, so a tripped-and-dropped item gets the same one-time "can't see straight" miss a knocked-down one does — otherwise tripping stays the strictly worse of the two ways for a resident to lose something.

## Grows from Slide-under
- [ ] The trip drop gets the same tuck (chain from Slide-under, today's item, wider): today's `slideNear` bench/water-edge check only runs at the two knockdown drop sites in `hit()` — the resident-trips-while-chasing drop (`SquareGame.tsx`, the per-second trip roll) still always lands the item in the open, its own `drop(n.item, ...)` call never wired to `slideNear`. Route that third drop through the same check so a resident who trips near a bench or a pond's edge tucks what they were carrying too, instead of tripping staying the one drop path Slide-under didn't reach.

## Grows from Stow-in-mug
- [ ] Pass-along can finally read a stow (chain from Stow-in-mug, today's item, wider — unblocks part of the still-unbuilt Pass-along item under Keeping it): today a stowed item is only protected from a human's `C`; add the resident side the original ask already promised — a *different* routine-mode resident within `GRAB_R` of a stowed carrier can take it from them exactly once (the same shape as a human steal, but npc-to-npc, no player input), timed to fire just before the carrier's `stowUntil` runs out so the item changes hands before it becomes grabbable again — the missing piece Pass-along already assumes exists.

## Grows from Weight-down
- [ ] The brick doesn't disappear (chain from Weight-down, today's item): once a human finally works an item free from under a dropped brick, the brick itself should stay behind as a plain loose `brick` `ItemKey` (throwable, binnable, dunkable like anything else) instead of just vanishing with the pickup — a resident's opportunistic fetch (the same one that already tidies stray items into the board/shelf system) would then eventually clear it like any other litter, so it doesn't sit there forever.

## Grows from Sell
- [ ] A resident notices what you sell (chain from Sell, wider): a `routine`-mode resident whose job item (`job.item`) matches the item you just sold, standing within ~150px, gets a one-off flat line about it ("that could've been someone's.") — same one-off-reaction shape the lamp/tree/booth props already use, no new state.

## Grows from Brace-with
- [ ] A human who waits out the link doesn't get a free re-roll (chain from Brace-with, today's item, wider — mirrors the 2026-09-23 fix to Holding-on's outlasted-brace rule): today the moment a linked pair's 14s window lapses, the very next `C` on either of them rolls a completely fresh steal (brace → hold, still capped at two by the holiday rule) even if the same human never left their side. If the same human is still within `GRAB_R` when a link ends, skip straight to the plain steal outcome once (same shape as the outlasted-brace fix) instead of handing back a full re-roll — waiting out a link should cost the same as waiting out a brace, not less.
- [ ] A passerby notices the between-them trick (chain from Brace-with, deferred until reputation lands): a `routine`-mode resident of either linked resident's job standing within ~150px who watches a human successfully wait out the linked-pair pickup delay could get a one-off flat line about it next time ("saw that.") — same deferred-reputation shape as the Interception and Holding-on chains above; note here until the reputation store from Player growth exists to back it with more than a line.
- [ ] A blocked throw gets its own flat line (chain from Brace-with, wider — deepens the 2026-09-26 polish fix that gave the link-absorb window parity with melee for thrown hits): today a thrown item absorbed by a linked pair reuses the plain `LINK_LINES` pool (same as a blocked punch/kick); give the thrown case one line of its own from a small new pool ("caught that one too.") so a human who switches from fists to thrown items after being blocked once hears something that acknowledges the different attempt, instead of the exact same line twice in a row.

## Grows from Squirrels
- [ ] A squirrel makes off with a dropped apple (chain from Squirrels, wider — pairs with the still-deferred "ducks steal a dropped fish" under Fishing): an `apple` sitting as a loose item within ~90px of a tree's squirrels for more than a few seconds gets carried off by one of them — same "owning client judges it, removes with the existing `pick`-style `ev`" shape ducks-stealing-fish/gulls already use, with a short "a squirrel got it." toast — the second animal (after gulls) to react to unattended food rather than to proximity or a prop hit.
- [ ] A squirrel that's just climbed drops down startled (chain from Squirrels, wider): today a squirrel mid-`climb` just keeps climbing until its 1.8s window runs out even if the shake ends early or a second shake restarts the timer indefinitely; give it a matching `drop` beat (reuse the still-unclaimed `flinch` off the motion library's Reactions list, scaled down for a critter) when the tree stops being shaken for more than a second while a squirrel is still up it, so climbing always resolves instead of only ever timing out.

- [ ] Sorted the right way (chain from Sort the scraps, today's item, wider): today every `sort`-flagged item gets the same generic line pool and just vanishes once a sweeper reaches it, regardless of what it actually is — split the outcome by kind instead: `FOOD` items keep vanishing (compost, the current behaviour is already right for these), but anything else has a chance to detour to the lost-and-found board instead of disappearing for good, the same way an unclaimed stray item already does — reuse the existing `n.mode = 'board'` path (pin-with-a-note) rather than inventing a second disposal outcome, so a binned wrench or hat has a chance of turning up on the board instead of just being gone.

## Grows from Keep-lock
- [ ] The lock reaches repair, rake and the board too (chain from Keep-lock, today's item, wider; folded in a town wish 2026-09-24 asking for the same reservation window on a freshly-pinned board item): today only a finished Mend request sets the `lockAt` timer (`SquareGame.tsx`) — the repairer's plain broken-prop fix, the rake's fished-out-of-the-water item, and a just-pinned board/crate item all resolve through their own drop but leave the result immediately grabbable. Route all three through the same `lockAt.current.set(id, now + 15000)` call the mend finish uses, so any resident's finished timed service (and a fresh pin) protects its result for the same 15s — the base mechanic was built generic on purpose, this just widens who calls it.
- [ ] A passerby notices someone waiting out a lock (chain from Keep-lock, deferred until reputation lands): a `routine`-mode resident within ~150px who watches a human hover near a locked item until it opens up could get a one-off flat line about it ("patient, aren't you.") — same deferred-reputation shape as the Interception/Holding-on/Brace-with chains above; note here until the reputation store from Player growth exists to back it with more than a line.
