## Climb grows too (owner, 2026-09-22 — the tower has had nothing new)
- [ ] Rest floors with life: every 5th band is a rest floor already; give it props from the town (a bench to `seat` on, a vending machine that sells a cup for coins, a bin, a lamp) and one or two residents doing what they do in Square (reading, on the phone, eating) with the same poses — the engine's props and jobs, not a Climb-only copy.
- [ ] Weather on the tower: the seeded daily weather (see Weather) reaches Climb — rain makes `ice` platforms out of `std` ones on some bands, wind gusts (the existing `windOf`) get stronger in a storm with a visible streak, snow above 300 m in winter months.
- [ ] Things to carry up: loose items on platforms (a hat, an umbrella) you can `C` to pick up and wear (the same wearing code as Square); carrying two things makes the charge jump 10% shorter; a resident on a rest floor asks for one of them (a quest that pays coins).
- [ ] Residents that climb: a couple of roster residents (jogger, courier, kid) actually climb the tower on a seeded schedule with the same jump physics as the player — you can see them charge, jump, miss and fall, and they say something flat when they land on you. Same `step()` code, no cheating.
- [ ] Hazards with motion: birds (from Animals) that cross a band and knock you sideways if you jump into them (`flap` critter already exists); a resident with a broom who sweeps a platform and pushes loose items — and you — off the edge if you stand still too long (`sweep` pose).
- [ ] Records that mean something: the best height already persists; add a plaque at the height of each day's best climber (their handle, their colour), visible to everyone at that band, and a small ceremony line from a resident when someone passes it.
- [ ] Windy bands brace you (thin axis: Climb, 2026-09-23 — reuses today's new pose): on a band where `windOf` rolls a strong gust, briefly force the climbing figure (the player, and residents once "Residents that climb" lands) into the new `brace` `FigPose` (added today, `stickman.ts`) — feet planted, no jump input read for ~0.4s — instead of only nudging position sideways the way wind does today; same idea as a gust that makes you dig in rather than just drift.
- [ ] A dropped item lands in the square below (thin axis: Climb, 2026-09-24 — the first time something that happens in one game shows up in the other, not just shared code): a loose item a climber lets go of, or drops on a fall, doesn't just vanish — write it into that player's `/api/goose` state (the same per-user persistence other cross-visit state already uses) as "waiting at the tower door"; the next time they (or, once "Residents that climb" lands, any resident) are in the square, it appears as a normal loose item there for anyone to take. Climb and Square run on separate rooms (`/ws/climb`, `/ws/square`), so this is a mailbox between visits, not a live sync — same tradeoff the patrol's own cross-system writes already accept.
- [ ] A personal-best cheer (thin axis: Climb, 2026-09-23 — the small, standalone half of the still-unbuilt "Records that mean something" plaque above): the moment a climber's height passes their own previous best (already persisted, `tower.ts`), a one-off flat line and a small burst of upward-drifting motes at the player's position — purely local, client-side celebration, no synced state and no new pose, unlike the shared plaque item which needs everyone to agree on the day's best.
- [ ] Idle fidgets on rest floors (thin axis: Climb, 2026-09-23 — the engine already knows how, Climb just never asks): a climber standing still on a rest floor between charges (the player, and residents once "Residents that climb" lands) cycles through the same seeded idle fidgets Square already uses (`yawn`/`stretch`/`look`/`check`/`shrug`/`sneeze`, `stickman.ts`) instead of a bare stand — same shared `(t + seed) % 22` clock check as Square's, no new state, since `figure()` already draws every one of these poses and Climb just never calls for them.
- [ ] Wind streaks, ahead of the storm variant (thin axis: Climb, 2026-09-24): `windOf` already varies gust strength per band (the value "Windy bands brace you" uses to force the `brace` pose) — before Weather brings a real storm to the tower, draw a light streak effect across bands with a strong gust (purely visual, keyed on the same seeded `windOf` value every client already computes, no new state) so a windy band reads as windy even between gust-brace moments — same de-risking approach Lamps-on-a-clock and Dawn-and-dusk light used for Night before the harder behaviour landed.
- [ ] A dropped item's landing puff at the base (thin axis: Climb, 2026-09-25): an item lost off the tower entirely — past band 0, so it misses "A dropped item lands in the square below" above's mailbox — just vanishes today; give it a small dust-puff sprite at the base line instead, the same one-off local-motes trick "A personal-best cheer" already uses for its own celebration, purely visual and client-local, no synced state.
- [ ] A landing thud puff for ordinary falls (thin axis: Climb, 2026-09-26 polish run — the small, standalone half "A dropped item's landing puff" and "A personal-best cheer" already established for other moments, most rotation-overdue axis alongside quests this run): today only a fall hard enough to pass `SPLAT` gets any landing feedback at all (the `hurt` squash-and-freeze). An ordinary landing after real airtime (say, `apex - y` past a small threshold but under `SPLAT`) gets nothing. Give it the same small local dust-puff sprite those two items already use — purely visual, client-local, no synced state — so landing reads as landing even when it isn't hard enough to hurt.
- [ ] Breath in the cold air, high up (thin axis: Climb, 2026-09-26 polish run — a further purely-visual de-risking piece, same family as Wind streaks and Lamps-on-a-clock): above some fixed band (say 200), draw a small fading vapor puff near the climbing figure's head on a slow clock-only cycle (a few seconds per breath) — purely local, no synced state, seeded only by band height and the wall clock, ahead of Weather-on-the-tower's harder snow branch.

- [ ] Town-proposed houses: allow `house` in the patrol's proposal kinds (`patrol/square-grow.mjs` KINDS — that file is the patrol's, so the game side must accept `house` spots from `site_meta.square_content` first). For every house spot that is not one of the three originals, the game generates an interior map (`houseInterior` with a seed from the spot key: room width, which furniture exists and where, wall colour) and an owner (a roster resident who owns no house yet, chosen by seed), adds a door exit both ways, and draws the exterior with the seeded variety from "House exteriors vary". The owner's routine gains the home stop like the original owners. Then tell the patrol it may propose houses by adding `house` to KINDS in `square-grow.mjs` (one line — allowed for this item only, note it in the log).

## Grows from A clinic on Market street
(2026-09-27, this run — three more town wishes arrived for the same subject: a dojo/Sensei's forward-roll recovery, a physiotherapy clinic's limp-gait rehab, and a bouldering gym's "recover from high knocks." All three are the bathhouse/orthotics-workshop shape below wearing different dressing — folded here with no new lines; the bouldering gym is additionally blocked on the same not-yet-existing river path the new "Skill buildings" system above needs.)
- [ ] Residents route past the clinic door on their own after a shove (town wish, 2026-09-27, deferred from the clinic item above — this run built the `C`-assist and the nurse's own opportunistic heal, but not the detour half): once a resident's `down` timer expires (dust → return, `SquareGame.tsx`), give them a seeded chance to splice a `clinic` stop onto the front of their next routine leg before resuming their normal stops — same seeded-detour shape the opportunistic-fetch/repair/rake checks already use, no new pose. Widens who ends up near the clinic instead of relying on wherever a shove happened to land someone.
- [ ] A bathhouse and masseur job (town wish, 2026-09-27, merged here — same subject as the clinic above, a service building + job where a resident recovers from being hurt, just a different spot and trigger: chilled/bruised rather than freshly knocked down): a `bathhouse` building spot (`world.ts`, same shape as `clinic`); a `masseur` job stationed there. A resident who was recently knocked down or dunked drifts there on their routine (same seeded-detour shape the clinic's own routed-detour note already uses) to sit and stretch (reuse the existing `stretch` idle-fidget pose) until an internal cooldown clears — the wish's own "fatigue meter" must stay an unshown internal timer, never a displayed number, per the owner's no-progression-bar rule. Same `C`-assist shape the clinic already establishes: a player can speed a resident's stay along the same way the clinic's heal is sped along.
- [ ] An orthotics workshop and orthotist job (town wish, 2026-09-27, merged here — the third slice of this family): an `orthotics` building spot (`world.ts`); an `orthotist` job. A short timed fitting there (reuse the `fix` pose, a service action on a person instead of a prop) unlocks a selectable limp gait for whoever was fitted — a new slow, uneven `FigPose` used only in the moments right after a knockdown, not a permanent handicap — and a faster recovery from a shove after that. A per-resident/per-player unlock in the "Player growth" sense (no number shown), not a stat; needs the clinic's own knockdown-recovery plumbing to exist first, so build it last of the three.

- [ ] A footbridge at the pond (thin axis: places, 2026-09-23 — the first place item that's a piece of terrain rather than a building or a whole map, per the axis's own definition): a short bridge prop spanning the little pond's narrow end in the park, drawn as a walkable strip a few px above the water instead of a `Spot` you interact with, connecting the two banks directly; anyone shoved while standing on it goes into the pond the same way a shove near the open water edge already can. Small, no new machinery — just a prop kind that changes where you can stand.
- [ ] A wooden dock on the little pond (thin axis: places, 2026-09-24 — a second terrain piece alongside the footbridge and the church steps, per the axis's own "terrain" note; hand-counted places as the thinnest of the eleven axes GROW.md names, tied with jobs): a short walkable jetty jutting a few steps out over the little pond in the park, reached by a short jump like the church steps — gives the still-unbuilt rowboat (see Vehicles) somewhere to be tied up and the still-unbuilt fishing pier idea a nearer cousin, without building either of those yet. `retired` sometimes stands at the end of it instead of the bank on its pond-side routine stop (reuse the existing `stand` pose, no new one needed).
- [ ] A maintenance yard on Market street (thin axis: places, 2026-09-24 — hand-counted places as the thinnest axis again; also the concrete spot the merged Pegboard hang-tool wish under Keep-lock above is waiting on): a small fenced side-yard off Market street — a shed wall, a pegboard, a bin — gives the `repairer` job a home base to route through on its own routine (reuse the existing `stand`/`sweep` acts, no new pose) instead of only ever passing through on the way to whatever just broke.
- [ ] A library on Market street (thin axis: places, 2026-09-25 — hand-counted places and quests the two thinnest again; gives the still-generic "library" in Building kinds an actual spot, the same way the clinic item gave "clinic" one): a new `library` building spot (`world.ts`, same shape as `bakery`/`post`/`clinic`) between the post office and the church; `C` near it with empty hands borrows the shelf's book the same way a house's bookshelf already lends a `read` pose, but taking one here also completes a `library1` task the way `shelf1` (Relay and shelving) does — a librarian job isn't required for the first slice, `priest`/`retired` already route past on `steps1`/`church` and can be given this stop later.
- [ ] A newsstand on Market street (thin axis: places, 2026-09-25 — places among the thinnest again): a new `newsstand` prop (`world.ts`, same shape as `bakery`/`library`) between the post office and the police station; `C` with empty hands takes a `paper` (existing `ItemKey`, today only ever starts out already carried by `postie`) the same free-lend way the library's shelf above hands out a book — no coin, no new pose, just an actual place to pick one up instead of it only ever spawning pre-carried.
- [ ] A marked crossing between Market street and the square (thin axis: places, 2026-09-26 — full-counted places at 8 open items, still one of the lowest alongside jobs): a painted street-crossing terrain strip at the street/square boundary, drawn on the ground like the church steps (no new pose, no physics change) — gives the still-open crossing-guard job (above, "Every prop does something") an actual marked spot to stand at instead of just "where the street path meets the square," the same terrain-not-building shape the footbridge and dock already are.
- [ ] A footpath directly between Market street and the park (thin axis: places, 2026-09-26 — hand-counted places the thinnest axis this run at ~9 open items, last topped up at run 49): today the only way from the park to Market street is through the square (`park`→`square`→`street`, two exits each way) — add a second, direct `Exit` pair connecting `street` and `park` (a narrow footpath terrain strip, same drawn-on-the-ground shape the marked crossing/church steps/footbridge already use, no new building or `PropKind`), so the town's own map graph gets an actual shortcut instead of only ever a hub-and-spoke through the square. The first place-axis item that changes how the maps connect rather than adding a spot inside one of them.
- [ ] A river path (thin axis: places, 2026-09-27 — places and motions hand-counted the two thinnest axes this run; also unblocks two already-written "Skill buildings" slices above, the boathouse and the river dance pavilion, both of which named this exact place before it existed): a new outdoor map `riverpath` (connect: park, the way `marketalley` connects to street), a narrow bank-side strip with a dock terrain piece (same drawn shape the footbridge/dock above use) — the town's fourth outdoor map, no props of its own required to be useful on day one, since the boathouse/dance-pavilion items above are what give it a reason to exist.
- [ ] A greenhouse behind the garden (thin axis: places, 2026-09-28 — jobs and places the two most rotation-overdue axes, untouched since run 56/run 61 respectively): a new `greenhouse` building spot next to the square's garden bed (`world.ts`, same small-structure-with-a-door shape `clinic`/`yogastudio` already use, no new map needed) — gives the still-generic "greenhouse" in Building kinds (below, "Quests grow with the town") an actual spot the way the clinic/library/newsstand items already did for theirs. `gardener` gains it as a routine stop (reuse the existing `water`/`stand` acts, no new pose), so the reverse rule needs no new code.

## Grows from Steps
- [ ] Wedding guests line the steps (chain from Steps, today's item, wider — ties into the still-unbuilt Relationships wedding): once the monthly church wedding lands (see Relationships), guests gather and sit on `steps1` before the ceremony (reuses the sittable spot `steps1` already added, no new prop) instead of everyone just spawning at the door — gives the procession an actual assembly point.

## Grows from Bus stop
- [ ] Shelter from the rain once Weather lands (chain from Bus stop, today's item): `busstop1` is the covered spot Weather's own rain plan already names ("their own door or the bus stop") — once the seeded daily Weather system ships, a resident without an umbrella caught in rain should route to the nearest door or `busstop` and wait it out (reuse the existing `stand`/`sit` pose, no new one), and a player standing under it in rain gets the same "no slowdown, no wet line" break an open umbrella gives. Note here until Weather exists to trigger it.

## Beyond the square (owner, 2026-09-22 — later: a farm, a tractor, digging into the mountain, many animals, builders)
- [ ] Vehicles: a tractor at the fields (see Fields) that the farmer drives along the rows (`drive` pose: seated, hands on the wheel, the tractor a drawn prop that moves; residents step out of its way, ducks scatter); a player can `C` to climb on when it is parked and drive it slowly (arrows), which the farmer objects to and chases. Later the same code gives the street a delivery van for the courier and a police car that arrives when the cop is called.
- [ ] A bike rack (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-22): a new prop by the market street and the park gate; `courier` and `jogger` take a bike from it at the start of their routine and ride between stops noticeably faster than walking (same `run` cadence but with a drawn bike prop under the figure, no new pose needed — the bike is carried like an item, not a body change). Players can take a bike the same way empty-handed residents do (`C` at the rack) and move faster until they park it back at any rack or drop it; a dropped bike is a loose prop others can also take. First vehicle a player can actually ride day one, ahead of the tractor.
- [ ] A luggage trolley for the courier (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-26 — most rotation-overdue axis after relationships-and-children, untouched since the 2026-09-24 polish's gardener's cart; deliberately not another item waiting on the still-unbuilt `push`/`pull`/`drive` poses, which the wheelbarrow/gardener's cart/pull-wagon/tractor/van/car/moped items above are all already stuck behind): a small two-wheel trolley `courier` straps their basket to on its `post`↔houses↔`bakery`↔`cafe` route, drawn trailing a step behind the figure the same "no body-pose change, just a prop under/behind the figure" way the bike rack handles riding — reuses the existing carry stance, so it needs no new `FigPose`, and raises `courier`'s effective stack cap by one while it's attached. Parked and empty at the post office, a player can `C` it to strap on and unstrap the same way, giving vehicles axis a second day-one item that isn't blocked on the missing push/pull pose.
- [ ] A produce handcart for the grocer (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-27 — most rotation-overdue axis alongside motions, untouched since the 2026-09-26 polish's luggage trolley; also ties into the still-open "The grocer collects" chain under Grows from Tree, which already wants the grocer detouring for loose apples): `grocer` pulls a small crate-laden handcart between the market stall and wherever a stray `apple`/stall item needs collecting, drawn trailing a step behind the figure the exact "no steering, no new `FigPose`, a prop under/behind the figure" trick the gardener's cart and luggage trolley already use. Parked and empty at the stall, a player with empty hands can `C` it to pull it themselves (same speed-not-control handling those two already specify) — a third vehicles-axis item that needs no push/pull/drive pose to land.
- [ ] A rowboat on the pond (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-22): a boat prop tied up at the little pond; `C` with empty hands boards it (new `row` `FigPose`: seated, oars pulling), drifts a slow seeded loop away from the bank and back, ducks give it a wider berth than a swimmer. `C` again gets out at the near bank. `retired` (already the pond-side job via the rod rack) takes the boat out on some stops instead of fishing, same pose, so the water is not only a fishing spot. No steering — the loop is the ride, same spirit as the bike being speed-not-control.
- [ ] A police car at the station (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-22): a parked car prop by the police station; when the cop job is summoned to chase (see Temperaments — call the police) and the target is more than ~400px away, the cop gets in (`drive` pose, seated, hands on the wheel — same pose the tractor item wants, first thing to actually use it) and drives over at `RUN` speed along the street's lane instead of walking, a short honk line as they arrive, then gets out to chase on foot like today. When it is parked and empty, a player can `C` to take it for a slow joyride the same way the tractor works (open world, nothing locked) — the cop who owns it notices, chases on foot, and the fine (see station/money items) is worse than any other theft, which is the deterrent rather than a lock.
- [ ] A delivery van for the courier (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-23): a parked van prop by the post office; `courier` drives it (new `drive` `FigPose` — none of today's other vehicle items have landed yet either, so this is the one that actually needs it first: seated, hands on the wheel) between post/bakery/houses on its routine instead of walking, stepping residents and scattering ducks out of the lane like the tractor wants to. Parked and empty, a player can `C` to climb in and joyride it slowly (open world, nothing locked, same spirit as the bike/tractor); the courier notices it is gone, chases on foot, and is short with you for the rest of the day.
- [ ] A wheelbarrow at the garden and building site (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-23 — doubles as the still-unbuilt `push` motion from the library): `gardener` (between garden beds) and, once Builders lands, builders (between the plank pile and the frame) push a loaded wheelbarrow instead of carrying by hand (new `push` `FigPose`, reused from the motion library's Work list rather than invented fresh); a player with empty hands can `C` an idle wheelbarrow to grab its handles and push it themselves (same `push` pose, slower than walking, no punch/kick while pushing, `C` again lets go where it stands) — a small utility vehicle, not a rideable one, so it needs no joyride/theft handling.
- [ ] A skateboard by the playground (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-23 — the small, kid-sized companion to the bike rack, and the first vehicle inside the park rather than the street or pond): a `skateboard` prop propped against the swings; `kid` residents ride it between playground stops on their routine — same `run` cadence with a drawn board under the feet, no new pose needed (the same trick the bike rack item already wants for `courier`/`jogger`). Players with empty hands can `C` it to ride (same speed-not-control handling the bike rack specifies: faster than walking, drop it anywhere with `C`, a dropped board is a loose prop anyone — human or kid — can take next).
- [ ] A moped for the postie (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-23 — postie already routes between the post office and all three houses, the natural first rider): a parked moped prop by the post office; `postie` rides it (new `drive` `FigPose` — none of today's other vehicle items have landed yet either, so this is just another job waiting on the same pose, seated, hands on the bars) between stops instead of walking, faster like the courier's van. Parked and empty, a player can `C` to take it for a slow joyride the same way the tractor/van work (open world, nothing locked); the postie notices it is gone, chases on foot, and is short with you about it for the rest of the day.
- [ ] A gardener's cart (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-24 — ties into today's polish fix that finally gave `gardener` real houseplant stops, `world.ts`): `gardener` now pulls a small two-bucket cart between `garden`/`garden2` and the three houseplant stops instead of carrying nothing extra between them — drawn as a prop trailing a step behind on the ground (no steering, same speed-not-control spirit as the bike), shares the still-unbuilt `push`/`pull` `FigPose` the wheelbarrow item above already wants rather than inventing a second one. Parked and idle, a player with empty hands can `C` it to pull it themselves between garden spots (slower than walking, `C` again lets go); watering from a full cart doubles the `water` action's puddle-sparkle duration for a few uses before it needs refilling at a garden.
- [ ] A kid's pull-wagon in the park (thin axis: vehicles, 2026-09-24): a small wagon prop by the swings; `kid` residents pull it between playground stops on their routine (one more job waiting on the still-unbuilt `push`/`pull` `FigPose` the wheelbarrow and gardener's cart items above already want) with a stuffed animal or ball riding loose inside that a player can take with `C` once the wagon is parked and empty — the passenger, not the wagon, is what changes hands. Parking it (`C` again) leaves it for the next kid or player to pull. First vehicle inside the play area itself rather than the street, pond or garden.
- [ ] The mountain: a new outdoor map beyond the fields with a slope (the 2.5D stage gets a ground line that rises), a path with switchbacks, a quarry face and a mine mouth. A `miner` job digs at the face (`dig` pose, pickaxe, a chip of rock each swing) and carries ore to a cart; players can dig too (`C` with empty hands) and find things (a seeded loot table: stone, a coin, a fossil that becomes a plaque in the square). Climbing the slope uses the tower's jump physics on the rising ground. Rockfalls on the path when it rains (Weather) that block it until a builder clears them.
- [ ] Animals, many: after ducks — pigeons, a cat, dogs (dogwalker), chickens at the farm (they follow whoever carries a sandwich), goats on the mountain (they butt you off the path), fish that jump in the pond at dusk, bees over fresh flowers, crows on the quarry. Every animal is a `critter` kind with two or three poses and one reaction to players, and one resident job that tends it.

## The motion library (owner, 2026-09-22 — the stick figure must have an enormous range; one per run when nothing else needs one)
- [ ] Reactions: `slip` (feet forward, arms up, lands on the back), `flee` (run with the head turned back), `flinch` (shoulders up, arms in), `brace` (feet planted, leaning against a pull), `shrug`, `wave`, `point`, `laugh` (head back, shaking), `cry` (hands to face), `sneeze`, `yawn`, `stretch`, `shiver` (cold), `fan` (hot, hand fanning the face).
- [ ] Work: `dig`, `drive`, `carry` (heavy, two hands low, slow gait), `push` and `pull` (a cart, the roundabout), `ladder` (climbing rungs), `hammer` (builder), `paint` (painter at an easel), `sweepwide` (long broom strokes), `serve` (tray), `cook` (pan flip), `rake`, `plant` (kneel, press soil), `harvest` (bend and pull).
- [ ] Play and rest: `slide`, `seesaw` (both ends), `spin` (on the roundabout, arms out), `wade`, `swim` (in the pond, if you fall in), `dance` (two variants — the solo one shipped 2026-09-27 with the community dance hall, "Skill buildings"; the partnered one is "Grows from Community dance hall" above), `cheer`, `clap`, `nap` (on a bench, different from the bed), `lean` (against a lamp or wall), `crouch`, `tiptoe`.
- [ ] Together: `hold` (walking hand in hand, two figures synced), `hug`, `kiss` (cheek), `argue` (facing, arms out), `handshake`, `highfive`, `carrychild` (a small figure on the shoulders).
- [ ] Transitions and idles: sit-down and stand-up in-betweens, a reach before taking, a bend before picking up, a turn step, three seeded idle fidgets while standing (weight shift, look around, check the phone), and a startle when something flies past.
Each pose is one `if (pose === '…')` block in `stickman.ts` with a Korean comment on the shape, ≤ 6 characters in the name (the room truncates), and at least one place in Square where a resident and a player both use it.
- [ ] A startle when something flies past (thin axis: motions, 2026-09-23 — closes the last unclaimed line in "Transitions and idles" above): a resident (or the player) who is *not* the target of a thrown item, but has one pass within ~40px of them without hitting, flinches — new `flinch` `FigPose` (shoulders up, arms drawn in, ~0.3s, from the still-unclaimed Reactions list too) instead of no reaction at all, the way a near-miss reads as nothing today. Judged the same way ducks scattering from a landed throw already is: client-local, off state (`thrown`/position) that is already synced, no new `ev` kind. Purely reactive for residents; the player side is the same check run against the player's own position, so both get it for free from one roll site.
- [ ] A point before setting off (thin axis: motions, 2026-09-23 — next unclaimed pose off the Reactions list above, `point`): the moment a resident's opportunistic fetch/repair/rake roll picks a target (`n.mode` flips from `routine` to `fetch`/`repair`/`rake` in the main loop, `SquareGame.tsx`) they briefly face the target and hold a new `point` `FigPose` (arm extended, forefinger out, ~0.4s) before setting off, instead of just turning and walking — a small "I see it, I'm going" beat that reads the same for a stray item, a broken prop or a dunked one. Purely reactive, no player mirror needed (the player has nothing that maps to "just noticed a fetch target").
- [ ] A cry at the third knockdown (thin axis: motions, 2026-09-24 — next unclaimed pose off the Reactions list above, `cry`): a small per-resident counter (`Npc.downCount`) increments each time `hit()`'s knockdown branch lands on them; the third knockdown in one visit (reset when they make it back to `routine`) plays a new `cry` `FigPose` (hands to face, ~0.5s) instead of the usual shoved line, from its own small line pool ("enough. just — enough."). Purely reactive, no player mirror needed (reverse-rule carve-out, same as Brace/Dust-off/Tether).
- [ ] A bend before picking up (thin axis: motions, 2026-09-25 — hand-counted motions the thinnest axis again; closes the "a reach before taking, a bend before picking up" line in "Transitions and idles" above): every `C` pickup in the game (a loose item, a steal, rummaging a bin, fishing a bite, taking from the board/shelf) snaps straight to holding with no transition — add a short (~0.2s) `bend` `FigPose` (knees flex, one arm reaching down) that plays for the split second before the item actually joins the stack, for both the player and any resident whose own fetch/steal/rummage roll just landed. Purely a wind-up on an action that already exists everywhere, so one pose reaches the largest number of moments of anything left on the Reactions/Transitions lists.
- [ ] A handshake when a delivery lands (thin axis: motions, 2026-09-25 — hand-counted motions the thinnest axis again, still 6 open items even after today's `fan` closed the last unclaimed generic Reactions-list idle fidget; next unclaimed pose off the "Together" list, `handshake`): the moment a `deliver`/`crate`/`shelf`/`reunite`-shaped task or quest actually hands an item to the asking resident, they trade a quick `handshake` `FigPose` (both arms out, a beat, back down) instead of just a thanks line — one pose reused by every existing hand-something-over completion (`complete('deliver:...')`, the board/shelf take, `reunite` once it ships), not a new mechanic. Purely reactive, no player-only half needed (both sides already meet at the same spot for the handoff).
- [ ] A nap on a bench (thin axis: motions, 2026-09-25 — motions thinnest again even after today's `laugh`; next unclaimed pose off the "Play and rest" list, `nap`): sitting still on a `bench`-kind spot specifically (not swing/sofa/bed, which already have their own seated feel) for a stretch (~15s, same "been still this long" shape the idle-fidget clock already proves out) drifts the seated `sit` pose into a new `nap` `FigPose` (head tilted onto one shoulder, slow rise-and-fall breathing) instead of holding the same upright seated frame forever; any move (or getting hit) snaps back to plain sitting or standing. Both the player and any resident whose routine stop is a bench long enough get it automatically once the timer is shared, so the reverse rule needs no separate wiring — same shape as the existing 22-second idle-fidget cycle, just gated on "how long at a bench" instead of "how long standing."
- [ ] A turn step (thin axis: motions, 2026-09-27 — most rotation-overdue axis, untouched since run 48's `handshake`; closes the "a turn step" line in Transitions and idles, the last unclaimed line on that list besides `slip`/`flee`, both still tied to their own bigger systems): a brief `turn` `FigPose` (~0.15s — weight shifts across, feet cross-step before the run cycle catches the new facing) plays whenever a moving figure's `face` flips from one direction to the other, instead of snapping instantly to the new heading — for the player and every resident alike, since it is judged off the same `face`/`moving` fields both already carry every frame. No new mechanic, no new state beyond noticing `face` changed since last frame; reaches every direction change in the game for one pose.

## Talking to residents means something (owner, 2026-09-22 — interaction and quests must deepen)
- [ ] Small talk with substance: `E` on a resident with no open ask gives a line drawn from who they are — their latest post's title paraphrased ("wrote something about the bus fares yesterday. nobody read it."), their job's mood, the weather, what a player did to them earlier today (they remember the last three knocks by handle) — and a small link chip under the bubble to that post (`/p/<id>`) so the square sends people into the forum. `ResidentLite.line` already carries the latest post title; extend it with the post id. Residents say these things to each other too when they pass (a two-line exchange, seeded).
- [ ] Asks that read like the resident: quest ask/thanks lines come from the job voice and, for residents with a persona line, from that; a baker asks for flour-adjacent things, the priest asks you to sit through a sermon, the kid asks for a push on the swing. Refusing an ask (walking away) is remembered for the day.
- [ ] Standing shown, not told: after reputation lands, the residents' greetings change first ("you again" / "morning" / silence and a step back), before any number is shown anywhere.

## Buildings that give roles (owner, 2026-09-22)
- [ ] Take a job: at the town hall (new building on the street; the mayor works there) `C` at the desk shows the day's openings — deputy at the police station, apprentice at the bakery, park keeper, sweeper, lifeguard at the pond, postie's helper. Taking one gives you the job's item and a small uniform mark (drawn on the figure, sent in `pos.s`), a duty list for the day (real ones: fine two people, sell five loaves, water every bed, clear every bin, pull three things from the fountain), pay in coins at the end, and standing with that job. While on duty the residents of that job treat you as one of them (they greet, they back you up in a chase); knock someone over while wearing the badge and you are fired on the spot. Residents also change jobs at the town hall on a seeded rota, so the roster shifts week to week.
- [ ] Other role buildings: the church (an usher role on Sundays), the school (a teacher for the kids, once there are kids), the clinic (a nurse who patches up residents knocked down — a `heal` interaction that shortens their `down` time), the fire station (later, with a fire). Each building = one role with three duties and one uniform mark.

## Money that means something (owner, 2026-09-22 — part-time jobs; being rich opens places; places to spend)
- [ ] Part-time work, right now: quick paid tasks you take on the spot without the town hall — carry crates for the grocer (three trips, coins per crate), hand out flyers for the busker, walk the dogwalker's dog for a lap of the park, hold the ladder for the builder, fish two things out of the fountain for the sweeper. Each is a resident's ask with a coin figure in it, a 2–3 minute loop with its own motions, and a payout line. Residents do the same jobs for each other on their routines.
- [ ] Places to spend: the café and the diner (see their items), the stall (hats, glasses, umbrellas, a better rod, a basket that carries more), the bakery, a barber (a new hair line on the figure, drawn from `pos.s`), a tailor (a colour band or a scarf on the figure), the post office (send an item to a resident's house), the church (a donation that raises standing with everyone), the cinema when the town builds one (sit in the dark with residents for a minute; a line about the film). Prices in coins, receipts as a toast, and the thing you bought visible on you.
- [ ] Money changes what you can *use*, never what you can *see or enter* (the town is open world — no locked doors, no doormen, no hidden maps): the boat sits on the pond for anyone to look at, but rowing it costs a rental (`row` pose); a house for sale has its door open and anyone walks through, but only the buyer's bed saves their spot and only the buyer furnishes it from the stall; the diner's back table seats anyone, but ordering there costs more and the waiter treats you differently. None of it is a level; it is more to do for people who have done more. Residents notice ("nice hat. expensive.") and the police fine rich players more.

## Umbrellas and the rest of the pocket (owner, 2026-09-23 — "what about using things, like putting up an umbrella")
- [ ] Umbrella: `C` with an umbrella and nothing around opens it — an `umbr` pose variant for stand/run/seat (the canopy drawn above the head, the arm up), it stays open while carried, `C` again closes it; in rain (Weather) an open umbrella keeps you dry (no slowdown, no wet line) and residents who carry one open it the moment rain starts and close it when it stops. Do this in the same run as Weather or the run right after, never before — an umbrella with no rain is a prop, not an interaction.
- [ ] Phone: `C` with a phone holds it up (`phone` pose), a resident within 120px who is on the phone too gets a "same call" line; keys: `C` at your own door (once houses can be owned) locks it; newspaper: `C` reads it standing (`read` pose, a headline from the day's top post above the head); broom: `C` sweeps loose items within 60px into a pile at your feet (three strokes, `sweep` pose) and the pile is one loose stack; basket: carrying it raises the stack limit from 3 to 5, drawn as the basket in hand.

## Fishing, later, as its own place (owner, 2026-09-23 — ponds are for the ducks; fishing was removed from the square)
- [ ] Fishing lives at a **fishing spot**, not in the town square: a river path or lake map (the town may propose it; otherwise add `riverside`, connect: park) with a wooden pier and two or three **rod stands** — a rod already set in a holder, a stool beside it, a bucket. No rod in the pocket, ever. `C` at a rod stand *starts fishing right there*: `cast` (whip forward, the float flies and lands with a ring) → `angle` (sit, float bobbing, idle fidgets) → `bite` (float dips, rod bends, `!`, 0.6 s to press `C`) → `reel` (crank, rod pulsing, 1.5 s) → `land` (fish held up, its name from `pond-items` above the head, then it is a loose `fish` item you can carry, give or bin) or `miss` / `snag`. Leaving the stand (any move) puts the rod back. Residents (retired, priest, jogger on a rest day) fish at the same stands with the same sequence and seeded outcomes; two people cannot share a stand. Task `catch a fish at the pier`, ask `bring me something from the river`. Everything visible to others (pose over the socket, the catch as an `ev`).

## Money, pocket, shops (owner, 2026-09-23 — "the diner, currency, inventory, all of that")
- [ ] Pocket panel: `I` (and a touch button) opens a small panel under the canvas — what you carry (up to 3, or 5 with a basket), what you wear, your coins, and today's earnings; items can be reordered (which one is on top matters for `C`) and dropped from the panel. Residents' pockets show when you `E` them ("carrying: a cup").
- [ ] Prices everywhere money changes hands: the stall, café, bakery and diner show a price above the counter, paying is a toast with the coins left, "no coins" is a resident line, and every paid thing appears in the pocket. Earnings: tasks, asks, part-time jobs (see Money that means something), a found coin at the fountain now and then.
- [ ] The diner first (owner priority): build the restaurant item above before the café loop if the café is not done yet; share the counter/queue/serve code between the two.

## Residents grow too — a house of their own, kept up (owner, 2026-09-23)
- [ ] Resident wallets and savings: every resident earns coins on their routine (a shift at their job = a few coins, seeded per day, persisted by the patrol in `site_meta.town_ledger`), spends at the café/stall, and saves. Show it nowhere as a number; show it as what they own.
- [ ] Buying a house: when a resident's savings pass a threshold and a plot is free (the town proposes plots as `plot` spots; the builders raise a house on one — see Builders), the resident buys it: the house gets that owner, the seeded exterior/interior from "House exteriors vary" and "Furnishing by owner", and the resident's routine gains the home stop. Over weeks more residents own homes; the roster's owners list grows; the map fills in. Population: Zero becomes a town of homeowners, not three.
- [ ] Keeping a house: owners sweep the step (`sweep`), water the window box, fix a broken window (after Wrecking), repaint (the painter job does it for coins), and add a room when rich (a second storey appears with scaffolding first). A neglected house (owner away, no coins) shows it: peeling paint, a dark window, weeds — and the sweeper complains about it. Players can help (carry a plank, water the box) and are remembered for it, or hurt it.
- [ ] Losing and moving: a wrecked house that is not rebuilt, or an owner who cannot pay, means the resident moves out — to the alley for a while (a bench and a bag), and the house goes back on the market. Nothing is permanent; the town churns.

## Game (Godot) — first slices (owner pivot, 2026-09-28; read game/README.md; build these before any web item)
- [ ] Residents as NPCs: load the town's residents (handles, jobs) from a JSON snapshot under `game/data/residents.json` (export it by hand from `site` this once; a fetch from `/api/residents` comes later), draw them with `Figure` at routine spots, `stand`/`run` between spots on a seeded clock like `goose.ts` `routineAt` — same seed, same wall-clock buckets, so the web Square and the game show the same resident in the same place at the same time.
- [ ] The first map: the square as data (`game/data/maps/square.json` mirroring `world.ts` MAPS.square: width, floor colours, spots with kind/x/d/name) and a `Prop` node that draws each `PropKind` procedurally (bench, lamp, tree, fountain, stall, booth, bin, garden, house facade) — vector, paper-and-ink; no sprites.
- [ ] Touch controls: on phone (landscape first), a left/right/up/down pad and three buttons (jump, hit, act) as `TouchScreenButton`s; the legend line hides on touch.
- [ ] Web export in CI: add `export_presets.cfg` (Web, GL Compatibility, threads off), a `build-game-web` job that downloads the export templates and writes `export/web/`, and publish it under the site's `/play/poz` as the demo (static files via the assets CDN; no Worker code).
- [ ] Multiplayer over the existing room: `WebSocketMultiplayerPeer` to `wss://population.town/ws/room?room=square` with a session token; positions and poses relayed like the web (`pos`/`ev`), other players drawn as `Figure`s with names; logged-out viewers spectate.
- [ ] Bots that fill the seats: when a player invites a friend and the slot is still empty after 20s, a resident joins *as a player* (moves with the same controller, same rules, dry manners) — the StarCraft computer player. First bot behaviour: wander, sit, pick up, avoid being hit.
- [ ] Assets folder and one SVG set: `game/assets/svg/` with the first vector props (bench, lamp, tree, bin) as SVG files imported as textures, matched to the procedural drawings so either can be used; document the size grid (1 unit = 1px at d=1).
- [ ] Climb inside the game: the tower as a second scene using the same `Player` physics (`tower.ts` constants), seeded platforms from `band()`, shared seed `0x505a`.
- [ ] The tower starts on the terrace (Climb axis, run 73 — Climb had exactly one `game/` line and no door in the town): a `climb` sign post on the lookout terrace's east end (a paper plank on an ink post, the town's highest ground is where the tower begins) registered as a spot; `C` at it loads `scenes/tower.tscn` — build that scene in the same run as the smallest playable slice of the line above: 40 platforms from `band()` with seed `0x505a`, widths and gaps in metres at 42px = 1m so the web tower and the game tower agree, the town's `Stick3D` with the town's hop (`JUMP_FULL`/`JUMP_HOLD`) and the `charge` pose (2D exists) for a held jump, a camera that only follows upward, exit by `C` on the bottom platform (returns to the terrace at the sign). No score, no rank: the highest platform reached is a chalk mark on the sign post (`Label3D`, "Reached 17."), and a resident whose routine picks the sign reads it (`read` pose) and says the number.
- [ ] A jump chain up the terrace's rock face (Climb axis, run 78 — Climb still has one `game/` line and no jumping anywhere in the town): three stone ledges on the terrace's north face (x 15.5/17/18.5, y 0.5/0.9/1.3, 0.8×0.15×0.5 boxes, `_terrace`), staggered like `tower.ts` `band()` platforms and spaced so `JUMP_FULL` (1.25m) clears each one only with the hold timed — a second way up the terrace for someone who can time hops, the steps stay the plain way; a `hop` landing pose (a 0.12s knee dip and both arms swung forward, its own timing, never the run cycle) on each ledge; residents take it too: one seeded resident (`uid % 24 == 11`) hops the chain instead of the steps when their next spot is the lookout, with the player's arc and landing squat (`crossings()` inserts the three ledges as waypoints with `act: "hop"`), and says "Shorter this way." at the top.
- [ ] Crate steps onto the parked truck (Climb axis, run 82 — the only jumping in the town is still the terrace line above, unbuilt): three wooden crates stacked as steps (0.45m, 0.9m, 1.35m tops) beside the parked truck behind the market (`_car("truck", (26, 0, -9.5))`), solid boxes the existing jump reaches one at a time, so the truck's cargo bed becomes a lookout you climb to; residents: the `driver` whose route passes stops once a morning and plays the existing `carry` pose moving the top crate to the bed and back, and a crate kicked (`_strike` within 0.8m) slides 0.3m off its stack so the steps have to be walked around until the driver restacks it.
- [ ] Skip the wide stones (Climb axis, run 84 — the stepping stones are the town's first place where a jump is a choice, and Climb still has no built `game/` line): a second line of four stones 1.5m downstream of the walking ones (x 24.5) set 1.0m apart so only a full `JUMP_FULL` hop lands them; a chalk tally post on the south landing counts the hops made without a splash and the player's best is said on arrival ("Four."); residents on a clear afternoon take the wide line one crossing in five with the same Jump3D arc and landing squat, and a miss is a swim to the bank — never a stun, never a lost item.

## Assets — make them diverse (owner, 2026-09-28; pick these before anything else; one theme per run, 6–12 entries in game/tools/assets.mjs)
- [ ] Farm set: barn facade, tractor, hay bale, wooden fence long, trough, chicken, cow, sheep, scarecrow, a vegetable row.
- [ ] House variants: a `houseVariant(seed)` helper in the generator that composes roof shape (gable/hip/flat), wall colour (paper, sand, sky, stone, brick), door colour (wood/accent), window rows and a chimney or balcony — emit 8 named variants (`house-01`…`house-08`) so the town's houses are never twins.
- [ ] Interiors: bed, sofa, tv on a stand, bookshelf, kitchen counter with a kettle, dining table with two chairs, rug, wall clock, a window with curtains (all under 120px so a room reads at 960 wide).
- [ ] Market goods and shop signs: bread basket, fish on ice, fruit crate, flower bucket, hanging sign blanks (round, shield, board) and five painted signs (bakery, fish, post, café, clinic) using shapes only, no text.
- [ ] Weather layer: rain drop, puddle (two sizes), snowflake, snow cap for the bench/lamp/tree, a gust (three strokes), a leaf in the wind, a wet-ground sheen strip.
- [ ] Ground and terrain: cobblestone strip, grass tuft (three), dirt path piece, curb, manhole, drain, a hill silhouette and a mountain silhouette for the back wall, a river band and a small wooden pier.
- [ ] Vehicles: car (hatchback), van, scooter, tram (front + one car), a rowing boat for the river, a wheelbarrow.
- [ ] Animals: pigeon, crow, rabbit, hedgehog, goldfish (for a bowl), a bee, a butterfly — each ≤ 30px and readable at depth 0.7.
- [ ] Items people carry (16–28px): keys, phone, sandwich, glasses, fish, fishing rod, coin, letter, coffee-to-go with lid, ice cream, balloon on a string, a small gift box.
- [ ] Night: lit variants of lamp, booth and every building window (`*-lit`), a moon, three star shapes; the game will switch by clock.
- [ ] UI icons (24px, ink on nothing): arrows, jump, hit, kick, act, invite (a hand waving), chat bubble, coin, heart, flag, gear.
- [ ] Web entrance: the site serves `game/assets/manifest.json` + the SVGs as a public gallery page under `/play/assets` so people (and the owner) can see the growing set without opening Godot — read-only, static files via the assets CDN; no Worker code.

## 3D town — the Omega Ruby bar (owner, 2026-09-28; `game/scenes/town3d.tscn` + `scripts/stick3d.gd` are the reference; build here before the Assets and Game sections)
- [ ] Stick3D poses (remainder, re-read 2026-09-28 vision run — shipped in `stick3d.gd` as `pose_request`/`action` states: seat(bench/chair), lean, read, shake, eat, drink, wave, rest(bed), swing, push, carry, throw, punch(jab/cross/hook), kick(+run/jet), flinch, getup, lying, yawn/shrug/look): still missing from the 2D library — sit (floor, cross-legged), phone, sweep, water, trip, brace, catch, laugh, dance, yoga, stretch, sneeze, shiver, fan — each a named state with its own timing, never a still frame; keep the 2D names.
- [ ] Stick3D feel (remainder — idle fidgets and knockback/get-up shipped, see GROW-LOG.md): foot planting (IK-lite: keep the grounded foot's world position fixed between steps), turn-in-place animation when the direction flips by >120° while stationary.
- [ ] Ink outline: an inverted-hull outline pass (or a screen-space edge shader) so buildings, trees and the figure read as drawn — the POZ look in 3D. Must work on the GL Compatibility renderer (web export).
- [ ] Face sets: `faces/` for more building kinds — bakery (awning stripe, display window), post office, clinic (cross), café, church (arched windows), shop — and a `houseSpec(seed)` in the generator that picks wall/roof/door/window layout so no two houses are twins; town3d reads the spec.
- [ ] Props as primitives (remainder — bench, lamp, bin, stall, swing, fence, counter exist inline in `town_build.gd`): fountain (basin cylinder + water disc + jet), booth, sign, rock, flowerpot, well — each a `_prop_<kind>(at)`; and move the existing builders into a new `props3d.gd` when the next one lands (`town_build.gd` is at 440 lines, the split threshold), sized against the 1.0m figure.
- [ ] Ground (remainder — grass and cobble tiles, cobble paths as 2cm boxes, the pond disc with a lighter rim exist): dirt/sand/water tiles; paths with real curbs (2–4cm lips); grass tufts and flowers as tiny crossed quads; the market plaza edged in a kerb.
- [ ] Trees and flora in 3D (remainder — the round blob-crown fruit tree with sway and three shakeable apples exists): tall, willow (hanging strands as thin quads), pine, bush, hedge (box), flower beds beyond the two house-front beds, a sapling; slight per-tree yaw variance.
- [ ] Residents in 3D (remainder — the roster spawns as `Resident`/`Stick3D` with web colours, walks between spots, sits, leans, shakes, shops at stalls, sleeps at home at night, shelters from rain): the routine is `randi()`-driven; port the web's seeded wall-clock buckets (`goose.ts` `routineAt`) so the site and the game show the same resident in the same place at the same time.
- [ ] Camera (remainder — both views follow with lerp; the 2.5D view hides roofs/front walls near you and, since the 2026-09-28 vision run, whole houses standing between you and the camera): look-ahead in the move direction, gentle zoom-out at speed, and fading (alpha) instead of popping for the behind-a-house case.
- [ ] Time of day (remainder — the 12-minute sun cycle, warm dusk colour, sky lerp and lamps-at-night exist): windows glow via an emissive glass material at night (swap the `dfe6ea` pane material after dusk), and the interior `_furniture("lamp")` lights follow the clock like the street lamps do.
- [ ] Web export of town3d as the site's demo (GL Compatibility, no SSAO), replacing the 2D Square on the home page when it runs at 60fps on a phone.
- [ ] 3D town — customization (owner, 2026-09-28): the rig has sockets (`socket_hat`, `socket_face`, `socket_back`, `socket_belt`, `hand_l/r`); build the first wearables as small primitives (cap, beanie, top hat, glasses, backpack, scarf) attachable at runtime, and a colour per person (`Stick3D.color`) from the web's `figureColor(uid)` so a person looks the same on the site and in the game.
- [ ] 3D town — reaching roofs (remainder — the flat-roof stair-house has stairs to a solid rooftop): a ladder on the north lane's middle house (rungs as boxes + an invisible ramp collider, same trick as `_stairs`), crates you can hop up at the market, and a crow that sits on that roof (Animals / "Bird rig").
- [ ] 3D town — interiors (remainder — every house has a plank floor, rug, bed, table with two movable chairs, shelf with books, pot and a lamp, seeded colours; residents own houses via `home_door` and sleep there): all nine interiors share one layout — add two more layouts (a kitchen with a counter and kettle; a workshop with a bench and tools) picked by seed so the lane's three houses differ inside, and close the door behind a resident once inside (today it stays open until they leave).
- [ ] 3D town — items in hands (remainder — apple/bread eaten in three bites and gone, cup `drink` pose, newspaper `read` standing, charged throw with a real arc): **a thrown item never hits anyone** — `_fly` only checks the ground; add the hit test (resident within 0.4m of the flying node → `hit(dir, body, heavy=false)`), read the paper while seated on a bench, and the cup half is the Items / "Café loop" line.

- [ ] Ground: grass tufts (tiny crossed quads, 3 blades) scattered by seed, flower clusters (5 colours) in beds and wild, worn dirt patches near doors and paths; a curved-edge cobble path with kerb.

## Motion library from the owner's board (2026-09-28; https://kr.pinterest.com/pkm4625/%EC%A1%B8%EB%9D%BC%EB%A7%A8/ ; one family per run, in `stick3d.gd` + `stickman.ts`)
- [ ] Carrying family: box on the shoulder, two boxes stacked, a sofa with two people (front and back), a wheelbarrow push, a suitcase drag — and the town use: movers' job that carries furniture between houses.
- [ ] Work family: sweep (exists), shovel dig, rake leaves, hammer a fence post, saw a plank, paint a wall with a roller, water with a can (exists), push a mower — builders and gardeners use them.
- [ ] Sport family: sprint start, hurdle, high jump, javelin throw, shot put, golf swing, tennis serve, basketball shot, bicycle ride (a bike prop), skateboard — a park sports day event.
- [ ] Together family: tug of war (rope prop, two teams lean back), handshake, hug, high five, carry a person piggyback, dance pair (spin), wedding walk, graduation cap toss.
- [ ] Everyday family: phone (exists), read a map, umbrella in rain (weather ties in), stretch after sitting, yawn (2D exists), sneeze, shiver in cold, fan in heat, sit on the floor cross-legged, lie on the grass and look at the sky.
- [ ] Water family: swim strokes (a river/pond you can enter), dive, float on the back, fish with a rod (cast, wait, tug, pull), splash a duck.
- [ ] Reaction family: surprised (arms up, step back), laugh (2D exists), cry, shrug (2D exists), think (hand on chin), point, wave (exists), bow, clap, cheer with both arms.
- [ ] Reaction family, first slice — `clap` (motions axis, polish run 83 — three trades now finish a job in public and nobody watches): `clap` in `stick3d_poses.gd` + `figure.gd` (0.2s arms-out windup, four 0.25s claps with the palms meeting at the chest and the elbows bouncing, 0.3s drop — `CLAP_T` 1.5); a resident in `routine` or `walk` within 6m of the cobbler's stool, the cutler's customer spot or the tailor's fitting stool when a customer's job ends (`_resoled`, `_sharpened`, `_mended`) stops and claps one time in four, facing the customer ("Well done." one time in three of those); the player gets the same `clap` on `C` with empty hands within 6m of a trade customer spot whose job ended in the last 3s (the bench's own `C` still wins when you face the bench). Plain outcome (walk on) stays 75%.
- [ ] Reaction family, second slice — `settle` (motions axis, run 104 — a shaken resident now walks into the Sunroom but sits down like anyone else): a new `settle` pose for the first second on a cushion after a knock-down (0.25s shoulders up to the ears and both hands pressed flat on the thighs, 0.5s a long exhale with the shoulders dropping and the head sinking, 0.25s back to the `crossleg` hands-on-knees) played by a resident arriving `shaken` (`_post_arrive`) and by the player when `cushion_use` runs within 30s of `player_up_at`; the storysitter's `story_calm` line waits for the exhale instead of landing on the sit-down; the same pose, standing, after a flinch that did not knock the body over (one time in three — plain flinch recovery stays the likely one).
- [ ] Everyday family, first slice — `backstr` (motions axis — the second-thinnest at twelve open `game/` lines, run 107; builds on today's `stoop`): the board's "stretch after sitting/bending" as a pose anyone plays after three stoops within a minute or after standing from a bench sat on for 60s+ (`stoop_n`/`sat_since` on the figure): 0.3s both hands go to the small of the back with the elbows out (windup), 0.7s the chest arches back 0.3 rad with the head tilting up and one heel lifting (hold, a small wobble at the top), 0.4s it unwinds forward past straight and settles (recovery); residents play it in `_leave` and after a stoop run, the player in `town_coins`/`town_meals` at the same triggers; a resident within 3m who sees it yawns (the `yawn` fidget is contagious once). Plain outcome: never on the first stoop or a short sit — most stand-ups stay plain.

## Animals — the standard (owner, 2026-09-28; `game/scripts/quad3d.gd` is the rig; each item ships with every minimum motion)
- [ ] A heron at the south landing (thin axis: animals, run 94 — the landings have water, posts and fish and no bird): on `bird3d.gd` with every bird minimum (bob, peck, flap, hop) plus a heron's own two (a slow stalk, knees high, and a frozen neck-cocked wait before the strike) — a tall grey bird (long neck = two segments, a dagger bill) stands on the south jetty's west post from 05:00 to 10:00, stalks the shallows beside the deck, strikes into the water every 20–40s (one in three comes up with a small fish it swallows, neck bulging); the punt nosing in or a person within 3m makes it flap off low over the river to the north bank and come back 30s after the landing is empty. A player holding a `fish` (run 91) who sets it on the deck gets it taken in one strike; residents who moor at the south landing look at it (`HeadLook`) and say `mind.line("heron")`.
- [ ] A fox at the woodpile at night (thin axis: animals, run 92 — the open-world sites have no animals): on `quad3d.gd` with every minimum motion, a red fox comes out of the cabin's ring of trees after dark, sniffs along the split stack (head low, tail straight), noses a loose log off the top so it rolls to the ground (the woodcutter grumbles next morning and re-stacks it), curls up for a while in the warm lee of the cabin wall by the stove (lie with tail over nose, ears flicking) when the stove is lit, and freezes then bounds off into the trees if the player comes within 5m running (walks away if approached slowly). Two species moves: `mouse` (the high pounce: rear legs load 0.3s → arc 0.5s → nose-first landing in the grass) and `scent` (zigzag trot, nose to ground).
- [ ] Bird rig (`bird3d.gd`): body, head bob, beak peck, two wings that flap, hop; pigeons, sparrows, a crow on a roof; fly to a tree when the player comes, land on the fence when it is quiet.
- [ ] Squirrel with the quad rig: on the ground near trees, freezes when you approach, climbs the trunk (spine flex up the cylinder) when you shake the tree, sits and eats a nut with its tail up.
- [ ] Cat life: sleeps on a warm roof or a bench at noon, walks the fence tops, sits in doorways, grooms, arches and hisses if you run at it, lets you pet it only if you approach slowly (speed < walking). (Town wish 2026-09-28, folded here run 70: a stray cat that walks between sunlit benches and porches, nudges a slow-moving resident for a petting animation, and hisses and bolts under cover when a human sprints past — the resident half is new: residents pet it too, with the player's `grab` reach-down.)
- [ ] Dog life: has an owner resident and walks with them on a routine, fetches a thrown apple (runs, picks it up in the mouth, brings it back, drops it, play-bows), sleeps by the door at night.
- [ ] Ducks with a rig: waddle on land (body sway), paddle in the pond (legs hidden, ripples), dip head to feed, flap-run when chased, ducklings following the mother in a line.
- [ ] Frog by the pond (hop cycle, throat puff), butterflies over flower beds (two quads, flutter path), bees (buzz path around one flower), fish shadows under the water.
- [ ] Goat with the quad rig (town wish 2026-09-28, folded run 72): a stubborn goat on the park verge — idle/walk/run/sit/lie/stretch/look/tail like the rest of the standard, plus its two moves: `graze` (head down, a slow side-to-side tear, tail flick) and `butt` (a two-step backing-up windup, head lowered, a short charge). It trails anyone carrying food at 1.5m (the same follow rule as the dog's bread-trailing in Scavengers slice 2; `C`-feed it to stop) and butts a *sprinter* (player or resident, same rule) who passes within 1m into the ported `trip` stumble — never a knock-down, at most 30% of passes, 8s cooldown, so running past stays the plain outcome (holiday rule).
- [ ] Rabbit and hedgehog life (thin axis: animals, 2026-09-28 — the Assets backlog already lists both as SVG entries but neither has a behaviour line here): rabbit uses the quad rig on the park lawn, freezes then bolts in a zigzag when approached (faster and more erratic than the squirrel's dart), thumps a hind leg before bolting; hedgehog is small and slow, curls into a ball (spine flexed tight, legs and head tucked) instead of fleeing when startled, only uncurling after a few quiet seconds.
- [ ] Hens at the plot (thin axis: animals, polish run 97 — the plot grows crops and nothing pecks at it; the bird rig line above is the prerequisite, build it as this slice if it has not landed): three hens on `bird3d.gd` (round body 0.22, a red comb box, short legs) in a low wattle run beside the plot gate with a little A-frame coop; idle (bob, blink, one-leg rest), walk (head-thrust gait), peck (three quick dips, grain specks), flap-hop over a dropped crop, dust-bathe (sit and shuffle, a puff of dust), flee from a running player (short flap-run), follow a player or resident carrying a `seed` or `bread` within 3m; a hen lays an `egg` item (new `make_item("egg")`, off-white ovoid 0.05) in the coop once a day at dawn; `C` in the coop takes it, eaten like food. The gardener collects it on their 7:00 round with a `stoop` pick-up; `minds.json` `hens`.

## Jobs and vehicles in `game/` (thin axes, 2026-09-28 vision run — neither axis had a single `game/` line; the web has dozens of jobs, the 3D town has none by name)
- [ ] (vehicles + jobs, 2026-10-17 — the vehicles axis, second thinnest at 99 open lines by whole-word keyword; wider, from the last twenty log lines: every vehicle in the town carries people or bread, none carries a build) The builders' materials cart: a two-wheeled flatbed cart (`props3d.gd` builder — bed 1.2×0.7, two spoked cylinder wheels, two handles; not a `Car3D`, it never enters the lanes) parked at the lumber pile; when `town_growth.gd` opens a site, the builder of run 108 (or any hired builder) loads four plank bundles (`plank` items stacked as boxes on the bed), pushes it along the pavement to the site (`push` pose family of the swing, the body behind the handles, 0.6 of WALK) and unloads them stage by stage, and the site's `_draw_site` stage only advances once the bundles are there — the build chain becomes legible on the street; the player can push the loaded cart too (C at the handles) and gets a line from the builder; `probe_growth` gains the cart's trip, the unload, the stalled stage, and `probe_traffic` stays at 0 crashes (the cart keeps to the pavement, outside z −0.1..4.1).
- [ ] (vehicles + economy, 2026-10-17 — the vehicles axis, second thinnest at 106 open lines by whole-word keyword; wider, from the last twenty log lines: the tower gets a resident a run and the roads to it still carry nothing on wheels that is not a car) The hand cart: a new `Cart3D` (`scripts/cart3d.gd` — two spoked wheels, a flat bed with low rails, two shafts; no motor, it moves only when a body is between the shafts) with a `pull` pose (both hands on the shafts at hip height, the torso leaning into the walk 0.2 rad, the shoulders working with the stride, a 0.3 s heave to start from standing — never a still frame); the general store's restock (run 114's `next` shelves) arrives by cart: at tower-dawn the storekeeper's helper (a new door job "carter", one resident) pulls the cart from the north lane's depot (a new small shed with a roller door at the lane's end — `town_build`, not in the road band) to the store's side door, four crates on the bed, unloads one crate a `haul`, and pulls the empty cart back; a parked cart can be taken by the player (C between the shafts, hands empty) and pulled at WALK × 0.8 — residents step aside as for cars (`town_systems` yields on the cart's raycast), a crate on the bed can be taken and sold at a counter for its `prices.json` buy price (the carter notices and the ledger of run 112 writes the debt); probe: the dawn run, the unload count, the player pull, the step-aside, the taken crate's debt.
- [ ] A storekeeper by name (jobs axis — ten open lines, the second-thinnest, run 114; builds on the general store): the store's `shopkeep` restocks instead of standing still all day — when a shelf has sold four of a kind (`next` ≥ 4 in its spot), the keeper leaves the counter ("Back in five." label on the door, `shop_open` false), walks to the tailor's stitchhouse for scarves/backpacks or to the delivery van at the market for a hat box (new `box` prop, two-hand carry pose `carry`), comes back and the shelf's `next` resets while the display refills one by one; one coin per restock through `wage(who, "restock")` (`prices.json` wage). Probe: four picks close the store, the keeper returns within 90 s, the shelf shows four again.
- [ ] A postie (jobs): one resident gets a satchel (a small box on `socket_back`) and a morning routine that walks the nine doors in x order, plays a `deliver` pose at each (reach forward, a short bow, recover — its own timing), and leaves a `letter` item in the mailbox on the houses that have one (`_house` builds a mailbox on ~50%; the lane's houses should all get one so the round is complete). The letter is a pickable item; a house's owner picks it up and reads it (`read`) when they come home. Player half: `C` at a mailbox with a letter in hand posts it; the postie then carries yours.
- [ ] A lamplighter by name (jobs axis — the thinnest at eight open `game/` lines, run 107; the 2026-09-29 town wish made concrete and its wish line deleted): one seeded adult (`uid % 24 == 11`, job "lamplighter") keeps a short pole (a 0.9m `_box` on `hand_r`) on a hook inside a lamphouse — a 1.6×2.0×1.4 shed with a half-door at the east end of the north lane (`lamphouse` spot, outside the road band); when `is_night()` turns true they walk the eight street lamps in x order and play a new `light` pose at each (0.3s the pole rises to the lamp head, 0.4s hold while that lamp's `OmniLight3D` fades in over 0.4s instead of all lamps snapping on in `_daylight`, 0.3s lower, a glance up), the reverse walk at dawn; a lamp not yet reached stays dark, so dusk reads as a route. The player `C` at the hook takes the pole and `C` at a dark lamp lights it the same way (the lamplighter skips lit lamps and says `mind.line("lit_already")`, new category); a resident under a lamp the moment it comes on looks up (`look` fidget). Plain outcome: every lamp is lit by 20:00 whoever did it.
- [ ] A builder by name (jobs axis — the thinnest at nine open `game/` lines, run 108; builds on the nap corner, whose stages today appear at 08:00 by themselves): one seeded jobless adult (`uid % 24 == 19`, job "builder", an ink-grey `cap` from `Wear` as the hard hat) walks at 07:30 to whatever site is open — the nap corner while `nap.stage < 3`, afterwards the canonical "Builders" plot under "Owner session 2026-09-29" — takes one plank from the pile with `grab` (the pile's boards become real `plank` items, a new `make_item`), carries it to the next corner post and plays the run 80 `hammer` pose there (three taps per post, four posts), and the stage appears when the last tap lands instead of on the clock; a plank the player carries from the pile to the site (`C` at a post while holding one) saves the builder one trip and earns `mind.line("builder_thanks")` (new category); passers keep the `building` line. Plain outcome: the stage still appears every morning whoever hammered it.
- [ ] A barista by name (jobs axis — the thinnest at seven open `game/` lines, run 110; closes the café dish nobody owns): the café door gets `job` "barista"; 09:00–17:00 they stand behind the café counter (a second `counter` slot at `pos − (0,0,1.6)`, yaw 0) wiping the top with a new `wipe` pose (0.3s the right hand lays a cloth down, a 1.2s loop of two flat circles with the shoulder leading and the head following the hand, 0.3s the cloth folds back), hand the cup over with `pass` (run 86) instead of the cup appearing in the customer's hand (customer plays the receiving half), close the café dish at 17:00 by the run 110 route (`till_closing` reads `sp["owner_job"]` instead of `has("stock")`) and say `till_mine` when the player palms it; a resident barista remembers theft the same way (`mind.stole`).
- [ ] The milk float on the north lane (vehicles axis — eight open lines, run 110): a slow open-sided electric cart (`Car3D` variant, max speed 0.35 of the taxi, no doors, a crate bed holding six `bottle` items — a thin white cylinder with a foil cap), AI-driven 06:30–08:00 along the north lane only (never the main road band), stopping 4s at each house door where the driver (`job` "milkman", one seeded adult `uid % 24 == 19`) steps down, carries one bottle with `carry`, sets it on the step with a new `setdown` pose (0.3s knees bend and the torso folds as in `stoop`, 0.1s the hand opens on the step, 0.4s rise with a small back-of-the-hand wipe across the brow), and picks up yesterday's empty with `stoop`; the first resident out of that door takes the bottle in and `drink`s it at the door; the player may take any bottle (a plain take, always succeeds) and the milkman says `mind.line("milk")` once (four lines). The float's bell (a 0.6s `ring` sound stand-in: a small Label3D "ting" above the cab) marks each stop.
- [ ] A bookkeeper by name (jobs axis — the thinnest at eight open `game/` lines, run 112; builds on the ledger table, whose page today writes itself): one seeded jobless adult (`uid % 24 == 15`, job "bookkeeper", `glasses` from `Wear`) sits on a stool added behind the table (a `clerk` spot, `yaw` 0, facing the customers) 10:00–12:00 and 15:00–17:00 with a new `scribe` pose (left hand holding the page flat, right hand writing in short strokes at 3 Hz with the head following the line, a dip to the inkwell every 4 s — its own timing); while they sit, every `tab_note` plays one writing stroke at the table even when the customer is at the counter 4 m away, and every `tab_paid` a strike-through (the same pose, the hand travelling the line once); at 17:00 they carry the bowl to the baker instead of the baker coming (`carrying_big`, the heavy carry) and say `mind.line("books")` (new category). Player half: C at the empty stool sits you there and your strokes write for residents arriving broke (a toast names them). Plain outcome: the ledger works exactly as now without them.
- [ ] The bowl rides the float (vehicles + economy axis — nine open lines, run 112; needs "The milk float on the north lane"): on the 17:00 round the float stops 6 s at the ledger table; if the baker is more than 20 m away or kneading, the driver lifts the bowl into the crate bed (the `setdown` timings reversed as a `lift` beat) and sets it on the bakery counter's left end at the round's end, where it merges into the till (`till += n`, discs shown) so the baker finds the tab money with the takings; the bowl rides visible in the bed (its discs jitter 0.004 m in y with the float's speed) and spills if the float is rammed above 0.6 of its speed (`drop_coin` ×n, bouncing). Resident half is the driver; player half: ride the back step (the passenger rule) and C at the table to lift the bowl yourself. Plain outcome: the float only carries what the baker has not collected.
- [ ] A handcart (vehicles): a two-wheeled wooden cart prop on the north lane (box bed + two cylinder wheels + two handles), pushable by the player and residents with `C` (the `push` pose already exists for the swing; the cart needs its walking variant `haul` — leaning in, arms straight, short steps), rolls along the lane with the pusher, holds up to three loose items placed on its bed with `C`; a resident hauls it between the market and the lane once a day.
- [ ] The bakery's delivery bicycle (vehicles axis, run 72): leans against the bakery wall left of the counter (frame = two thin boxes, two cylinder wheels with spokes as four crossed bars, a front basket) — at 08:00 the baker takes one loaf per lane house, rides it up the x=0 path to the north lane with a new `cycle` pose from the board's cycling family (seat, hands on the bars, legs pedalling on the wheel phase, a lean into turns — `stick3d_poses.gd` + `stickman.ts`), drops the loaf at each lane door plaque, rides back; the player rides it with `C` (← → ↑ ↓ steer, faster than running, no jumping, dismount with `C`), residents step aside for it the way they avoid each other.
- [ ] A gardener by name (jobs axis, run 70 — the plot is worked by whoever wanders in; a job means one resident whose day is the plot): one seeded resident (`uid % 24 == 5`) weights the three `plot` spots ×6 in `_pick_spot`, starts the day holding the can (`carrying_kind = "can"`, so the pump's can is the spare), wears the straw hat, and when a row is ripe carries the crop to the market's first stall and sets it on the stall top (a fourth goods sphere appears — stall stock, the first thing a resident makes that another can take). Greeting them gets "Rows want water." while any row is dry.
- [ ] Stallholders by name (jobs axis, run 74 — the four market stalls are furniture nobody works): four seeded residents (`uid % 24 in [8, 14, 17, 21]`) each own one stall; a `stall` spot behind each counter (registered by `_market`, `yaw` toward the plaza) weighted ×6 for its holder in `_pick_spot`, closed at night and in rain (the holder goes home like the baker leaves the oven); a `tidy` pose there (both hands rearranging goods on the counter at chest height — a reach, a place, a half step sideways, its own timing, `stick3d_poses.gd` + `stickman.ts`) and one line to a player who stops within 1.5m ("Fresh today." / "Just in." / "Have a look."). "Sell a crop at the market stall" under Economy then has someone to sell to.
- [ ] A wheelbarrow at the plot (vehicles axis, run 77 — the garden is the one workplace with something heavy to move, and the board has a wheelbarrow sheet): a one-wheeled barrow prop parked by the pump (a box tray on a cylinder wheel, two handle bars, `town_places.gd`), `C` empty-handed grips it and walking pushes it (`barrow` pose: both arms down and back holding the handles, torso leaning 0.2 forward, the tray tilting with speed — its own 0.3s lift and set-down), a picked crop dropped on it while gripping rides in the tray (up to three, stacked as they are), `C` again sets it down; the gardener resident wheels ripe rows to the market stall on it once a day, three crops per trip, and says "Three today." — the first vehicle in the town that carries goods.
- [ ] A ferryman by name (jobs axis, run 78 — the punt is rowed by whoever wanders onto the jetty; a job means one resident whose day is the water): one seeded resident (`uid % 24 == 2`) weights the `boat` spot ×6 in `_pick_spot`, wears the straw hat, and instead of the 12m loop rows to wherever a resident is waiting at a new `hail` spot on the meadow bank (x 18, z 12.7 — the terrace's foot) and back; a resident whose next spot is across the river east of x 8 picks `hail` 30% of the time (`crossings()` skips the bridge for them, the punt is their crossing), plays `hail` (one arm up, a slow wave every 2s — its own pose) until the punt arrives, boards beside the ferryman on a second thwart at the stern (`SEAT_OFF` + 0.7 in x, seated, no oars) and steps off on the far bank; the player can hail the same way with `C` at the `hail` spot and ride as the passenger — the first time a resident carries the player somewhere.

- [ ] A bicycle rack by the fountain (vehicles axis, run 81 — vehicles is the thinnest axis with three open lines, all of them pushed or parked; the board has a cycling sheet): three bicycle props in a rail on the east plaza (frame = two thin boxes, two cylinder wheels with four crossed spoke bars, a bell) — `C` on one mounts the player (a `cycle` pose: seated on the saddle, hands on the bar, feet on pedals that turn with speed, torso leaning into the speed), 1.5× walk speed, no jump, `C` dismounts and parks it where you stand; two seeded residents (`uid % 24 in [3, 15]`) ride one to the market rail at 08:00 and back at 17:00 with the same pose — the first vehicle a resident and the player both ride that is not a car.
- [ ] A ford for wheels (vehicles axis, run 84 — the stones and the bridge are foot-only, so the tractor parked on the meadow and every car are cut off from the south bank): a gravel ford at x 40 (a paler, shallower strip of the river band, 4m wide, cobble-textured bed showing through), where a car drives at ×0.4 with spray off each wheel (`CPUParticles3D`, only while moving in the ford) and nobody inside swims; the delivery van's route crosses it once a day to leave a crate at the terrace foot, and a walker who wades it swims as anywhere else. Structural under the strict rule (a new traversable connection for vehicles).
